# Visnu Purana 3

## Book Three: Society

## Chapter 1 - The past and present Manvantaras

## \|\| 3.1.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*kathitā gurūṇāsamyagbhūsamudrādisaṃsthitiḥ \|*  
*sūryādināṃ ca saṃsthānaṃ jyotiṣāṃ cātivistarāt \|\| 1 \|\|*

Master, you’ve given me an excellent and detailed description of the earth, the oceans and the rest, as well as the nature of the sun, the stars and other luminaries. 1

## \|\| 3.1.2 \|\|

*devādīnāṃ tathā sṛṣṭirṛṣīṇāṃ cāpi varṇitā \|*  
*cāturvarṇyasya cotpattistiryagyonigatasya ca \|\| 2 \|\|*

Similarly, you’ve explained the creation of the gods and sages, and the origin of the four communities and the realm of animals. 2

## \|\| 3.1.3 \|\|

*dhruvaprahlādacaritaṃ vistarācca tvayoditam \|*  
*manvantarāṇyaśeṣāṇi śrotumicchāmyanukramāt \|\| 3 \|\|*

You’ve also described in detail the feats of Dhruva and Prahlāda. Now I wish to hear about each Manvantara in order. 3

## \|\| 3.1.4 \|\|

*manvantarādhipāṃścaiva śaka3devapurogamān \|*  
*bhavatā kathitānetāñchrotumicchāmyahaṃ guro \|\| 4 \|\|*

I’d like to hear you speak of each age’s overlord, my guru, with mighty Indra at their head. 4

## \|\| 3.1.5 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*atītānāgatānīha yāni manvantarāṇi vai \|*  
*tānyahaṃ bhavataḥ samyaklathayāmi yathā kramam \|\| 5 \|\|*

I’ll now give you a full and orderly account of the past and future Manvantaras. 5

## \|\| 3.1.6 \|\|

*svāyaṃbhuvo manuḥ pūrvaṃ paraḥ svārociṣas tathā \|*  
*uttamastāmasaścaiva raivataścākṣuṣas tathā \|\| 6 \|\|*

The initial Manu was Svāyaṃbhuva, then came Svārociṣa, Uttama, Tāmasa, Raivata and Cākṣuṣa. 6

## \|\| 3.1.7 \|\|

*ṣaḍete manavotītāḥsāṃprataṃ tu raveḥsutaḥ \|*  
*vaivasvateyaṃ yastvetatmaptamaṃ vartatentaram \|\| 7 \|\|*

These six Manus have already passed. The current one is Vaivasvata, the Sun’s own son, and his Manvantara is the seventh. 7

## \|\| 3.1.8 \|\|

*svāyaṃbhuvaṃ tu kathitaṃ kalpādāvantaraṃ mayā \|*  
*devāḥ saptarṣayaś caiva yathāvat kathitā mayā \|\| 8 \|\|*

I’ve already spoken of the Manvantara of Svāyaṃbhuva at the beginning of the age, and I’ve told you all about the gods and sages of that period. 8

## \|\| 3.1.9 \|\|

*ata ūrdhvaṃ pravakṣyāmi manoḥsvārociṣasya tu \|*  
*manvantarādhipānsamyagdevarṣiṃstatsutāṃs tathā \|\| 9 \|\|*

Now I’ll list the overlords, the deities, the seers and their progeny, who lived in the Manvantara of the Manu Svārociṣa. 9

## \|\| 3.1.10 \|\|

*pārāvatāḥ satuṣitā devāḥ svārociṣe 'ntare \|*  
*vipaścattatra devendro maitreyāsīnmahābalaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

During that period, the deities were the Pārāvatas and Satuṣitas, and mighty Vipaścit was the king of gods, Maitreya. 10

## \|\| 3.1.11 \|\|

*ūrjastaṃbhas tathā prāṇovātortha pṛṣabhas tathā \|*  
*nirayaś ca parīvāṃś ca tatra saptarṣayo 'bhavan \|\| 11 \|\|*

Ūrja, Stambha, Prāṇa, Dattoli, Ṛṣabha, Niścara and Arvarīvant were the seven sages. 11

## \|\| 3.1.12 \|\|

*caitrakiṃpuruṣādyāś ca sutāḥsvārociṣasya tu \|*  
*dvitīyametadvyākhyātamantaraṃ śṛṇu cottamam \|\| 12 \|\|*

Caitra, Kimpuruṣa and others were the sons of Svārociṣa. I’ve described the second Manvantara. Now listen to the one that followed. 12

## \|\| 3.1.13 \|\|

*tṛtīyepyantare brahmannuttamo nāma yo manuḥ \|*  
*suśāntirnāma devendro maitreyāsītsutāñchṛṇu \|\| 13 \|\|*

In the third Manvantara, brahmin, the Manu was named Uttama and the regal deity Suśānti was the king of gods, Maitreya. 13

## \|\| 3.1.14 \|\|

*sudhāmānas tathā satyā japāścātha pratardanāḥ \|*  
*vaśavartiś ca pañcaite gaṇā dvādaśakāḥsmṛtāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

There were five hosts of deities, the Sudhāmans, Satyas, Śivas, Pratardanas and Vaśavartins, each of which comprised twelve gods. 14

## \|\| 3.1.15 \|\|

*vasiṣṭhatanayā hyote sapta saptarṣayobhavan \|*  
*ajaḥ paraśudīptādyāstathottamamanoḥsutāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

The seven sons of Vasiṣṭha were the seven sages, and Aja, Paraśu, Divya and the others were the sons of Manu Uttama. 15

## \|\| 3.1.16 \|\|

*tāmasasyāntare devāḥsupārā harayas tathā \|*  
*satyāś ca sudhiyaścaiva saptaviṃśatikā gaṇāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

During the Manvantara of Tāmasa, the deities were Surūpas, Haris, Satyas and Sudhis. Each host had twenty-seven gods. 16

## \|\| 3.1.17 \|\|

*śivirindras tathā cāsīcchatayajñopalakṣamaḥ \|*  
*saptarṣa yaś ca ye teṣāṃ teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|\| 17 \|\|*

Śibi, also known as Śatakratu or ‘Hundred Sacrifices’, was their king. Now hear from me the names of the seven sages at that time: 17

## \|\| 3.1.18 \|\|

*jyotirdhāmā puthuḥ kātyaścaitrognirdhanakas tathā \|*  
*pīvaraścarṣayo hyete sapta tatrāpi cāntare \|\| 18 \|\|*

Jyotirdhāman, Pṛthu, Kāvya, Caitra, Agni, Vanaka and Pīvara were the sages then. 18

## \|\| 3.1.19 \|\|

*naraḥ khyātiḥ keturūpo jānujaṅghādayas tathā \|*  
*putrāstu tāmasasyāsannājānaḥsumahābalāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The mighty kings named Nara, Khyāti, Śāntahaya, Jānujaṅgha and others were the sons of Tāmasa. 19

## \|\| 3.1.20 \|\|

*pañcame vāpi maitreya raivato nāma nāmataḥ \|*  
*manurvibhuś ca tatrendro devāṃścātrāntare śṛṇu \|\| 20 \|\|*

In the fifth Manvantara, the Manu was known as Raivata by name, and Vibhu was the king of gods, Maitreya. Now listen to the names of the deities of that period: 20

## \|\| 3.1.21 \|\|

*amitābhā bhūtarayā vaikuṇṭhāḥsusamedhasaḥ \|*  
*ete devagaṇāstatra caturdaśa caturdaśa \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Amitābhas, Bhūtarayas, Vaikuṇṭhas and Sumedhases. Each host had fourteen gods. 21

## \|\| 3.1.22 \|\|

*hiraṇyaromā vedāśrirūrdhvabāhustathāvaraḥ \|*  
*vedabāhuḥsudhāmā ca parjanyaś ca mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*ete saptarṣayo vipra tatrāsanraivatentare \|\| 22 \|\|*

Hiraṇyaroman, Vedaśrī, Ūrdhvabāhu, Vedabāhu, Sudhāman, Parjanya and Mahāmuni were the seven sages during the Manvantara of Raivata, brahmin. 22

## \|\| 3.1.23 \|\|

*balabandhuś ca saṃbhāvyaḥsatyakādyāś ca tatsutāḥ \|*  
*narendrāś ca mahāvīryā babhūvurmunisattama \|\| 23 \|\|*

The valiant kings Balabandhu, Susaṃbhāvya and Satyaka, among others, were his sons, best of sages. 23

## \|\| 3.1.24 \|\|

*svārociṣaścauttamaś ca tāmaso raivatas tathā \|*  
*priyavratānvayā hyete catvāro manavaḥsmṛtāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

The four Manus Svārociṣa, Uttama, Tāmasa and Raivata are thought to be Priyavrata’s offspring. 24

## \|\| 3.1.25 \|\|

*viṣṇumārādhya tapasā sa rājarṣiḥ priyavrataḥ \|*  
*manvantarādhipānetāllaṃbdhavānātmavaṃśajān \|\| 25 \|\|*

Having propitiated Viṣṇu with his austerities, the sage-king Priyavrata was granted these Manvantara overlords as his descendants. 25

## \|\| 3.1.26 \|\|

*ṣaṣṭhe manvaṃrate cāsīccākṣuṣākhyas tathā manuḥ \|*  
*manojavastathaivendro devānapi nibodha me \|\| 26 \|\|*

In the sixth Manvantara, the Manu was known as Cākṣuṣa, and Manojava was the king of gods. Now hear about the deities: 26

## \|\| 3.1.27 \|\|

*āpyāḥ prasūtā bhavyāś ca pṛthukāścā divaukasaḥ \|*  
*mahānubhāvā lakhāś ca pañcaite hyaṣṭakā gaṇāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

The Āpyas, Prasūtas, Bhavyas, the divine Pṛthugas and the resolute Lekhas were the five hosts, each of which comprised eight deities. 27

## \|\| 3.1.28 \|\|

*sumedhā virajāścaiva haviṣmānuttamo madhuḥ \|*  
*atināmā sahiṣṇuś ca saptāsanniti carṣayaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Sumedhas, Virajas, Haviṣmant, Uttama, Madhu, Atināman and Suhiṣṇu were the seven sages. 28

## \|\| 3.1.29 \|\|

*ūruḥ pūruḥ śatadhyumnapramukhāḥ sumahābalāḥ \|*  
*cākṣuṣasya manoḥ putrāḥ pṛthivīpatayo 'bhavan \|\| 29 \|\|*

The sons of the Manu Cākṣuṣa—mighty Ūru and Pūru, with Śatadyumna as the foremost—were the kings. 29

## \|\| 3.1.30 \|\|

*vivasvataḥsuto vipra śrāddhadevo mahādyutiḥ \|*  
*manuḥsaṃvartate dhīmān sāṃprataṃ saptamentare \|\| 30 \|\|*

The son of Vivasvat, brahmin, that brilliant god presiding over funerary rites, is the wise Manu who oversees the present Manvantara, the seventh. 30

## \|\| 3.1.31 \|\|

*ādityavasurudrādyā devāścātra mahāmune \|*  
*purandarastathaivātra maitreya tridaśeśvaraḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

The Ādityas, Vasus, Rudras and others are the deities of this period, great sage, and Indra, conqueror of cities, is the lord of thirteen gods, Maitreya. 31

## \|\| 3.1.32 \|\|

*vaśiṣṭhaḥ kāśyapothātrirjamadagniḥsagautamaḥ \|*  
*viśvāmitrabharadvājau sapta saptarṣayo 'bhavan \|\| 32 \|\|*

Vasiṣṭha, Kāśyapa, Atri, Jamadagni, Gautama, Viśvāmitra and Bharadvāja are the seven sages of this period. 32

## \|\| 3.1.33 \|\|

*ikṣvākuś ca nṛgaścaiva dhṛṣṭaḥ śaryātireva ca \|*  
*nariṣyantaś ca vikhyāto nābhāgoriṣṭa eva ca \|\| 33 \|\|*

Ikṣvāku, Nṛga, Dhṛṣṭa, Śaryāti, famed Nariṣyanta, Nābhāga, Diṣṭa, 33

## \|\| 3.1.34 \|\|

*karūṣaś ca pṛṣadhraś ca sumahāṃllokaviśrutaḥ \|*  
*manorvaivasvatasyaite nava putrāḥ sudhārmikāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Karūṣa and Pṛṣadhra, rich and famous—these are the nine good sons of Manu Vaivasvata. 34

## \|\| 3.1.35 \|\|

*viṣṇuśaktiranaupamyā sattvodriktā sthitau sthitā \|*  
*manvantareṣvaśeṣeṣu devatvenādhitiṣṭhati \|\| 35 \|\|*

The unequalled energy of Viṣṇu, abounding in vitality, infuses all creation and presides over every Manvantara in the form of a deity. 35

## \|\| 3.1.36 \|\|

*aṃśena tasyā jajñe 'sau yajñaḥsvāyaṃbhuventare \|*  
*ākūtyāṃ mānaso deva utpannaḥ prathamentare \|\| 36 \|\|*

A portion of that energy appeared as Yajña during the period of Svāyaṃbhuva. This divine being arose from Svāyaṃbhuva’s mind and was born to his daughter Ākūtī in the first Manvantara. 36

## \|\| 3.1.37 \|\|

*tataḥ punaḥ sa vai devaḥ prāpte svārociṣentare \|*  
*tuṣitāyāṃ samutpanno hyājiṣastuṣitaiḥ saha \|\| 37 \|\|*

Later, that same deity was born to Tuṣitā as Ajita during the period of Svārociṣa, along with the other Tuṣita gods. 37

## \|\| 3.1.38 \|\|

*auttamapyantare devastuṣitastu punaḥsa vai \|*  
*satyāyāmabhavatsatyaḥ satyaiḥ saha surottamaiḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

In the period of Uttama, Tuṣita was born again to Satyā as Satya, together with the peerless Satya deities. 38

## \|\| 3.1.39 \|\|

*tāmasasyāntare caiva saṃprāpto punareva hi \|*  
*haryāyāṃ haribhiḥ sārdha harireva babhūva ha \|\| 39 \|\|*

And when the period of Tāmasa began, he was born again to Harī as Hari, along with the Hari gods. 39

## \|\| 3.1.40 \|\|

*raivatepyantare devaḥsaṃbhūtyāṃ mānaso hariḥ \|*  
*saṃbhūto raivataiḥ sārdha devair devavaro hariḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

During the period of Raivata, the most excellent deity Hari was born as Saṃbhūta, mind-begotten son of Saṃbhūtī, along with the Rājasa deities. 40

## \|\| 3.1.41 \|\|

*cākṣuṣe cāntare devo vaikuṇṭhaḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|*  
*vikuṇṭhāyāmasau jajñe vaikuṇṭhair daivataiḥ saha \|\| 41 \|\|*

In the period of Cākṣuṣa, the highest divine spirit was born as Vaikuṇṭha to Vikuṇṭhā, together with the Vaikuṇṭha gods. 41

## \|\| 3.1.42 \|\|

*manvantaretra saṃprāpte tathā vaivasvatedvija \|*  
*vāmanaḥ kāśyapādviṣṇuradityāṃ saṃbabhūva ha \|\| 42 \|\|*

In the present Vaivasvata Manvantara, brahmin, Viṣṇu was born as Vāmana, the divine dwarf, the son of Kaśyapa and Aditi. 42

## \|\| 3.1.43 \|\|

*tribhiḥ kramair imāṃl lokāñ jitvā yena mahātmanā \|*  
*purandarāya trailokyaṃ dattaṃ nihatakaṇṭakam \|\| 43 \|\|*

That great being conquered the threefold worlds in just three strides and entrusted them to Indra, vanquisher of cities, having freed them from all obstacles. 43

## \|\| 3.1.44 \|\|

*ityetāstanavastasya saptamanvantareṣu vai \|*  
*saptasvevābhavanvipra yābhiḥ saṃvardhitāḥ prajāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Such are the seven forms of Viṣṇu, brahmin, arising in each of the seven Manvantaras for the preservation of all creatures. 44

## \|\| 3.1.45 \|\|

*yasmād viṣṭamidaṃ viśvaṃ yasya śaktyā mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*tasmātsa procyate viṣṇurviśordhātoḥ praveśanāt \|\| 45 \|\|*

Because the whole world is infused (viṣṭam) with the energy of that great being, he is called Viṣṇu, from the root viś, meaning ‘enter’. 45

## \|\| 3.1.46 \|\|

*sarve ca devā manavaḥsamastāḥsaptarṣayo ye manusūnavaś ca \|*  
*indraścayo 'yaṃ tridaśeśabhūto viṣṇoraśoṣāstu vibhūtayastāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

All the gods, the Manus, the seven sages, the Manus’ sons, and whoever is Indra, lord of thirteen deities, are all just forms of Viṣṇu’s might. 46

## Chapter 2 - The future Manvantaras

## \|\| 3.2.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*proktānyetāni bhavatā sapta manvantarāṇi vai \|*  
*bhaviṣyāṇyapi viprarṣa mamākhyātuṃ tvamarhasi \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve described these seven Manvantaras, brahmin sage, please tell me now about the periods yet to come. 1

## \|\| 3.2.2 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sūryasya patnī saṃjñābhūttanayā viśvakarmaṇaḥ \|*  
*manuryamo yamī caiva tadapatyāni vai mune \|\| 2 \|\|*

Viśvakarman’s daughter Saṃjñā was married to the Sun, and their offspring were the Manu Vaivasvata, Yama and Yamī, sage. 2

## \|\| 3.2.3 \|\|

*asahantī tu sā bhartustejaśchāyāṃ yuyoja vai \|*  
*bhartṛśuśrūṣaṇe 'raṇyaṃsvayaṃ ca tapase yayau \|\| 3 \|\|*

Unable to endure her husband’s energy, Saṃjñā engaged Chāyā to serve him, and she herself retired to the forest to undertake austerities. 3

## \|\| 3.2.4 \|\|

*saṃjñeyamityathārkaś ca chāyāyāmātmajatrayam \|*  
*śanaiścaraṃ manuṃ cānyaṃ tapatīṃ cāpyajājanat \|\| 4 \|\|*

Thinking Chāyā to be Saṃjñā, the Sun had three more children with her: sluggish Saturn, another Manu named Sāvarṇi, as well as Tapatī. 4

## \|\| 3.2.5 \|\|

*chāyāsaṃjñā dadau śāpaṃ yamāya kupitā yadā \|*  
*tadānyeyamasau buddhirityāsīdyamasūryayoḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

One day, Chāyā, enraged with Yama, cursed him. Only then did Yama and the Sun realise that she wasn’t Saṃjñā. 5

## \|\| 3.2.6 \|\|

*tato vivasvānākhyātāṃ tayaivāraṇyasaṃsthitām \|*  
*samādhidṛṣṭyādadṛśe tāmaśvāṃ tapasi sthitām \|\| 6 \|\|*

When Chāyā admitted to the Sun that Saṃjñā was in the forest, he perceived through his yogic power that she was practising austerities in the form of a mare. 6

## \|\| 3.2.7 \|\|

*vājirūpadharaḥ sotha tasyāṃ devāvathāśvinau \|*  
*janayāmāsa revantaṃ retasoṃte ca bhāskaraḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Appearing as a stallion, the Sun mounted Saṃjñā and sired the heavenly twins the Aśvins, as well as Revanta. 7

## \|\| 3.2.8 \|\|

*āninye ca punaḥ saṃjñāṃ svasthānaṃ bhagavān raviḥ \|*  
*tejasaḥ śamanaṃ cāsya viśvakarmā cakāra ha \|\| 8 \|\|*

The lordly Sun then led Saṃjñā to his own abode, where Viśvakarman pared back his energy. 8

## \|\| 3.2.9 \|\|

*bhramamāropya sūryaṃ tu tasya tejoniśātanam \|*  
*kṛtavānaṣṭamaṃ bhāgaṃ sa vyaśātayadavyayam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Turning the Sun upon his lathe, the heavenly craftsman reduced his brilliance by just one-eighth, without impairing his eternal light. 9

## \|\| 3.2.10 \|\|

*yattasmādvaiṣṇavaṃ tejasśātitaṃ viśvakarmaṇā \|*  
*jājvalyamānamapatattadbhūmau munisattama \|\| 10 \|\|*

The blazing energy of the Sun shaved off by Viśvakarman fell back to earth, best of sages. 10

## \|\| 3.2.11 \|\|

*tvaṣṭaiva tejasā tena viṣṇoścakramakalpayat \|*  
*triśūlaṃ caiva śarvasya śibikāṃ dhanadasya ca \|\| 11 \|\|*

There, the heavenly artisan Tvaṣṭṛ used that energy to fashion Viṣṇu’s discus, Śiva’s trident and a palanquin for wealth-giving Kubera. 11

## \|\| 3.2.12 \|\|

*śaktiṃ guhasya devānāmanyeṣāṃ ca yadāyudham \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ tejasā tena viśvakarmā vyavardhayat \|\| 12 \|\|*

Viśvakarman then used the rest to make Kārttikeya’s lance and all the other deities’ weapons. 12

## \|\| 3.2.13 \|\|

*chāyāsaṃjñāsuto yosau dvitīyaḥ kathito manuḥ \|*  
*pūrvajasyāsavarṇosau sāvarṇistena kathyate \|\| 13 \|\|*

Chāyā’s second son was also called a Manu and, because he had the same complexion (savarṇa) as his elder brother, he was called Sāvarṇi. 13

## \|\| 3.2.14 \|\|

*tasya manvantaraṃ hyotatsāvarṇikamathāṣṭamam \|*  
*tacchṛṇuṣva mahābhāga bhaviṣyatkathayāmi te \|\| 14 \|\|*

His Manvantara, the Sāvarṇi period, will be the eighth. Listen, blessed sage, while I describe it for you. 14

## \|\| 3.2.15 \|\|

*sāvarṇistu manuryosau maitreya bhavitā tataḥ \|*  
*sutapāścāmitābhāś ca mukhyāścāpi tathā surāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Sāvarṇi will be the following Manu, Maitreya, and the Sutapas, Amitābhas and Mukhyas will be the gods. 15

## \|\| 3.2.16 \|\|

*teṣāṃ gaṇāś ca devānāmekaiko viṃśakaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*saptarṣinapi vakṣyāmi bhaviṣyānmunisattama \|\| 16 \|\|*

Each host will include twenty deities. Now I’ll name the seven sages, best of seers: 16

## \|\| 3.2.17 \|\|

*dīptimān gālavo rāmaḥ kṛpo droṇis tathā paraḥ \|*  
*matputraś ca tathā vyāsa ṛṣyaśṛṅgaś ca saptamaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Dīptimant, Gālava, Rāma, Kṛpa, Drauṇi and my son Vyāsa, with Ṛśyaśṛṅga as the seventh. 17

## \|\| 3.2.18 \|\|

*viṣṇu prasādādanaghaḥ pātālāntaragocaraḥ \|*  
*virocanasutasteṣāṃ balirindro bhaviṣyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

By the grace of Viṣṇu, Bali, blameless son of Virocana, whose realm is the underworld of Pātāla, will be the king of gods. 18

## \|\| 3.2.19 \|\|

*virajāścorvarīvāṃś ca nirmokādyāstathāpare \|*  
*sāvarṇestu manoḥ putrā bhaviṣyanti nareśvarāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The kings Virajas, Arvarīvant, Nirmoha and others will be Manu Sāvarṇi’s sons. 19

## \|\| 3.2.20 \|\|

*navamo dakṣasāvarṇirbhaviṣyati mune manuḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

The ninth Manu will be Dakṣasāvarṇi, Maitreya. 20

## \|\| 3.2.21 \|\|

*pārā marīcigarbhāś ca sudharmāṇas tathā tridhā \|*  
*bhaviṣyanti tathā devā hyokaiko dvādaśo gaṇaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Pāras, Marīcigarbhas and Sudharmans will be the threefold hosts of gods then, each with a dozen deities. 21

## \|\| 3.2.22 \|\|

*teṣāmidro mahāvīryo bhaviṣyatyadbhuto dvija \|\| 22 \|\|*

Their king will be mighty Adbhuta, brahmin. 22

## \|\| 3.2.23 \|\|

*savano dyutimān bhavyo vasurmedhātithis tathā \|*  
*jyotiṣmān saptamaḥ satyastatraite ca maharṣayaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Savana, Dyutimant, Bhavya, Vasu, Medhātithi and Jyotiṣmant, with Satya as the seventh—these will be the seven great sages. 23

## \|\| 3.2.24 \|\|

*dhṛtaketurdīptiketuḥ pañcahastanirāmayau \|*  
*pṛthuśravādyāś ca tathā dakṣamāvarṇikātmajāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Dhṛtaketu, Dīptiketu, Pañcahasta, Nirāmaya, Pṛthuśravas and others will be Dakṣasāvarṇi’s sons. 24

## \|\| 3.2.25 \|\|

*dasamo brahmasāvarmirbhaviṣyati mune manuḥ \|*  
*sūdhāmāno viśuddhāś ca śatasaṃkhyās tathā surāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

The tenth Manu will be Brahmasāvarṇi, sage. The gods will be Sudhāmans, Viruddhas and Śatasaṃkhyas. 25

## \|\| 3.2.26 \|\|

*teṣām indraś ca bhavitā śāntir nāma mahābalaḥ \|*  
*saptarṣayo bhaviṣyanti ye tathā tāñchṛṇuṣva ha \|\| 26 \|\|*

Mighty Śānti by name will be their king. Now hear about the seven sages of that period: 26

## \|\| 3.2.27 \|\|

*haviṣmānsukṛtaḥsatyastapomūrtistathāparaḥ \|*  
*nābhāgo 'pratimaujāścasatyaketustathaiva ca \|\| 27 \|\|*

Haviṣmant, Sukṛti, Satya, Apāṃmūrti, Nābhāga, Apratimaujas and Satyaketu. 27

## \|\| 3.2.28 \|\|

*sukṣetraścottamaujāścabhūriṣeṇādayo daśa \|*  
*brahmasāvarṇiputrāstu rakṣiṣyanti vasuṃdharām \|\| 28 \|\|*

The ten sons of Brahmasāvarṇi—Sukṣetra, Uttamaujas, Bhūriṣeṇa and the rest—will protect the earth. 28

## \|\| 3.2.29 \|\|

*ekādaśaś ca bhavitā dharmasāvarṇiko manuḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

The eleventh Manu will be Dharmasāvarṇika. 29

## \|\| 3.2.30 \|\|

*vihaṅgamāḥ kāmāgamā nirvāṇā ṛṣayas tathā \|*  
*gaṇāstvete tadā mukhyā devānāṃ ca bhaviṣyatām \|*  
*aikaikastriṃśakasteṣāṃ gaṇaścendraś ca vai pṛṣā \|\| 30 \|\|*

The Vihaṃgamas, Kāmagamas and Nirmāṇarucis will be the principal hosts of deities then, each comprising thirty gods, with Vṛṣa as their king. 30

## \|\| 3.2.31 \|\|

*niḥ svaraścāgnitejāś ca vapuṣmānghaṇirāruṇiḥ \|*  
*haviṣmānanaghaścaiva bhāvyāḥ saptarṣayas tathā \|\| 31 \|\|*

Niścara, Agnitejas, Vapuṣmant, Viṣṇu, Āruṇi, Haviṣmant and Anagha will be the seven sages. 31

## \|\| 3.2.32 \|\|

*sarvatragasśudharmā ca devānīkādayas tathā \|*  
*bhaviṣyanti manostasya tanayāḥ pṛtiviśvarāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

The kings Sarvaga, Sarvadharman, Devānīka and others will be that Manu’s sons. 32

## \|\| 3.2.33 \|\|

*rudraputrastu sāvarṇirbhavitā dvādaśo manuḥ \|*  
*ṛkudhāmā ca tatredro bhavitā śṛṇu me surān \|\| 33 \|\|*

The twelfth Manu will be Sāvarṇi, son of Rudra, with Ṛtudhāman as king of gods. Now hear about the deities from me. 33

## \|\| 3.2.34 \|\|

*haritā rohitā devās tathā mumanaso dvija \|*  
*sukarmāṇaḥ surāpāś ca daśakāḥ pañca vai gaṇāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

The Haritas, divine Lohitas, Sumanases, Sukarmans and Supāras will be the hosts of gods, each with fifteen deities, brahmin. 34

## \|\| 3.2.35 \|\|

*tapasvī sutapāścaiva tapomūrtistaporatiḥ \|*  
*tapodhṛtirdyutiścānyaḥ saptamastu tapodhanaḥ \|*  
*saptarṣayastvime tasya putrānapi nibodha me \|\| 35 \|\|*

Tapasvin, Sutapas, Tapomūrti, Taporati, Tapodhṛti, with Dyuti as another and Tapodhana as the seventh will be the sages. 35

## \|\| 3.2.36 \|\|

*devavānupadevaś ca devaśreṣṭhādayas tathā \|*  
*manostasya mahāvīryā bhaviṣyanti mahānṛpāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

The kings Devavant, Upadeva, Devaśreṣṭha and others will be that Manu’s mighty sons. 36

## \|\| 3.2.37 \|\|

*trayodaśo rucirnāmā bhaviṣyati mune manaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

The thirteenth Manu will be known as Raucya, sage. 37

## \|\| 3.2.38 \|\|

*sutrāmāṇaḥ sukarmāṇaḥ sudharmāṇastathāmarāḥ \|*  
*trayastriṃśadvibhedāsta devānāṃ yatra vai gaṇāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

The Sutrāmans, Sukarmans and Sudharmans will be the various hosts of gods, each with thirty-three deities. 38

## \|\| 3.2.39 \|\|

*divaspatirmahāvīryasteṣāmindrobhiviṣyati \|\| 39 \|\|*

Mighty Divaspati will be their king. 39

## \|\| 3.2.40 \|\|

*nirmohastattvadarśi ca niṣprakaṃpyo nirutsukaḥ \|*  
*dhṛtimānavyayaścānyaḥsaptamaḥsutapā muniḥ \|*  
*saptarṣayastvamī tasya putrānapi nibodhe me \|\| 40 \|\|*

The seven sages will be Nirmoha, Tattvadarśin, Niṣprakampa, Nirutsuka, Dhṛtimant and Avyaya, with Sutapas as the seventh. 40

## \|\| 3.2.41 \|\|

*citrasenavicitrādyā bhaviṣyanti mahīkṣikaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

The kings will be Citrasena, Vicitra and others. 41

## \|\| 3.2.42 \|\|

*bhaumaścaturdaśaścātra maitreya bhavitā manaḥ \|*  
*śucirindraḥ suragaṇastatra pañca śṛṇuṣva tān \|\| 42 \|\|*

Bhautya will be the fourteenth Manu, Maitreya. Śuci will be the king of gods and there’ll be five heavenly hosts. Listen to their names: 42

## \|\| 3.2.43 \|\|

*cākṣuṣāś ca pavitrāś ca kaniṣṭhā bhrājikās tathā \|*  
*vācavṛddhaś ca vai devāḥsaptarṣīnapi me śṛṇu \|\| 43 \|\|*

The Cākṣuṣas, Pavitras, Kaniṣṭhas, Bhrājiras and Vācāvṛddhas will be the deities. Now hear from me the seven sages: 43

## \|\| 3.2.44 \|\|

*agnibāhuḥ śuciḥ śukro māgadhognīdhra eva ca \|*  
*yuktas tathā jitaścānyo manuputrānataḥ śṛṇu \|\| 44 \|\|*

Agnibāhu, Śuci, Śukra, Māgadha, Agrīdhra and Yukta, with Jita as the other. Listen to the Manu’s sons: 44

## \|\| 3.2.45 \|\|

*ūrugaṃbhīrabuddhyādyā manostasya sutā nṛpāḥ \|*  
*kathitā muniśārdula pālayiṣyanti ye mahīm \|\| 45 \|\|*

The kings Ūru, Gambhīra, Budhna and others will be the sons of Manu. Those whom I’ve named will protect the earth, you tiger of a sage. 45

## \|\| 3.2.46 \|\|

*caturyugānte vedānāṃ jāyate kila viplavaḥ \|*  
*pravartayanti tānetya bhuvaṃ saptarṣayo divaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

At the end of every cycle of four ages, the Vedas disappear, but seven sages descend from heaven to the world to propound them once again. 46

## \|\| 3.2.47 \|\|

*kṛtekṛte smṛter vipra praṇetā jāyate manuḥ \|*  
*devā yajñabhujaste tu yāvanmanvantaraṃ tu tat \|\| 47 \|\|*

In each Kṛta age, a Manu arises to promulgate smṛti, the sacred texts recalled by men, brahmin. The deities enjoy the sacrifices during each Manvantara. 47

## \|\| 3.2.48 \|\|

*bhavanti ye manoḥ putrā yāvanmanvaṃrataṃ tu taiḥ \|*  
*tadanvayodbhavaiś caiva tāvadbhūḥ parīpālyate \|\| 48 \|\|*

The Manu’s sons and their descendants also rule the world for the duration of that period. 48

## \|\| 3.2.49 \|\|

*manuḥsaptarṣayo devā bhūpālāś ca manoḥ sutāḥ \|*  
*manvantare bhavantyete śakraścaivādhikārimaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

A Manu, seven sages, the deities, the Manu’s sons who rule the earth and one king of gods preside over every period. 49

## \|\| 3.2.50 \|\|

*caturdaśabhir etais tu gatair manvantarair dvija \|*  
*sahasrayugaparyataḥ kalpo niḥśeṣa ucyate \|\| 50 \|\|*

When fourteen Manvantaras have passed, an aeon or kalpa of one thousand cycles of four ages is said to be complete. 50

## \|\| 3.2.51 \|\|

*tāvatpramāṇā ca niśā tato bhavati sattama \|*  
*brahmarūpadharaḥ śete śeṣāhāvaṃbusaṃplave \|\| 51 \|\|*

Then comes a night of the same duration, best of sages, during which Viṣṇu, who also takes the form of Brahmā, reposes on the serpent Śeṣa in the middle of the ocean. 51

## \|\| 3.2.52 \|\|

*trailokyamakhilaṃ grastvā bhagavānādikṛdvibhuḥ \|*  
*svamāyāsaṃstito vipra sarvabhūto janārdanaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

After consuming all three worlds, the lord, creator and universal Janārdana, rests amid his own illusory power, brahmin. 52

## \|\| 3.2.53 \|\|

*tataḥ prabuddho bhagavān yathā pūrvaṃ tathā punaḥ \|*  
*sṛṣṭiṃ karotyavyayātmā kalpekalpe rajogumaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

Following which, the eternal lord awakes, and, taking on the quality of energy, creates the world again at the onset of each new aeon as before. 53

## \|\| 3.2.54 \|\|

*manavo bhūbhujaḥseṃdrā devāḥsaptarṣayas tathā \|*  
*sāttvikoṃśaḥ sthitikaro jagato dvijasattama \|\| 54 \|\|*

Viṣṇu’s stainless aspect—which manifests as Manus, sovereigns, seven sages, deities and their kings—maintains the universe, best of brahmins. 54

## \|\| 3.2.55 \|\|

*caduryugopyasau viṣṇuḥ sthitivyāpāralakṣamaḥ \|*  
*yugavyavasthāṃ kurute tathā maitreya tacchṛṇu \|\| 55 \|\|*

Now you’ll hear how Viṣṇu, in the character of the maintainer of the world, undertakes its preservation during every age, Maitreya. 55

## \|\| 3.2.56 \|\|

*kṛte yuge paraṃ jñānaṃ kapilādisvarūpadhṛk \|*  
*dadāti sarvabhūtātmā sarvabhūtahite rataḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

In the Kṛta, Viṣṇu, at the heart of all creation, delighting in the welfare of the world, takes the form of Kapila and other sages, and teaches highest knowledge. 56

## \|\| 3.2.57 \|\|

*cakravartisvarūpeṇa tretāyāmapi sa prabhūḥ \|*  
*duṣṭānāṃ nigraha kurvanparipāti jagattrayam \|\| 57 \|\|*

In the Tretā, in the form of a universal monarch, the lord protects the threefold worlds while destroying wicked beings. 57

## \|\| 3.2.58 \|\|

*vedamekaṃ caturbhedaṃ kṛtvā śākhāśatair vibhuḥ \|*  
*karoti bahulaṃ bhūyo vedavyāsasvarūpadhṛk \|\| 58 \|\|*

In the form of Veda-vyāsa, having split the single Veda into four, the lord arranges these in lesser branches by the hundreds. 58

## \|\| 3.2.59 \|\|

*vedāṃstu dvāpare vyasya kalerante punarhariḥ \|*  
*kalkisvarūpī durvṛttanmārge sthāpayati prabhuḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

Once he’s arranged the Vedas in the Dvāpara, Lord Hari takes the form of Kalki at the end of the Kali yuga, setting sinners on the path of virtue. 59

## \|\| 3.2.60 \|\|

*evametajjagatsarvaṃ śaśvatpāti karoti ca \|*  
*hanti cānteṣvanantātmā nāstyasmavdyatireki yat \|\| 60 \|\|*

In this way, the infinite being creates, protects and, in the end, destroys the world. There’s nothing more beside him. 60

## \|\| 3.2.61 \|\|

*bhūtaṃ bhavyaṃ bhaviṣyaṃ ca sarvabhūtānmahātmanaḥ \|*  
*tadatrānyatra vā vipra sadbhāvaḥ kathitastava \|\| 61 \|\|*

I’ve described to you the past, the present and the future of creation, brahmin, and mighty Viṣṇu’s actual nature in this world and elsewhere. 61

## \|\| 3.2.62 \|\|

*manvantarāṇyaśeṣāṇi kathitāni mayā tavā \|*  
*manvantarādhipāṃścaiva kimanyatkathayāmi te \|\| 62 \|\|*

I’ve also told you all about the Manvantaras and those who oversee them. What else would you like to hear? 62

## Chapter 3 - The Vyāsas arrange the Vedas; The syllable Oṃ

## \|\| 3.3.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*jñātametanmayā tvatto yathā sarvamidaṃ jagat \|*  
*viṣṇurviṣṇo viṣṇutaś ca na paraṃ vidyate tataḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

I’ve learned from you how this world is Viṣṇu, is in Viṣṇu, is from Viṣṇu and how there’s nothing else beside him. 1

## \|\| 3.3.2 \|\|

*etattu śrotumicchāmi vyastā vedā mahātmanā \|*  
*vedavyāsasvarūpeṇa yathā tena yugeyuge \|\| 2 \|\|*

But I’d like to hear how the Vedas are arranged by that great being in the form of a Veda-vyāsa, or ‘arranger of the Vedas’, in each successive age. 2

## \|\| 3.3.3 \|\|

*yasminyasminyuge vyāso yo ya āsīnmahāmune \|*  
*tantamācakṣva bhagavañchāśābhedāṃś ca me vada \|\| 3 \|\|*

Tell me of the varied forms of Vyāsa that appear in every aeon, sage and master, and how the Vedic branches are divided. 3

## \|\| 3.3.4 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*vedadrumasya maitreya śākhābhedāḥsahasraśaḥ \|*  
*na śakto vistarādvaktuṃ sekṣepeṇa śṛṇuṣva tam \|\| 4 \|\|*

As the Veda-tree is divided into branches by the thousand, Maitreya, it’s impossible to describe them one by one, so listen to this summary. 4

## \|\| 3.3.5 \|\|

*dvāparedvāpare viṣṇurvyāsarūpī mahāmune \|*  
*vedamekaṃ subahudhā kurute jagato hitaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

In each Dvāpara age, Viṣṇu takes the form of Vyāsa, sage, and divides the single Veda into several parts to benefit the world. 5

## \|\| 3.3.6 \|\|

*vīryaṃ tejo balaṃ cālpaṃ manuṣyāṇāmavekṣya ca \|*  
*hitāya sarvabhūtānāṃ vedabhedānkaroti saḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Seeing humankind lacks courage, strength and energy, he divides the Veda for the good of every being. 6

## \|\| 3.3.7 \|\|

*yayāsau kurute tanvā vedamekaṃ pṛthak prabhuḥ \|*  
*vedavyāsābhidhānā tu sā ca mūrtirmadhrudviṣaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

The form that Viṣṇu takes, that enemy of Madhu, when he divides the Veda, is known as ‘Veda-vyāsa’. 7

## \|\| 3.3.8 \|\|

*yasminmanvaṃrate vyāsā yeye syustānnibodha me \|*  
*yathā ca bhedaḥ śākhānāṃ vyāsena kriyate mune \|\| 8 \|\|*

Now you’ll learn about the varied Vyāsas who appear in this Manvantara, sage, and how the division into branches is undertaken. 8

## \|\| 3.3.9 \|\|

*aṣṭaviṃśatikṛtvo vai vedo vyasto saharṣibhiḥ \|*  
*vaivasvatentare tasmindvāpareṣu punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

The Vedas have been divided on twenty-eight occasions by great sages during each successive Dvāpara age in this Vaivasvata Manvantara. 9

## \|\| 3.3.10 \|\|

*vedavyāsā vyatītā ye hyaṣṭarviśati sattama \|*  
*caturdhā yaiḥ kṛto vedo dvāpareṣu punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Twenty-eight Veda-vyāsas, who arranged the Vedas into four in each Dvāpara age, best of sages, have already gone by. 10

## \|\| 3.3.11 \|\|

*dvāpare prathame vyastaḥsvayaṃ vedaḥ svayaṃbhuvā \|*  
*dvitīye dvāpare caiva vedavyāsaḥ prajāpati \|\| 11 \|\|*

In the first Dvāpara, the Vedas were arranged by the self-existent deity Brahmā. In the second, the Veda-vyāsa was the patriarch Manu. 11

## \|\| 3.3.12 \|\|

*tṛtīye cośanā vyāsaścaturthe ca bṛhaspatiḥ \|*  
*savitā pañcame vyāsaḥ ṣaṣṭhe mṛtyuḥ smṛtaḥ prabhuḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

In the third, the arranger was Uśanas, and in the fourth Bṛhaspati. Savitṛ was the arranger in the fifth, and in the sixth was Mṛtyu, Lord of Death. 12

## \|\| 3.3.13 \|\|

*saptame ca tathaivendro vasiṣṭhaścāṣṭame smṛtaḥ \|*  
*sārasvataś ca navame tridhāmā daśame smṛtaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

In the seventh it was Indra, and Vasiṣṭha in the eighth, Sārasvata in the ninth and Tridhāman in the tenth. 13

## \|\| 3.3.14 \|\|

*ekādaśe tu triśikho bharadvājastataḥ paraḥ \|*  
*trayodaśe cāntarikṣo varṇī cāpi caturdaśe \|\| 14 \|\|*

In the eleventh it was Trivṛṣan, and Bhāradvāja followed him. In the thirteenth it was Antarikṣa, and Varṇin in the fourteenth, 14

## \|\| 3.3.15 \|\|

*trayyāruṇaḥ pañcadaśe ṣoḍaśe tu dhanañjayaḥ \|*  
*kratuñjayaḥ saptadaśe tadūrdhvaṃ ca jayaḥsmṛtaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Trayyāruṇa in the fifteenth, in the sixteenth Dhanaṃjaya, Kṛtaṃjaya in the seventeenth and Ṛṇjaya in the eighteenth. 15

## \|\| 3.3.16 \|\|

*tato vyāso bharadvājo bharadvājācca gautamaḥ \|*  
*gautamāduttaro vyāso haryātmā yobhidhīyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

The next Vyāsa was Bharadvāja, and after him came Gautama. After Gautama the Vyāsa was Uttama, also known as Haryātman. 16

## \|\| 3.3.17 \|\|

*atha haryātmanonte ca smṛto vājaśravāmuniḥ \|*  
*somaśuṣkāyaṇas tasmāt tṛṇabinduriti smṛtaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

After Haryātman was Vena or Vājaśravas, then Soma Śuṣmāyaṇa or Tṛṇabindu. 17

## \|\| 3.3.18 \|\|

*ṛkṣobhūdbhārgavastasmādvālmīkiryobhidhīyate \|*  
*tasmādasmatpitā śaktirvyāsastasmādahaṃ mune \|\| 18 \|\|*

Then came Ṛkṣa, scion of Bhṛgu, also known as Vālmīki. After him was my father, Śakti, sage, and I was the Vyāsa after him. 18

## \|\| 3.3.19 \|\|

*jātukarṇobhavanmattaḥ kṛṣṇadvaipāyanastataḥ \|*  
*aṣṭaviṃśatirityete vedavyāsāḥ purātanāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Jātukarṇa followed me, then Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana. These are the twenty-eight Veda-vyāsas of ancient times. 19

## \|\| 3.3.20 \|\|

*eko vedaścaturdhā tu taiḥ kṛto dvāparādiṣu \|\| 20 \|\|*

They split the single Veda into four in each Dvāpara age. 20

## \|\| 3.3.21 \|\|

*bhaviṣye dvāpare cāpi drauṇirvyāso bhaviṣyati \|*  
*vyatīte mama putresmin kṛṣṇadvaipāyane mune \|\| 21 \|\|*

In the next Dvāpara period, Droṇa’s son Drauṇi will be the Vyāsa after my son, the sage Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana, has passed. 21

## \|\| 3.3.22 \|\|

*dhruvamekākṣaraṃ brahma om ityeva vyavasthitam \|*  
*bṛhatvādbṛṃhaṇatvācca tadbrahyotyabhidhīyate \|\| 22 \|\|*

Oṃ is defined as the Absolute (brahman) in the form of one eternal syllable. It’s so called on account of its greatness (bṛhatvāt) and its capacity to strengthen (bṛṃhaṇatvāt). 22

## \|\| 3.3.23 \|\|

*praṇavāvasthitaṃ nityaṃ bhūrbhuvaḥsvaritīryate \|*  
*ṛgyajuḥsāmātharvāṇo yattasmai brahmaṇe namaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

I bow to the Absolute in the form of the everlasting syllable, Oṃ, invoked as ‘earth, sky and heaven’ (bhūr, bhuvas, svaḥ) in the Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas. 23

## \|\| 3.3.24 \|\|

*jagataḥ pralayotpattyoryattatkāraṇasaṃjñitam \|*  
*mahataḥ paramaṃ guhyaṃ tasmai subrahmaṇe namaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

I bow to the Absolute, known as the cause of creation and destruction of the world, the deepest secret of mahat, the principle of Greatness. 24

## \|\| 3.3.25 \|\|

*agādhāpāramakṣayyaṃ jagatsaṃmohanālayam \|*  
*svaprakāśapravṛttibhyāṃ puruṣārthaprayojanam \|\| 25 \|\|*

Unfathomable, unbound and undecaying, it’s the source of illusion in the world. With brilliance and efficacy, it’s the cause by which the purpose of the highest spirit is accomplished. 25

## \|\| 3.3.26 \|\|

*saṃkhyajñānavatāṃ niṣṭhā gatiḥ śamadamātmanām \|*  
*yattadavyaktamamṛtaṃ pravṛttirbrahma śāśvatam \|\| 26 \|\|*

The Absolute is the crown for those who know the Sāṃkhya teachings, and the goal for those who pacify and regulate themselves. It’s unmanifest, undying efficacy. 26

## \|\| 3.3.27 \|\|

*pradhānamātmayoniś ca guhāsaṃsthaṃ ca śabdyate \|*  
*avibhāgaṃ tathā śukramakṣayaṃ bahudhātmakam \|\| 27 \|\|*

It’s also called the essence, the origin of the Self, the heart of all reality. It’s indivisible, pure, imperishable and manifold. 27

## \|\| 3.3.28 \|\|

*paramabrahmaṇe tasmai nityameva namonamaḥ \|*  
*yadrūpaṃ vāsudevasya paramātmasvarūpiṇaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

I bow again and again to that eternal, highest Absolute, a form of Vāsudeva, who is himself a form of the highest spirit. 28

## \|\| 3.3.29 \|\|

*etadbrahma tridhā bhedamabhedamapi sa prabhuḥ \|*  
*sarvabhedeṣvabhedosaubhidyate bhinnabuddhibhiḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

This Absolute is the threefold lord, yet also one. That undivided being is thought to be divided among all creation by those who regard him as divisible. 29

## \|\| 3.3.30 \|\|

*sa ṛṅmayaḥsāmamayaḥ sarvātmā sa yajurmayaḥ \|*  
*ṛgyajuḥsāmasārātmā sa evātmā śarīriṇām \|\| 30 \|\|*

This universal being, composed of the Ṛg, Sāma and Yajur Vedas, is their essence. He is indeed the heart of all embodied beings. 30

## \|\| 3.3.31 \|\|

*sa bhidyate vedamayaḥsvavedaṃ karoti bhedair bahubhiḥ saśākham \|*  
*śākhāpraṇetā sa samastaśākhājñānasvarūpo bhagavānasaṃgaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Consisting of the Vedas, he is divided, but he made these texts and splits them into many. The eternal lord is author of their branches, and takes the form of knowledge of them all. 31

## Chapter 4 - The origin of the four Vedas

## \|\| 3.4.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ādyo vedaścatuṣpādaḥ kṛtaḥ sāhasrasaṃmitaḥ \|*  
*tato daśagumaḥ kṛtsno yajñoyaṃ sarvakāmadhuk \|\| 1 \|\|*

The ancient four-limbed Veda had a hundred thousand verses, and all ten kinds of sacrifice, like a wish-fulfilling cow, are derived from it. 1

## \|\| 3.4.2 \|\|

*tatotra matsuto vyāso aṣṭaviṃśatimentare \|*  
*vedamekaṃ catuṣpādaṃ caturdhā vyabhajatprabhuḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

In this, the twenty-eighth Dvāpara age, my holy scion Vyāsa arranged that single four-limbed text. 2

## \|\| 3.4.3 \|\|

*yathā ca tena vai vyastā vedabyāsena dhīmatā \|*  
*vedās tathā samastais tair vyastā vyastais tathā mayā \|\| 3 \|\|*

Just as wise Veda-vyāsa arranged it, so was it divided by all the former Vyāsas and by me as well. 3

## \|\| 3.4.4 \|\|

*tadanenaiva vedānāṃ śākhābhedāndvijottama \|*  
*caturyugeṣu paṭhitānsamasteṣvavadhāraya \|\| 4 \|\|*

You should know that the division into branches that he undertook, best of brahmins, is likewise done in every fourfold era. 4

## \|\| 3.4.5 \|\|

*kṛṣṇadvaipāyanaṃ vyāsaṃ viddhi nārāyaṇaṃ prabhum \|*  
*ko hyanyo bhuvi maitreya mahābhāratakṛdbhavet \|\| 5 \|\|*

Understand that Vyāsa, Kṛṣṇa-Dvaipāyana, is Lord Nārāyaṇa himself, Maitreya. Who else on earth could compose the Mahābhārata? 5

## \|\| 3.4.6 \|\|

*tena vyastā yathā vedā matputreṇa mahātmanā \|*  
*dvāpare hy atra maitreya tasmiñ śṛṇu yathātatham \|\| 6 \|\|*

Listen now, Maitreya, while I explain how my great son arranged the Vedas during this Dvāpara age. 6

## \|\| 3.4.7 \|\|

*brahmaṇā codito vyāso vedānvyastuṃ pracakrame \|*  
*atha śiṣyānprajagrāha caturo vedapāragān \|\| 7 \|\|*

Urged by Brahmā, he began to organise the texts, and first accepted four disciples, all well-steeped in Vedic lore. 7

## \|\| 3.4.8 \|\|

*ṛgvedapāṭhakaṃ pailaṃ jagrāha sa mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*vaiśaṃpāyananāmānaṃ yajurvedasya cāgrahīt \|\| 8 \|\|*

That great sage adopted Paila as disciple for the Ṛg, and Vaiśampāyana for the Yajur Veda. 8

## \|\| 3.4.9 \|\|

*jaiminiṃ sāmavedasya tathaivātharvavedavit \|*  
*sumantustasya śiṣyobhūdvedavyāsasya dhīmataḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

For the Sāma, Veda-vyāsa took on Jaimini. Sumantu, who understood the Atharva Veda, became a student of that prudent sage. 9

## \|\| 3.4.10 \|\|

*romaharṣaṇanāmānaṃ mahābuddhiṃ mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*sūtaṃ jagrāha śiṣyaṃ sa itihāsapurāṇayoḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Vyāsa accepted Romaharṣaṇa, a wise disciple, as narrator for the histories and purāṇas. 10

## \|\| 3.4.11 \|\|

*eka āsīdyajurvedastaṃ caturdhā vyakalpayat \|*  
*cāturhetramabhūttasmiṃstena yajñamathākarot \|\| 11 \|\|*

The Yajur Veda was a single text, but he divided it in four, and, in doing so, he brought about the fourfold cāturhotra sacrifice. 11

## \|\| 3.4.12 \|\|

*ādhvaryavaṃ yajurbhis tu ṛgbhir hotraṃ tathā muniḥ \|*  
*audgātraṃ sāmābhiścakre brahmatvaṃ cāpyatharvabhiḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

The sage assigned the yajus prayers to the director of the sacrifice, the adhvaryu; the ṛc verses to the sacrificing priest or hotṛ; the sāman hymns to the cantor or udgātṛ; and the atharva incantations to the brahmin. 12

## \|\| 3.4.13 \|\|

*tataḥsa ṛca uddhṛtya ṛgvedaṃ kṛtavānmuniḥ \|*  
*yajṛṃṣi ca yajurvedaṃ sāmavedaṃ ca sāmābhaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Vyāsa compiled the ṛc verses as the Ṛg Veda, yajus prayers as the Yajur Veda and the sāman hymns as the Sāma Veda. 13

## \|\| 3.4.14 \|\|

*rājñāṃ cātharvavedena sarvakarmāṇi ca prabhuḥ \|*  
*kārayāmāsa maitreya brahmatvaṃ ca yathāsthiti \|\| 14 \|\|*

With the atharva incantations, that master created all the rituals for the kings and for the office of the brahmins, as per usage. 14

## \|\| 3.4.15 \|\|

*soyameko yathā vedastarustena pṛthakkṛtaḥ \|*  
*caturdhātha tato jātaṃ vedapādapakānanam \|\| 15 \|\|*

He split in four that single spreading Veda-tree and so began a Veda forest. 15

## \|\| 3.4.16 \|\|

*bibheda prathamaṃ vipra pailo ṛgvedapādapam \|*  
*indrapramitaye prādādbāṣkalāya ca saṃhite \|\| 16 \|\|*

First, Paila split the Ṛg in two, and gave one part to Indrapramati and one to Bāṣkala. 16

## \|\| 3.4.17 \|\|

*caturdhā sa vibhedātha bāṣkalopi ca saṃhitām \|*  
*bodhādibhyo dadau tāś ca śiṣyebhyaḥ samahāmuniḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

Wise Bāṣkala split his part in four, and gave each one to his disciples, Baudhya and the others. 17

## \|\| 3.4.18 \|\|

*bodhyāgnimāḍhakau tadūdyājñavalkyaparāśarau \|*  
*pratiśākhāstu śākhāyāstasyāste jagṛhurmune \|\| 18 \|\|*

Baudhya, Agnimāṭhara, Yājñavalkya and Parāśara, sage, adopted these subsections of that branch. 18

## \|\| 3.4.19 \|\|

*indrapramitirekāṃ tu saṃhitāṃ svasūtaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*māṇḍukeyaṃ mahātmānaṃ maitreyādhyāpayattadā \|\| 19 \|\|*

Indrapramati taught one collection to his son, great Māṇḍukeya, Maitreya. 19

## \|\| 3.4.20 \|\|

*tasya śiṣyapraśiṣyebhyaḥ putraśiṣyakramādyayau \|\| 20 \|\|*

This was then passed down in turn from his disciples and their disciples to their sons and their disciples. 20

## \|\| 3.4.21 \|\|

*vedamitrastu śākalyaḥ saṃhitāṃ tāmadhītavān \|*  
*cakāra saṃhitāḥ pañca śiṣyebhyaḥ pradadau ca tāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

Vedamitra, also known as Śākalya, studied that collection. He divided it in five and gave each part to his disciples. 21

## \|\| 3.4.22 \|\|

*tasya śiṣyāstu ye pañca teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|*  
*mudgalo gomakhaścaiva vātsyaḥ śālīya eva ca \|*  
*śarīraḥ pañcamaścāsīnmaitreya sumahāmatiḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Now hear their names from me: Mudgala, Gālava, Vātsya and Śālīya, with the great sage Śiśira as the fifth, Maitreya. 22

## \|\| 3.4.23 \|\|

*saṃhitātritayaṃ cakre śākapūrṇastathetaraḥ \|*  
*niruktamakarottadūccaturthaṃ munisattama \|\| 23 \|\|*

Śākapūṇi split that same collection differently by dividing it in three and adding an appendix as the fourth division, best of sages. 23

## \|\| 3.4.24 \|\|

*kraiñco vaitālikastadvadbalākaś ca mahāmuniḥ \|*  
*niruktaś ca caturthobhūdvedavedāṅgapāragaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Krauñca, Vaitālaki and Balāka, that great sage, were his disciples, and Nirukta, master of the Vedas and their supplements, was the fourth. 24

## \|\| 3.4.25 \|\|

*ityetāḥ pratiśākhābhyo hyanuśākhā dvijottama \|*  
*bāṣkalaścāparāstistraḥsaṃhitāḥ kṛtavāndvija \|*  
*śiṣyaḥ kālāyanirgārgyastṛtīyaś ca kathājapaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

This is how the later branches grew from all these subbranches, best of brahmins. Bāṣkala made three other compilations, brahmin. Kālāyani was his disciple, as was Gārgya, with Java as the third. 25

## \|\| 3.4.26 \|\|

*ityetā bahvṛcāḥ proktāḥ saṃhitā yaiḥ pravartitāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

It is they who spread abroad these sets of Vedic verses. 26

## Chapter 5 - The branches of the Yajur Veda

## \|\| 3.5.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*yajurvetadarosśākhāḥsaptaviṃśanmahāmuniḥ \|*  
*vaiśaṃpāyananāmāso vyāsaśiṣyaścakāra vai \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa’s wise disciple Vaiśampāyana split the tree-like Yajur Veda into twenty-seven. 1

## \|\| 3.5.2 \|\|

*śiṣyebhyaḥ pradadau tāś ca jagṛhustepyanukramāt \|\| 2 \|\|*

He then conferred each branch upon his disciples, who accepted them in turn. 2

## \|\| 3.5.3 \|\|

*yājñavalkyastu tatrābhūdbāhyarātasuto dvija \|*  
*śiṣyaḥ paramadharmajño guruvṛttiparaḥsadā \|\| 3 \|\|*

His disciple Yājñavalkya, Brahmarāta’s son, understood the highest virtues and was always dedicated to the service of his master, brahmin. 3

## \|\| 3.5.4 \|\|

*ṛṣiryodya mahāmeroḥ samāje nāgamiṣyati \|*  
*tasya vai saptarātrāttu brahmahattyā bhaviṣyati \|\| 4 \|\|*

‘One who fails to join our gathering on Mount Meru for seven nights will be guilty of brahminicide.’ 4

## \|\| 3.5.5 \|\|

*pūrvamevaṃ munigaṇaiḥ samayo yaḥ kṛto dvija \|*  
*vaiśaṃpāyana ekastu taṃ vyatikrāntavāṃstadā \|\| 5 \|\|*

Such was the first agreement of the gathered sages, brahmin, but Vaiśampāyana was the only one to break it. 5

## \|\| 3.5.6 \|\|

*svastrīyaṃ bālakaṃ sotha padā spṛṣṭamatāḍayat \|\| 6 \|\|*

He later tripped over his sister’s son and caused the infant’s death. 6

## \|\| 3.5.7 \|\|

*śiṣyānāha sa bhoḥ śiṣyā brahmahattyāpahaṃ vratam \|*  
*caradhvaṃ matkṛte sarve na vicāryamidaṃ tathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

The sage called on his disciples to do penance to absolve him of his crime, yet none of them was willing. 7

## \|\| 3.5.8 \|\|

*athāha yājñavalkyastu kim ebhir bhagavan dvijaiḥ \|*  
*kleśitair alpatejobhiścāriṣyehamidaṃ vratam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Yājñavalkya then addressed his guru, ‘Master, how can I do penance with these lazy, mediocre brahmins?’ 8

## \|\| 3.5.9 \|\|

*tataḥ kruddho guruḥ prāha yājñavalkyaṃ mahāmunim \|*  
*mucyatāṃ yattvayādhītaṃ matto viprāvamānaka \|\| 9 \|\|*

His wise guru, enraged by this, replied to him, ‘Forget all that you have learned from me. You’re a failure as a brahmin! 9

## \|\| 3.5.10 \|\|

*nistejaso vadasyenānyattvaṃ brāhmaṇaṃpuṅgavān \|*  
*tena śiṣyeṇa nārthosti mamājñābhaṅgakāriṇā \|\| 10 \|\|*

You say these other brilliant men are mediocre, but what good are you, a follower who disobeys my words?’ 10

## \|\| 3.5.11 \|\|

*yājñavalkyastataḥ prāha bhaktyaitat te mayoditam \|*  
*mamāpyalaṃ tvayādhītaṃ yanmayā tadidaṃ dvija \|\| 11 \|\|*

Yājñavalkya said, ‘I told you this in good faith, brahmin, but now I’ve had enough. This is what I’ve learned from you!’ 11

## \|\| 3.5.12 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukto rudhirāktāni sarūpāṇi yajūṃṣisaḥ \|*  
*chardayitvā dadau tasmai yayau sa svecchayā muni \|\| 12 \|\|*

Parāśara:

So saying, the sage vomited the blood-stained yajus prayers in physical form, handed them to his guru and walked away. 12

## \|\| 3.5.13 \|\|

*yajūṃṣyatha visṛṣṭāni yājñavalkyena vai dvija \|*  
*jagṛhustittirā bhūtvā taittirīyāstu te tataḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

The other disciples all turned into partridges (tittira), brahmin, and gobbled up the prayers that Yājñavalkya had disgorged. That’s why these texts are known as Taittirīyas. 13

## \|\| 3.5.14 \|\|

*brahmahattyāvrataṃ cīrṇaṃ guruṇā coditais tu taiḥ \|*  
*cākrurādhvaryavaṃ te tu caraṇānmunisattama \|\| 14 \|\|*

Those who performed penance for brahminicide at the urging of their guru became known as Caraka priests, on account of their undertaking (caraṇa), best of sages. 14

## \|\| 3.5.15 \|\|

*yājñavalkyopi maitreya prāṇāyāmaparāyaṇaḥ \|*  
*tṛṣṭāva prayataḥsūryaṃ yajūṃṣyabhilaṣaṃstataḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

But Yājñavalkya, who knew the art of breath control, Maitreya, wanted to win back the yajus prayers and devoutly worshipped Sūrya. 15

## \|\| 3.5.16 \|\|

*yājñavalkya uvāca*  
*namaḥ savitre dvārāya mukter amitatejase \|*  
*ṛgyajuḥsāmabhūtāya trayīdhāmne ca te namaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Yājñavalkya:

I bow to you, Savitṛ, door to liberation, whose brilliance has no measure. The Ṛg, Yajur and Sāma Vedas are your three abodes; I bow to you. 16

## \|\| 3.5.17 \|\|

*namognīṣomabhūtāya jagataḥ kāraṇātmane \|*  
*bhāskarāya paraṃ tejaḥsauṣumṇarucibibhrate \|\| 17 \|\|*

In the form of fire and moon, you are the origin of the world, light-giving sun, pure energy and origin of the ray Sauṣumṇa. 17

## \|\| 3.5.18 \|\|

*kalākāṣṭhānimeṣādikālajñānātmarūpiṇe \|*  
*dhyeyāya viṣṇurūpāya paramākṣararūpiṇe \|\| 18 \|\|*

I bow to you, one with the notion of time, consisting of kalā, kāṣṭhā, nimeṣa and other measures. You should be contemplated as a form of Viṣṇu; you are the syllable Oṃ. 18

## \|\| 3.5.19 \|\|

*bibharti yaḥsuragaṇānāpyāyenduṃ svaraśmibhiḥ \|*  
*svadhāmṛtena ca pitṝṃs tasmai tṛptyātmane namaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

You support the hosts of deities, having sustained the moon with your own rays, and the ancestral spirits with sweet nectarine libations. I bow to you, the essence of nourishment. 19

## \|\| 3.5.20 \|\|

*himāṃbugharmavṛṣṭīnāṃ kartā bhartā ca yaḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*tasmai trikālarūpāya namaḥsūryāyavedhase \|\| 20 \|\|*

You bring cold and water, heat and rain, Lord, then take them away again. I bow to you who are three seasons, sun and arranger of all things. 20

## \|\| 3.5.21 \|\|

*apahanti tabho yaś ca jagatosya jagatpatiḥ \|*  
*tasya dhāmadharo devo namastasmai vivasvate \|\| 21 \|\|*

You dispel darkness from the world and are its lord. You are the deity who is the abode of goodness. I bow to you, the brilliant sun. 21

## \|\| 3.5.22 \|\|

*satkarmayogyo na jano naivāpaḥ śuddhikāraṇam \|*  
*yasminnanudite tasmai namo devāya bhāsvate \|\| 22 \|\|*

Before you’ve risen, people are unfit for virtuous acts, nor does water purify. I bow to you, resplendent god. 22

## \|\| 3.5.23 \|\|

*spṛṣṭo yadaṃśubhir lokaḥ kriyāyogyo hi jāyate \|*  
*pavitrakāraṇātmāya tasmai śuddhātmane namaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Touched by your rays, the world is fit for rituals. I bow to you, the cause of purity. You are purity itself. 23

## \|\| 3.5.24 \|\|

*namaḥ savitre sūryāya bhāskarāya vivasvate \|*  
*ādityāyādibhūtāya devādīnāṃ namo namaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

I bow to Savitṛ, the sun, light-bringer, the shining one. To Aditi’s offspring, first of beings including gods and all the rest, I bow and bow again. 24

## \|\| 3.5.25 \|\|

*hiraṇmayaṃ rathaṃ yasya ketavomṛtavājinaḥ \|*  
*vahanti bhuvanālokacakṣuṣaṃ taṃ namāmyaham \|\| 25 \|\|*

Your chariot is golden, your banners bestow nectar. I bow to you, the universal eye. 25

## \|\| 3.5.26 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityevamādibhis tena stūyamānaḥsa vai raviḥ \|*  
*vājirūpadharaḥ prāha vrīyatāmiti vāñchitam \|\| 26 \|\|*

Parāśara:

After Yājñavalkya had praised the Sun with these and other words, the deity took the form of a stallion. ‘Make a wish,’ he said. 26

## \|\| 3.5.27 \|\|

*yājñavalkyastadā prāha praṇipatya divākaram \|*  
*yajūṃṣi tāni me dehi yāni saṃti na me gurau \|\| 27 \|\|*

Falling to the ground, Yājñavalkya besought the Sun, maker of the day, ‘Give me those yajus prayers that even my guru doesn’t know.’ 27

## \|\| 3.5.28 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evamukto dadau tasmai yajūṃṣi bhagavānraviḥ \|*  
*ayātayāmasaṃjñāni yāni vetti na tadguruḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the lordly Sun bestowed on him the yajus prayers known as Ayātayāma, unknown even to his guru. 28

## \|\| 3.5.29 \|\|

*yajūṃṣi yair adhītāni tāni viprair dhijottama \|*  
*vājinaste samākhyātāḥ sūryopyaśvobhavadyataḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Because the Sun in equine form revealed these prayers, brahmins who learn them are called Vājins, ‘horse-possessors’, best of brahmins. 29

## \|\| 3.5.30 \|\|

*śākhābhedāstu teṣāṃ vai daśa pañca ca vājinām \|*  
*kāṇvādyāḥsumahābhāgā yājñavalkyāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

The Vājins are divided into fifteen branches, all stemming from Yājñavalkya, Kaṇva and other blessed sages. 30

## Chapter 6 - The branches of the Sāma and Atharva Vedas; The purāṇas

## \|\| 3.6.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*sāmavedataroḥ śākhā vyāsaśiṣyasya jauminiḥ \|*  
*krameṇa yena maitreya vibhedaśṛṇu tanmama \|\| 1 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Now hear of Vyāsa’s student Jaimini, Maitreya, who duly arranged the branches of the tree-like Sāma Veda. 1

## \|\| 3.6.2 \|\|

*susaṃtustasya putrobhūtsūtvānaścābhavatsutaḥ \|*  
*adhītavantau caikaikāṃ saṃhitāṃ tau māhamatī \|\| 2 \|\|*

Jaimini’s son was Sumantu and his son was Sukarman. These two wise men each mastered one compilation. 2

## \|\| 3.6.3 \|\|

*sahasrasaṃhitābhedaṃ sukarmātatsutastataḥ \|*  
*cakāra taṃ ca tacchiṣyau jagṛhāte mahāvratau \|\| 3 \|\|*

Sumantu’s son Sukarman created the Sāhasra Saṃhitā, a compilation with a thousand verses. This was taken up by his two disciples, both adherents of great vows: 3

## \|\| 3.6.4 \|\|

*hiraṇyanābhaḥ kausalyaḥ pauṣpiṃjiś ca dvijottama \|*  
*udīcyāḥsāmagāḥ śiṣyāstasya pañcaśataṃ smṛtāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Hiraṇyanābha Kausalya and Pauṣpiñji, best of brahmins. The latter had fifteen disciples known as the northern singers of the Sāma Veda. 4

## \|\| 3.6.5 \|\|

*hiraṇyanābhāttāvatyaḥsaṃhitā yair dvijottamaiḥ \|*  
*gṛhītāstepi cocyante paṇḍitaiḥ prācyasāmagāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

The brilliant brahmins who received the fifteen compilations from Hiraṇyanābha are known by the wise as the eastern singers of that tradition. 5

## \|\| 3.6.6 \|\|

*lokākṣirnaidhamiścaiva kakṣivāṃllāṅgalis tathā \|*  
*pauṣpijiśiṣyāstadbhedaiḥ saṃhitā bahulīkṛtāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Lokākṣi, Kuthumi, Kuṣīdin and Lāṅgali were Pauṣpiñji’s disciples. Their followers and their followers’ disciples further raised the number of compilations. 6

## \|\| 3.6.7 \|\|

*hiraṇyanābhaśiṣyastu caturviśatisaṃhitāḥ \|*  
*provāca kṛtanāmāsau śiṣyebhyaś ca mahāmuniḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Hiraṇyanābha’s wise disciple, Kṛti by name, taught twenty-four compilations to his disciples. 7

## \|\| 3.6.8 \|\|

*taiś cāpi sāmavedo 'sau śākhābhir bahulīkṛtaḥ \|*  
*atharvaṇāmatho vakṣye saṃhitānāṃ samuccayam \|*  
*atharvavedaṃ sa muniḥsusaṃturamitadyutiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

They, too, split the Sāma Veda into many branches. I’ll now describe the compilations of the Atharva Veda. Sumantu was a brilliant sage. 8

## \|\| 3.6.9 \|\|

*śiṣyamadhyāpayāmāsa kabandhaṃ so 'pi taṃ dvidhā \|*  
*kṛtvā tudevadarsāya tathā pathyāya dattavān \|\| 9 \|\|*

He taught the Atharva Veda to his disciple Kabandha, who divided it in two and gave these to Devadarśa and Pathya. 9

## \|\| 3.6.10 \|\|

*devadarśasya śiṣyāstu medhobrahmabalis tathā \|*  
*śaulkāyaniḥ pippalādastathānyo dvijasattama \|\| 10 \|\|*

Devadarśa’s disciples included Maudga, Brahmabali, Śaulkāyani and Pippalāda, best of sages. 10

## \|\| 3.6.11 \|\|

*pathyasyāpi trayaḥ śiṣyāḥ kṛtā yair dvija saṃhitāḥ \|*  
*jābāliḥ kumudādiś ca tṛtīyaḥ śaunako dvija \|\| 11 \|\|*

Pathya had three disciples who also made compilations, brahmin: Jājali, Kumudādi, with Śaunaka as the third, brahmin. 11

## \|\| 3.6.12 \|\|

*śaunakastu dvidhā kṛtvā dadāvekāṃ tu babhrave \|*  
*dvitīyāṃ saṃhitāṃ prādātsaindhavāya ya saṃjñine \|\| 12 \|\|*

Śaunaka split his part in two and gave half to Babhru, and the second he gave to his follower named Saindhava. 12

## \|\| 3.6.13 \|\|

*saindhavānmujikeśaś ca dvedhabhinnāstridhā punaḥ \|*  
*nakṣatrakalpo vedānāṃ saṃhitānāṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 13 \|\|*

Mañjakeśa further divided the Veda he received from Saindhava into two. Sections dealing with astrology, rules governing the sacrifice, mantras, 13

## \|\| 3.6.14 \|\|

*caturthaḥsyādāṅgirasaḥ śāntikalpaścapañcamaḥ \|*  
*śreṣṭhāstvatharvaṇāmete saṃhitānāṃ vikalpakāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

With incantations as the fourth and spells for dispelling evil as the fifth—these are the major divisions of the Atharva Veda. 14

## \|\| 3.6.15 \|\|

*ākhyānaiś cāpy upākhyānair gāthābhiḥ kalpaśuddhibhiḥ \|*  
*purāṇasaṃhitāṃ cakrepurāṇārthaviśāradaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Vyāsa, skilled in the import of the purāṇas, compiled these texts from myths and legends, verses and historical accounts. 15

## \|\| 3.6.16 \|\|

*prakhyāto vyāsaśiṣyobhūtsūto vai romaharṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*purāmasaṃhitāṃ tasmai dadau vyāso mahāmatiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

The great sage then conferred this purāṇic compilation on his famous disciple, Romaharṣaṇa the bard. 16

## \|\| 3.6.17 \|\|

*sumatiścāgnivarcāś ca mitrāyuḥśāṃsapāyanaḥ \|*  
*akṛtavraṇasāvarṇiṣpaṭśiṣyāstasya cābhavan \|\| 17 \|\|*

Romaharṣaṇa had six disciples: Sumati, Agnivarcas, Mitrāyu, Śāṃśapāyana, Akṛtavraṇa and Sāvarṇi. 17

## \|\| 3.6.18 \|\|

*kāśyapaḥ saṃhitākartā sāvarṇiḥ śāṃsapāyanaḥ \|*  
*romaharṣāṇikācānyā tisṝṇāṃ mūlasaṃhitā \|\| 18 \|\|*

Akṛtavraṇa, scion of Kaśyapa, Sāvarṇi and Śāṃśapāyana were the creators of three root compilations, and Romaharṣaṇa’s was the fourth. 18

## \|\| 3.6.19 \|\|

*catuṣṭayena bhedena saṃhitānāmidaṃ mune \|\| 19 \|\|*

This, the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, is based on these four compilations, sage. 19

## \|\| 3.6.20 \|\|

*ādyaṃ sarvapurāṇānāṃ purāṇaṃ brāhmamucyate \|*  
*aṣṭādaśapurāṇāni purāṇajñāḥ pracakṣate \|\| 20 \|\|*

The first of all the purāṇas is said to be the Brahma, but eighteen are enumerated by experts in the field: 20

## \|\| 3.6.21 \|\|

*brāhmaṃ pādmaṃ vaiṣṇavaṃ ca śaivaṃ bhāgavataṃ tathā \|*  
*tathānyaṃ nāradīyaṃ ca mārkaṇḍeyaṃ ca saptamam \|\| 21 \|\|*

The Brahma, Padma, Viṣṇu, Śiva and the Bhāgavata. The Nārada is another, with the Mārkaṇḍeya as the seventh, 21

## \|\| 3.6.22 \|\|

*āgneyamaṣṭamaṃ caiva bhaviṣyannavamaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*daśamaṃ brahmavaivartaṃ laiṅgamekādaśaṃ smṛtam \|\| 22 \|\|*

The Agni is eighth, Bhaviṣya the ninth, the tenth is the Brahmavaivarta and the Liṅga is eleventh. 22

## \|\| 3.6.23 \|\|

*vārāhaṃ dvādaśaṃ caiva skāndaṃ cātra trayodaśam \|*  
*caturdaśaṃvāmanaṃ ca kaurmaṃ pañcadaśaṃ tathā \|*  
*mātsyaṃ ca gāruḍaṃ caiva brahmāṇḍaṃ ca tataḥ param \|*  
*mahāpurāṇānyetāni hyaṣṭādaśa mahāmune \|\| 23 \|\|*

The Varāha is the twelfth and the Skanda is thirteenth. The fourteenth is the Vāmana, and the Kūrma is the fifteenth. These are followed by the Matsya, Garuḍa and Brahmāṇḍa purāṇas. 23

## \|\| 3.6.24 \|\|

*tathā copa purāṇāni munibhiḥ kathitāni ca \|*  
*sargaś ca pratisargaś ca vaṃśamanvantarāṇi ca \|*  
*sarveṣveteṣu kathyante vaṃśānucārītaṃ ca yat \|\| 24 \|\|*

The first creation of the world, the subsequent re-creations, the genealogies of the patriarchs, the Manvantaras and accounts of royal dynasties are found in all these texts. 24

## \|\| 3.6.25 \|\|

*yadetattava maitreya purāṇaṃ kathyate mayā \|*  
*etadvaiṣṇavasaṃjñaṃ vai pādmasyasamanantaram \|\| 25 \|\|*

The purāṇa that I’m teaching you, Maitreya, is known as the Viṣṇu and is listed after Padma. 25

## \|\| 3.6.26 \|\|

*sarge ca pratisarge ca vaṃśamanvantarādiṣu \|*  
*kathyate bhagavān viṣṇur aśeṣeṣv iva sattama \|\| 26 \|\|*

Lord Viṣṇu is mentioned at every stage, including the creation, re-creation, genealogies, Manvantaras and the rest without exception, best of brahmins. 26

## \|\| 3.6.27 \|\|

*aṅgāni vedāścatvāro mīmāṃsānyāyavistaraḥ \|*  
*purāṇaṃ dharmaśāstraṃ ca vidyā hyetāścaturdaśa \|\| 27 \|\|*

The four Vedas, the six Vedāṅgas, Mīmāṃsā, Nyāya, the purāṇas and Dharma Śāstras make up the fourteen branches of knowledge. 27

## \|\| 3.6.28 \|\|

*āyurvedodhanurvedo gāndharvaścaiva te trayaḥ \|*  
*arthaśāstraṃ caturthaṃ tu vidya hyaṣṭādaśaiva tāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

The Āyur, Dhanur and Gāndharva Vedas, with the Artha Śāstra as the fourth, make eighteen in all. 28

## \|\| 3.6.29 \|\|

*jñeyā brahmarṣayaḥ pūrvaṃ tebhyo devarṣayaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*rājarṣayaḥ punastebhya ṛṣiprakṛtayastrayaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

You should know that sages are of three descriptions: first are brahmin sages, then come divine sages and after them are royal seers. 29

## \|\| 3.6.30 \|\|

*iti śākhāḥsamākhyātaḥ śākhābhedāstathaiva ca \|*  
*kartāraścaiva śākhānāṃ bhedahetustathoditaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Thus, I’ve described the branches of the Vedas and their subbranches, the creators of each and the reason they’re divided. 30

## \|\| 3.6.31 \|\|

*sarvamanvantareṣvevaṃ śākhābhedāḥsamāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

The same division into branches is carried out in every Manvantara. 31

## \|\| 3.6.32 \|\|

*prājāpatyā śrutirnityā tadvikalpāstvime dvija \|\| 32 \|\|*

As the primal Veda is eternal, these branches are merely differences in appearance, brahmin. 32

## \|\| 3.6.33 \|\|

*etatte kathitaṃ sarvaṃ yatpṛṣṭohamihatvayā \|*  
*maitreya vedasaṃbandhaḥ kimanyatkathayāmi te \|\| 33 \|\|*

I’ve told you all you asked about the Vedas. What else shall I tell you now, Maitreya? 33

## Chapter 7 - How to escape death: Yama’s song

## \|\| 3.7.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*yathā vatkathitaṃ sarvaṃ yatpṛṣṭosiṃ mayā guro \|*  
*śrotumicchāmyahaṃ tvekaṃ tadbhavānprabravītu me \|\| 1 \|\|*

You’ve told me precisely all I asked about, my guru, but please tell me one more thing I’d like to hear. 1

## \|\| 3.7.2 \|\|

*saptadvīpāni pātālavidhayaś ca mahāmune \|*  
*spatalokāś ca yentasthā brahmāṇḍasyāsya sarvataḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Seven continents, great sage, the underworlds and seven realms all lie within the egg of Brahmā. 2

## \|\| 3.7.3 \|\|

*sthūlaiḥ sūkṣmais tathā sūkṣmasūkṣmāsūkṣmataraiḥ smṛtā \|*  
*sthūlātsthūlataraiś caiva sarvaṃ prāṇibhir āvṛtam \|\| 3 \|\|*

This whole world is filled with living things: small, smaller and the smallest, as well as large, larger and the largest. 3

## \|\| 3.7.4 \|\|

*aṅgulasyāṣṭabhāgopi na sosti munisattama \|*  
*vasaṃti prāṇino yatra karmabandhanibandhanāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

There’s not one-eighth of an inch where living creatures, tied by bonds of karma, are not found, great sage. 4

## \|\| 3.7.5 \|\|

*sarve caite vaśaṃ yānti yamasya bhagavan kila \|*  
*āyuṣonte tathā yānti yātanāstatpracoditāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Yet all fall prey to Yama, Lord of Death, and when their lives are at an end, master, he drags them off for torture. 5

## \|\| 3.7.6 \|\|

*yātanābhyaḥ paribhraṣṭā devādyāsvatha yoniṣu \|*  
*jantavaḥ parivartante śāstrāṇāmeṣa nirṇayaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

And when they’ve been released from hell, they’re born again among the gods or some other class of being, or so the scriptures say. 6

## \|\| 3.7.7 \|\|

*sohamicchāmi tacchrotuṃ yamasya vaśavartinaḥ \|*  
*na bhavantinarā yena tatkarma kathayasva me \|\| 7 \|\|*

I’d like to hear about pure conduct by which we may escape from Yama’s grip. Pray speak on this. 7

## \|\| 3.7.8 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ayameva mune praśro nakulena mahātmanā \|*  
*pṛṣṭaḥ pitāmahaḥ prāha bhīṣmo yattacchṛṇuṣva me \|\| 8 \|\|*

Big-hearted Nakula once asked the same of his grandsire, so now you’ll hear what Bhīṣma said to him. 8

## \|\| 3.7.9 \|\|

*bhīṣṇa uvāca*  
*purā mamāgato vatsa sakhā kāliṅgako dvijaḥ \|*  
*sa māmuvāca praṣṭo vai mayā jātismaro muniḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Bhīṣma:

Long ago, a friend of mine, a brahmin from Kaliṅga, had come to visit me, dear boy. He said that he’d once asked this very question of a sage who knew his former lives. 9

## \|\| 3.7.10 \|\|

*tenākhyātamidaṃ sarvamitthaṃ caitadbhaviṣyati \|*  
*tathā ca tadabhūdvatsa yathoktaṃ tena dhīmatā \|\| 10 \|\|*

That sage described the current state, and how things would be in future, and everything took place exactly as he foretold. 10

## \|\| 3.7.11 \|\|

*sa pṛṣṭaś ca mayā bhūyaḥ śraddadhānena vai dvijaḥ \|*  
*yadyadāha na taddṛṣṭamanyathā hi mayā kvacit \|\| 11 \|\|*

Filled with faith, I asked the brahmin more about it, and I’ve seen nothing anywhere that contradicts his words. 11

## \|\| 3.7.12 \|\|

*ekadā tu mayā pṛṣṭametadyadbhavatoditam \|*  
*prāha kāliṅgako vipraḥsmṛtvā tasya munervacaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

This is what he told me when I asked him long ago, recalling what that sage had said to him. 12

## \|\| 3.7.13 \|\|

*jātismareṇa kathito rahasyaḥ paramo mama \|*  
*yamakiṅkarayoryobhūtsaṃvādastaṃ bravīmi te \|\| 13 \|\|*

This man who recalled his former lives revealed to him a secret most profound. It was this dialogue between Lord Yama and his minion that I’ll now repeat for you. 13

## \|\| 3.7.14 \|\|

*kāliṅga uvāca*  
*svapuruṣamabhivīkṣya pāśahastaṃ vadati yamaḥ kila tasya karṇamūle \|*  
*parihara madhusūdanaprapannānprabhurahamanyanṛṇāmavaiṣṇavānām \|\| 14 \|\|*

The Kaliṅgan:

Seeing his minion with noose in hand, Yama whispered in his ear, ‘Leave the devotees of Madhusūdana alone, for I’m the lord of all, except the Vaiṣṇavas. 14

## \|\| 3.7.15 \|\|

*ahamamaravarārcitena dhātrā yama iti lokahitāhite niyuktaḥ \|*  
*hariguruvaśagosmi na svatantraḥ prabhavati saṃyamane mamāpi viṣṇuḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

I was told by Brahmā, the disposer revered by hosts of gods, to govern (yam) every being in this world according to their deeds, both good and bad. But I myself am subject to my guru, Hari, and cannot act alone. When it comes to governance, Viṣṇu is my superior. 15

## \|\| 3.7.16 \|\|

*kaṇṭa kamukuṭakarṇikādibhedaiḥ kanakamabhedamapīṣyate yathaikam \|*  
*surapaśumanujādikalpanābhir harir akhilābhir udīryate tathaikaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Just as gold is all one kind, but may be crafted into bracelets, bands and earrings, in that same way, Hari is present in all the deities, animals, mortals and the rest. 16

## \|\| 3.7.17 \|\|

*kṣititalaparamāṇavonilānte punarupayānti yathaikatāṃ dharitryāḥ \|*  
*surapaśumanujādayastathānte guṇakalaṣeṇa sanātanena tena \|\| 17 \|\|*

Just as drops of water, drawn from earth by wind, return to earth when the wind subsides, in that same way, deities, animals, mortals and the rest, separated on account of their impure qualities, are in the end united with that eternal being. 17

## \|\| 3.7.18 \|\|

*harimamaravarārcitāṅghripadmaṃpraṇamati yaḥ paramārthato hi martyaḥ \|*  
*tamapagatasamastapāpabandhaṃ vraja parihṛtya yathāgnimājyasiktam \|\| 18 \|\|*

A mortal who understands this highest truth and bows to Hari, whose lotus-like feet are worshipped by the hosts of gods, is freed from all the bonds of sin. Avoid such persons, as you would a fire that’s fed with oil, and continue on your way.’ 18

## \|\| 3.7.19 \|\|

*iti yamavacanaṃ niśamya pāśī yama puruṣastamuvāca dharmarājam \|*  
*kathaya mama vibho samastadhāturbhavati hareḥ khalu yādṛśosya bhaktaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

When Yama’s servant, noose in hand, heard his master’s words, he asked the king of justice, ‘Tell me, master, how shall I know a devotee of Hari, the arranger of the universe?’ 19

## \|\| 3.7.20 \|\|

*yama uvāca*  
*na calati nijavarmadharmato yaḥsamamatirātmasuhṛdvipakṣapakṣe \|*  
*na harati na ca hanti kiñciducchaiḥ sthitamanasaṃ tamavehi viṣṇubhaktam \|\| 20 \|\|*

Yama:

One who never strays from traditions of his community, who is equally disposed to friend and foe, who takes nothing, harms no-one and is pure of mind—know him to be a devotee of Viṣṇu. 20

## \|\| 3.7.21 \|\|

*kalikalupamalena yasya nā'tmā vimalamatemalinīkṛtastamenam \|*  
*manasi kṛtajanārdanaṃ manaṣyaṃ satatamavehi hareratīva bhaktam \|\| 21 \|\|*

One whose self remains unstained by filth and folly in the age of Kali, whose thoughts are pure, always holding Janārdana in his heart when delusion ends—know him to be a faithful devotee of Hari. 21

## \|\| 3.7.22 \|\|

*kanakamapi rahasyavekṣya buddhyā tṛṇamiva yaḥsamavaiti vai parasvam \|*  
*bhavati ca bhagavatyananyacetāḥ puruṣavaraṃ tamavehi viṣṇubhaktam \|\| 22 \|\|*

One who finds another’s hidden gold yet thinks it no better than a straw, and one whose heart is always set on the lord alone—know that fine being to be a devotee of Viṣṇu. 22

## \|\| 3.7.23 \|\|

*sphaṭikagiriśilāmalaḥ kva viṣṇurmanasi nṛṇāṃ kva ca matsarādidoṣaḥ \|*  
*na hi tuhinamayūkharaśmipuñje bhavati hutāśanadīptijaḥ pratāpaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

Where, on the one hand, is Viṣṇu, a mountain of purest crystal, and on the other, the selfishness and sundry faults that fill the hearts of men? The heat produced by blazing fires will not be found in webs of cooling moonbeams. 23

## \|\| 3.7.24 \|\|

*vimalamatiramatsaraḥ praśāntaḥ śucicaritokhilasattvamitrabhūtaḥ \|*  
*priyahitavacano 'stamānamāyo vasati sadā hṛdi tasya vāsudevaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Vāsudeva always occupies the heart of one whose mind is pure, selfless, tranquil, of unblemished conduct, a friend to all, whose words are kind and beneficial and who resists the lure of fame. 24

## \|\| 3.7.25 \|\|

*vasati hṛdisanātane ca tasminbhavati pumāñjagatosya saumyarūpaḥ \|*  
*kṣitirasamatiramyamātmanontaḥ kathayati cārutayaiva sālapotaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

When eternal Viṣṇu dwells within a person’s heart, he seems to all the world to be resplendent, as a śāla sapling’s beauty betrays the splendid essence of the earth beneath it. 25

## \|\| 3.7.26 \|\|

*yamaniyamavidhūta kalpaṣāṇāmanudinamacyutasaktamānasānām \|*  
*apagatamadamānamatsarāṇāṃ tyaja bhaṭa dūratareṇa mānavānām \|\| 26 \|\|*

Keep well away, my servant, from those whose sins have been assuaged by obligatory and voluntary acts of penance, whose hearts are daily fixed on Viṣṇu and who are free from folly, selfishness and pride. 26

## \|\| 3.7.27 \|\|

*hṛdi yadi bhagavānanādirāste harirariśaṅkhagadādharovyātmā \|*  
*tadaghavighātakartṛbhinnaṃ bhavati kathaṃ sati cāndhakāramarke \|\| 27 \|\|*

If Lord Hari, beginningless and eternal, bearer of the sword, the conch and mace, abides in a person’s heart, his sins are destroyed by Viṣṇu, conqueror of all misdeeds, for how can darkness reign when the sun is shining? 27

## \|\| 3.7.28 \|\|

*harati paradhanaṃ nihanti jantūnvadati tathā nṛtaniṣṭurāṇi yaś ca \|*  
*aśubhajanitaduramadasya puṃsaḥ kaluṣamaterhṛdi tasya nāstyanantaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

The eternal lord will not be found in the heart of one who steals another’s wealth, who harms a living thing, whose speech is false or harsh, who’s proud of shameful deeds or harbours impure thoughts. 28

## \|\| 3.7.29 \|\|

*na sahati parasaṃpadaṃ vinindāṃ kaluṣamatiḥ kurute satāmasādhuḥ \|*  
*nayajati na dadāti yaś ca saṃtaṃ manasi na tasya janārdano 'dhamasya \|\| 29 \|\|*

Janārdana dwells not in the heart of the rogue who eyes another’s wealth, the wicked man who vilifies the good, he who neglects the sacrifice or gives nothing to the worthy. 29

## \|\| 3.7.30 \|\|

*paramasuhṛdibāndhave kalatre mutatanayāpitṛmātṛbhṛtyavarge \|*  
*śaṭhamatirupayāti yorthatṛṣṇāṃ tamadhamaceṣṭamavehi nāsya bhaktam \|\| 30 \|\|*

A deceitful man who begs a loan from his bosom friend, kinsman, spouse, son, daughter, father, mother or servant—recognise that such a lowly wretch is not a devotee of Viṣṇu. 30

## \|\| 3.7.31 \|\|

*aśubhamatirasatpravṛtti saktaḥsatatamanāryakuśīlasaṃgamattaḥ \|*  
*anudinakṛtapāpabandhayuktaḥ paruṣapaśurna hi vāsudevabhaktaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Of impure thought, attached to vice, ever smitten by the company of the vulgar and influential and who daily exerts himself in wicked ways—this bestial being isn’t Vāsudeva’s devotee. 31

## \|\| 3.7.32 \|\|

*sakalamidamahaṃ ca vāsudevaḥ paramapumānparameśvaraḥsa ekaḥ \|*  
*iti matiracalābhavatyanante hṛdayagate vrajatānvihāya dūrāt \|\| 32 \|\|*

‘This whole world, including me, is Vāsudeva, the ultimate being, the highest lord, the One’—when such pure thoughts as these reach eternal Viṣṇu in their hearts, let those people be and pass them by. 32

## \|\| 3.7.33 \|\|

*kamalanayana vāsudeva viṣṇo dharaṇidharācyuta śaṅkhacakrapāṇe \|*  
*bhavaśaraṇamitirayanti ye vai tyaja bhaṭa dūratareṇa tānapāpān \|\| 33 \|\|*

Keep well away, my servant, from sinless men who say, ‘Lotus-eyed Vāsudeva, Viṣṇu, supporter of the world, eternal bearer of the conch and discus, be my refuge!’ 33

## \|\| 3.7.34 \|\|

*vasati manasi yasya sovyayātmā puruṣavarasya na tasya dṛṣṭipāte \|*  
*tava gatiratha vā mamāsti cakrapratihatavīryabalasya sonyalokyaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Don’t go near that worthy man in whose heart the everlasting being abides, for he is destined for a realm that’s different from mine, as my power is checked by Viṣṇu’s discus. 34

## \|\| 3.7.35 \|\|

*kāliṅga uvāca*  
*iti nijabhaṭaśāsanāya devo ravita nayasya kilāha dharmarājaḥ \|*  
*mama kathitamidaṃ ca tena tubhyaṃ kuruvara samyagidaṃ mayāpi coktam \|\| 35 \|\|*

The Kaliṅgan:

Such was the command that the god of death, the Sun’s own son and lord of justice, made to his emissary. This is what the sage told me, and I’ve told you, best of Kurus, exactly what he said. 35

## \|\| 3.7.36 \|\|

*śrībhīṣma uvāca*  
*nakulaitanmamākhyātaṃ pūrvaṃ tena dvijanmanā \|*  
*kaliṅgadeśādeśyetya prītena sumahātmanā \|\| 36 \|\|*

Bhīṣma:

Thus spoke my friend in former times, Nakula, the kind-hearted brahmin from Kaliṅga. 36

## \|\| 3.7.37 \|\|

*mayāpyetadyathānyāyaṃ samyagvatsa tavoditam \|*  
*yathā viṣṇumṛtenānyattrāṇāṃ saṃsārasāgare \|\| 37 \|\|*

I’ve relayed the whole account to you, dear boy, as is fitting, since Viṣṇu is the only means of rescue from the ocean of saṃsāra. 37

## \|\| 3.7.38 \|\|

*kiṅkarāḥ pāśadaṇḍāś ca na yamo na ca yātanāḥ \|*  
*samarthāstasya yasyātmā keśavālaṃbanaḥsadā \|\| 38 \|\|*

The minions with their staves and ropes, Yama himself and the torments he inflicts, hold no fear for those whose inner self is always fixed on Keśava. 38

## \|\| 3.7.39 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*etanmune samākhyātaṃ gītaṃ vaivasvatena yat \|*  
*tvatpraśnānugataṃ samyakkimanyacchrotumicchasi \|\| 39 \|\|*

Parāśara:

In answer to your question, sage, I’ve retold in full this song of Yama, the Sun’s own son. What further would you like to hear? 39

## Chapter 8 - The four communities

## \|\| 3.8.1 \|\|

*maitreya uvāca*  
*bhagavanbhagavāndevaḥ saṃsāravijigīṣubhiḥ \|*  
*samākhyāhi jagannātho viṣṇurārādhyate yathā \|\| 1 \|\|*

Tell me, master, how those who wish to flee saṃsāra worship the divine lord, the ruler of the world. 1

## \|\| 3.8.2 \|\|

*ārādhitācca govindādārādhanaparair naraiḥ \|*  
*yatprāpyate phalaṃ śrotuṃ taccecchāmi mahāmune \|\| 2 \|\|*

I’d also like to hear about the fruits of praising Govinda, sage, won by those intent upon such practices. 2

## \|\| 3.8.3 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*yatpṛcchati bhavānetatsagareṇa mahātmanā \|*  
*aurvaḥ prāha yathā pṛṣṭastanme nigadataḥ śṛṇu \|\| 3 \|\|*

The question you’ve just asked was also put to Aurva by Sagara, that great sage. Listen and I’ll tell you what he said. 3

## \|\| 3.8.4 \|\|

*sagaraḥ praṇipattyainamaurvaṃ papracchabhārgavam \|*  
*viṣṇorārādhanopāyasaṃbandhaṃ munisattama \|\| 4 \|\|*

Bowing deeply, Sagara questioned Aurva, the scion of Bhṛgu, about the perfect way to worship Viṣṇu, best of sages, 4

## \|\| 3.8.5 \|\|

*phalaṃ cārādhite viṣṇo yatpuṃsāmabhijāyate \|*  
*sa cāha pṛṣṭo yatnena tasmai tanmekhilaṃ śṛṇu \|\| 5 \|\|*

And the benefits that people gain by doing so. Now listen to his reply, Maitreya. 5

## \|\| 3.8.6 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*bhaumaṃ manorathaṃ svargaṃ svarge ramyaṃ ca yatpadam \|*  
*prāpnotyārādhite viṣṇau nirvāṇamapi cottamam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Aurva:

When Viṣṇu is praised, one gains one’s wishes in this world, and then in heaven as sought by blessed ones, and finally supreme nirvāṇa. 6

## \|\| 3.8.7 \|\|

*yadyadicchati yāvacca phalamārādhite 'cyute \|*  
*tattadāpnoti rājendra bhūri svalpamathāpi vā \|\| 7 \|\|*

One receives whatever reward of any size, great or small, that one desires, your majesty, when one worships everlasting Viṣṇu. 7

## \|\| 3.8.8 \|\|

*yattu pṛcchasi bhūpāla kathāmārādhyate hariḥ \|*  
*tadahaṃ sakalaṃ tubhyaṃ kathayāmi nibodha me \|\| 8 \|\|*

Since you ask, ruler of the earth, I’ll tell you how he’s worshipped and the rewards that result therefrom, so listen: 8

## \|\| 3.8.9 \|\|

*varṇāśramācāravatā puruṣeṇaparaḥ pumān \|*  
*viṣṇurārādhyate panthā nānyastattoṣakārakaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, the highest being, is worshipped by a person who practises the traditions of his community; there’s no other path that pleases him. 9

## \|\| 3.8.10 \|\|

*yajanyajñānyajatyenaṃ japantyenaṃ japannṛpa \|*  
*nighnannanyānhinastyenaṃ sarvabhūto yato hariḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

The sacrificer worships him, as does one who offers prayers, sire. But harming others injures Hari, too, because he’s everything. 10

## \|\| 3.8.11 \|\|

*tasmātsadācāravatā puruṣeṇa janārdanaḥ \|*  
*ārādhyastu svavarṇoktadharmānuṣṭhānakārimā \|\| 11 \|\|*

Janārdana is therefore worshipped by any person of good conduct who follows the traditions and the practices set down for his community. 11

## \|\| 3.8.12 \|\|

*brāhmaṇaḥ kṣatriyo vaiśyaḥ śudraś ca pṛthivīpate \|*  
*svadharmatatparo viṣṇumārādhayati nānyathā \|\| 12 \|\|*

A brahmin, kṣatriya, vaiśya or śūdra, sire, who’s intent upon his duties worships Viṣṇu; there is no other way. 12

## \|\| 3.8.13 \|\|

*parāpavādaṃ paiśunyamanṛtaṃ ca na bhāṣate \|*  
*anyodvegakaraṃ vāpi toṣyate tenakeśavaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

Keśava is pleased by one who doesn’t curse or lie or slander, which might upset another being. 13

## \|\| 3.8.14 \|\|

*paradāraparadravyaparahiṃsāsu yo ratim \|*  
*na karoti pumānbhūpa toṣyate tena keśavaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

He is pleased by one who doesn’t covet another’s wife or goods, and never thinks of doing harm, your majesty. 14

## \|\| 3.8.15 \|\|

*na tāḍayati no hanti prāṇino na ca hiṃsakaḥ \|*  
*yo manuṣyo manuṣyendra toṣyate tena keśavaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

Keśava’s pleased by one who doesn’t strike or kill a living thing, sire. 15

## \|\| 3.8.16 \|\|

*devādvijagurūṇāṃ ca śuśrūṣāsu sadodyataḥ \|*  
*toṣyate tena govindaḥ puruṣeṇanareśvara \|\| 16 \|\|*

Govinda’s pleased by one who’s always keen to serve the gods, the brahmins and his guru, sire. 16

## \|\| 3.8.17 \|\|

*yathātmani ca putre ca sarvabhūteṣu yattathā \|*  
*hitakāmo haristena sarvadā toṣyate sukham \|\| 17 \|\|*

Hari’s ever gratified by one who wants the welfare of all beings, as for his son or for himself. 17

## \|\| 3.8.18 \|\|

*yasya rāgādidoṣeṇa na duṣṭaṃ nṛpa mānasam \|*  
*viśuddhacetasā viṣṇustoṣyate tena sarvadā \|\| 18 \|\|*

Viṣṇu’s always pleased by one whose mind is free from faults like anger, and whose thoughts are without stain, your majesty. 18

## \|\| 3.8.19 \|\|

*varṇāśrameṣu ye dharmāḥ śāstroktā munisattama \|*  
*teṣu tiṣṭhannaro viṣṇumāduṣṭaṃ nṛpa mānasam \|\| 19 \|\|*

One who undertakes the duties of his community and stage of life, as ordained by scripture, sire, worships Viṣṇu; there is no other way. 19

## \|\| 3.8.20 \|\|

*sagara uvāca*  
*tadahaṃ śrotumicchami varṇadharmānaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*tathaivāśramadharmaṃś ca dvijavarya bravīhi tān \|\| 20 \|\|*

Sagara:

As this is so, I wish to hear about the duties of each community and stage of life. So, tell me all, you brahmin hero. 20

## \|\| 3.8.21 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*brāhmaṇakṣatriyaviśāṃ śudrāṇāṃ ca yathākamam \|*  
*tvamekāgramatirbhūtvā śṛṇu dharmānmayoditān \|\| 21 \|\|*

Aurva:

Listen well while I explain the duties of the brahmins, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and śūdras in that order. 21

## \|\| 3.8.22 \|\|

*dānaṃ dadyād yajed devān yajñiḥ svādhyāyatatparaḥ \|*  
*nityodakī bhavedvipraḥ kuryāccāgniparigraham \|\| 22 \|\|*

A brahmin, intent upon the study of the Vedas, should practise generosity and worship gods with sacrifices. He should always be supplied with water and maintain the sacred fires. 22

## \|\| 3.8.23 \|\|

*vṛttyarthaṃ yājayeccānyānanyānadhyāpayettathā \|*  
*kuryātpratigrahādānaṃśuklārthānnyāyato dvijaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

To earn a living, he may perform a sacrifice for others or do some teaching. He may accept donations as befits a brahmin because he is respected. 23

## \|\| 3.8.24 \|\|

*sarvabhūtahitaṃ kuryānnāhitaṃ kasyāciddvijaḥ \|*  
*maitrī samastabhūteṣu brāhmaṇasyottamaṃ dhanam \|\| 24 \|\|*

He should act to benefit all beings and disadvantage none, because his greatest treasure is benevolence to everything. 24

## \|\| 3.8.25 \|\|

*grāvṇiratne ca pārakye samabuddhirbhaveddvijaḥ \|*  
*ṛtāvabhigamaḥ patnyāṃ śastate cāsya pārthiva \|\| 25 \|\|*

A brahmin should be equally disposed towards a rock and to a jewel belonging to another man. It’s also been prescribed that he approach his wife at the proper season, sire. 25

## \|\| 3.8.26 \|\|

*dānāni dadyādicchāto dvijebhyaḥ kṣatriyopi vā \|*  
*yajecca vividhair yajñair adhīyīta ca pārthivaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

A kṣatriya should willingly make donations to the brahmins, and such a king should offer various sacrifices and undertake the study of the Vedas. 26

## \|\| 3.8.27 \|\|

*śastrājīvo mahīrakṣā pravarā tasya jīvikā \|*  
*tatrāpi prathamaḥ kalpaḥ pṛthivīparipāranam \|\| 27 \|\|*

Military life and defence of the realm are his principal occupations, but his primary duty is the protection of the world. 27

## \|\| 3.8.28 \|\|

*dharitrīpālanenaiva kṛtakṛtyā narādhipāḥ \|*  
*bhavanti tṛpateraṃśā yato yajñādikarmaṇām \|\| 28 \|\|*

A king fulfils his duty by defending his dominion and, on that account, a portion of the merit of the sacrifice and other rituals accrue to him. 28

## \|\| 3.8.29 \|\|

*duṣṭānāṃ śāsanādrājā śiṣṭānāṃ paripālanāt \|*  
*prāpnotyabhimatāṃllokānvarṇasaṃsthāṃ karoti yaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

By punishing the wicked and protecting virtuous people, a king who keeps each order in its place attains the place in heaven that he desires. 29

## \|\| 3.8.30 \|\|

*pāśupālyaṃ ca vāṇijyaṃ kṛṣiṃ ca manujeśvara \|*  
*vaiśyāya jīvikāṃ brahmā dadau lokapitāmahaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

Brahmā, universal grandsire, gave the occupations of animal husbandry, trade and agriculture to the vaiśya, sire. 30

## \|\| 3.8.31 \|\|

*tasyāpyadhyayanaṃ yajño dānaṃ dharmaś ca śasyate \|*  
*nityanaimittikādīnāmanuṣṭhānaṃ ca karmaṇām \|\| 31 \|\|*

Study, sacrifice and generosity are also duties ordained for him, as well as the performance of regular and occasional rites. 31

## \|\| 3.8.32 \|\|

*dvijātisaṃśritaṃ karma tādarthyaṃ tena poṣaṇam \|*  
*krayavikrayajair vāpi dhanaiḥ kārūdbhavena vā \|\| 32 \|\|*

The śūdra’s intended duty is to serve the twice-born orders, and sustain himself thereby, or with income raised from trading or practising a craft. 32

## \|\| 3.8.33 \|\|

*śudrasya sannatiḥ śaucaṃ sevā svāminyamāyayā \|*  
*amantrayajño hyasteyaṃ tatsaṃgo viprarakṣaṇam \|\| 33 \|\|*
