The śūdra makes donations and offerings of cooked food, and performs each ritual for his forebears, or hires another to perform them for him. 33

## \|\| 3.8.34 \|\|

*dānaṃ ca dadyācchūdropi pākayajñair yajeta ca \|*  
*pitryādikaṃ ca tatsarvaṃ śudraḥ kurvīta tena vai \|\| 34 \|\|*

Acquisition of wealth for the support of their dependants, approaching wives at the proper season, sire, 34

## \|\| 3.8.35 \|\|

*bhṛtyādibharaṇārthāya sarveṣāṃ ca parigrahaḥ \|*  
*ṛtukālebhigamanaṃ svadāreṣu mahīpate \|\| 35 \|\|*

Compassion towards all creatures, patience, humility, truth, purity, resilience, cheerfulness, eloquence, 35

## \|\| 3.8.36 \|\|

*dayā samastabhūteṣu titikṣā nātimānitā \|*  
*satyaṃ śaucamanāyāso maṅgalaṃ priyavāditā \|\| 36 \|\|*

Friendliness, generosity and kindness are said to be the virtues for every order, sire. 36

## \|\| 3.8.37 \|\|

*maitryaspṛhā tathā tadvadakārpaṇyaṃnareśvara \|*  
*anusūyā ca sāmānyavarṇānāṃ kathitā guṇāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

These are also features common to every stage of life. Now hear about the virtues and the practices to be followed by brahmins and the others in times of strife. 37

## \|\| 3.8.38 \|\|

*āśramāṇāṃ ca sarveṣāmete sāmānyalakṣaṇāḥ \|*  
*guṇāṃstathāvaddharmāṃś ca viprādīnāmimāñchṛṇu \|\| 38 \|\|*

It’s said a brahmin may adopt the practices of a kṣatriya or a vaiśya in a crisis, and a vaiśya may adopt a kṣatriya’s ways, but neither a kṣatriya nor a vaiśya may adopt the practices of a śūdra if it’s possible to avoid them, sire. 38

## \|\| 3.8.39 \|\|

*kṣāttraṃ karma dvijasyoktaṃ vaiśyaṃ karma tathā padi \|*  
*rājanyasya ca vaiśyoktaṃ śūdrakarma na caitayoḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

If they must adopt another’s ways in times of crisis, then they shouldn’t mix the practices of different orders. 39

## \|\| 3.8.40 \|\|

*sāmarthye sati tattyājyamubhābhyāmapi pārthiva \|*  
*tadevāpadi kartavyaṃ na kuryātkarmasaṃkaram \|\| 40 \|\|*

I’ve told you all about the duties of each community. Now listen while I describe the duties for each stage of life. 40

## Chapter 9 - The four stages of life

## \|\| 3.9.1 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*bālaḥ kṛtopanayano vedāharaṇatatparaḥ \|*  
*gurugehe vasedbhūpa brahmacārī samāhitaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Once he’s been invested with the sacred thread, sire, a boy should live in his guru’s home as a devout brahmacārin, a celibate student, intent on mastering the Vedas. 1

## \|\| 3.9.2 \|\|

*śaucā cāravrataṃ tatra kāryaṃ śuśrūṣaṇaṃ guroḥ \|*  
*vratāni caratā grāhyo vedaś ca kṛtabuddhinā \|\| 2 \|\|*

Pure in conduct, he should serve his master, observe the vows and study with all his heart. 2

## \|\| 3.9.3 \|\|

*ubhe saṃdhye raviṃ bhūpa tathaivāgniṃ samāhitaḥ \|*  
*upatiṣṭhettadā kuryādgurorapyabhivādanam \|\| 3 \|\|*

At dawn and dusk, he should devoutly venerate the sun, attend the sacred fire, your majesty, and pay homage to his guru. 3

## \|\| 3.9.4 \|\|

*sthite tiṣṭhedvājedyāte nīcair āsīta cāsati \|*  
*śiṣyo gurornṛpaśreṣṭha pratikūlaṃ na saṃcaret \|\| 4 \|\|*

He should stand when his guru stands, walk when he walks and sit below his guru when he sits. The disciple should do nothing to upset his master, best of kings. 4

## \|\| 3.9.5 \|\|

*tenai voktaṃ paṭhedvedaṃ nānyacittaḥ puraḥsthitaḥ \|*  
*anujñātaś ca bhikṣānnamaśnīyādguruṇā tataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

When directed, he should chant the Veda before his teacher, with his mind on nothing else. With permission, he may eat the food received as alms. 5

## \|\| 3.9.6 \|\|

*avagāhedapaḥ pūrvamācāryeṇāvagāhitaḥ \|*  
*samijjalādikaṃ cāsya kālyaṃ kālyamupānayet \|\| 6 \|\|*

He should bathe in water that his teacher used for washing, then bring him firewood, water and other requisites each morning. 6

## \|\| 3.9.7 \|\|

*gṛhītagrāhyavedaś ca tato 'nujñām avāpya ca \|*  
*gārhasthyamāviśet prājño niṣpannaguruṇiṣkṛtiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

When he’s finally mastered the appropriate Veda and received his guru’s blessing, having recompensed him fully, that learned man may then become a householder. 7

## \|\| 3.9.8 \|\|

*vidhināvāptadārastu dhanaṃ prāpya svakarmaṇā \|*  
*gṛhasthakāryamakhilaṃ kuryādbhūpāla śaktitaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

He should take a wife with proper rites and, having earned some money through his efforts, he should diligently fulfil the duties of that station, sire. 8

## \|\| 3.9.9 \|\|

*nivāpena pitṝnarcan yajñair devāṃstathātithīn \|*  
*annair munīṃś ca svādhyāyair apatyena prajāpatim \|\| 9 \|\|*

He should honour his ancestral spirits with oblations, the deities with sacrifices, guests with food, sages with recitation, the progenitor Brahmā with offspring, 9

## \|\| 3.9.10 \|\|

*bhūtāni balibhiścaiva vātsalyenākhilaṃ jagat \|*  
*prāpnoti lokānpuruṣo nijakarmasamārjitān \|\| 10 \|\|*

All living creatures with offerings and all the world with kindness. Such a person reaches those heavenly realms that are won through one’s own actions. 10

## \|\| 3.9.11 \|\|

*bikṣābhujaś ca ye kecitparivraḍbrahmacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*tepyatraiva pratiṣṭhante gārhasthyaṃ tena vai param \|\| 11 \|\|*

Some wandering brahmacārins live on alms, after which they establish their own households. 11

## \|\| 3.9.12 \|\|

*vedāharaṇakāryāya tīrthasnānāya ca prabho \|*  
*aṭanti vasudhāṃ viprāḥ pṛthivīdarśanāya ca \|\| 12 \|\|*

These brahmins journey widely in accord with Vedic teaching, to bathe at sacred fords, master, and to see the world. 12

## \|\| 3.9.13 \|\|

*aniketā hyanāhārā yatra sāyaṅgṛhāś ca ye \|*  
*teṣāṃ gṛhasthaḥ sarveṣāṃ pratiṣṭhā yonireva ca \|\| 13 \|\|*

For those who roam with neither home nor food, finding shelter where they can at dusk, the householder offers sustenance and refuge. 13

## \|\| 3.9.14 \|\|

*teṣāṃ svāgatadānādivaktavyaṃ madhuraṃ nṛpa \|*  
*gṛhāgatānāṃ dadyācca śayanāsanabhojanam \|\| 14 \|\|*

To his home he welcomes guests with kindly words, sire, offering a bed, a seat and food to eat. 14

## \|\| 3.9.15 \|\|

*atithiryasya bhagnāśo gṛhātpratinivartate \|*  
*sa dattvā duṣkṛtaṃ tasmai puṇyamādāya gacchati \|\| 15 \|\|*

When a traveller turns with disappointment from a person’s door, he leaves his misdeeds with the householder and takes that person’s good luck with him. 15

## \|\| 3.9.16 \|\|

*avajñānamahaṅkāro daṃbhaścaiva gṛha sataḥ \|*  
*paritāpopaghātau ca pāruṣyaṃ ca na śasyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

In the homes of virtuous people, one meets neither disrespect, pride, deceit, regret, harm nor insult. 16

## \|\| 3.9.17 \|\|

*yastu samyakkarotyevaṃ gṛhasthaḥ paramaṃ vidhim \|*  
*sarvabandhavinirmukto lokānāpnotyanuttamān \|\| 17 \|\|*

A householder who follows these noble precepts to the letter will be released from all his bonds and ascend to the highest realms. 17

## \|\| 3.9.18 \|\|

*vayaḥparīṇato rājankṛtakṛtyo gṛhāśramī \|*  
*putreṣu bhāryāṃ nikṣipya vanaṃ gacchetsahaiva vā \|\| 18 \|\|*

In ripe old age, your majesty, a householder who has done his duty should venture to the forest, leaving his wife in the care of his sons, or he may take her with him. 18

## \|\| 3.9.19 \|\|

*parṇamūlaphalāhāraḥ keśaśmaśrujaṭādharaḥ \|*  
*bhūmiśāyī bhavettatra muniḥsarvātithirnṛpa \|\| 19 \|\|*

There, that sage should live on leaves and roots and berries, his hair and beard and matted locks untended. He’ll sleep directly on the ground, sire, welcoming all comers as his guests. 19

## \|\| 3.9.20 \|\|

*carmakāśakuśaiḥ kuryātparidhānottarīyake \|*  
*tadvattriṣavaṇaṃ snānaṃ śastamasya nareśvaraḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

He’ll fashion upper and lower garments from skins, or kāsa and kuśa-grass, your majesty. It’s ordained that he should bathe three times a day. 20

## \|\| 3.9.21 \|\|

*devatābhyarcanaṃ homaḥsarvābhyāgatapūjanam \|*  
*bhikṣābalipradānaṃ ca śastamasya nareśvara \|\| 21 \|\|*

It’s prescribed that he should worship deities with fire sacrifices, sire, honour all who come as guests and offer food to mendicants. 21

## \|\| 3.9.22 \|\|

*vanyastrehena gātrāṇāmabhyaṅgaścāsya śasyate \|*  
*tapaś ca tasya rājendra śītoṣṇādisahiṣṇutā \|\| 22 \|\|*

It’s ordained that he should rub his limbs with forest oils and, during his austerities, your majesty, he must endure the heat and cold and other trials. 22

## \|\| 3.9.23 \|\|

*yastvetāṃ niyataścaryāṃ vānaprasthaścarenmuniḥ \|*  
*sa dahatyagnivaddoṣāñjayellokāṃś ca śāśvatān \|\| 23 \|\|*

The forest-dwelling sage who takes this course with self-restraint burns away his misdeeds like a fire, and wins the everlasting realms. 23

## \|\| 3.9.24 \|\|

*caturthaścāśramo bhikṣoḥ projyate yo manīṣibhiḥ \|*  
*tasya svarūpaṃ gadato mama śrotuṃ nṛpārhasi \|\| 24 \|\|*

The fourth stage, that of mendicant, is mentioned by the wise, your majesty. Listen while I describe it. 24

## \|\| 3.9.25 \|\|

*putradravyakalatreṣu tyaktastreho narādhipa \|*  
*caturthamāśramasthānaṃ gacchennirdhūtamatsaraḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

One who sheds affection for his sons, possessions and his wife, sire, should begin the final stage of life, freed from all his selfishness. 25

## \|\| 3.9.26 \|\|

*traivargikāṃstyajetsarvānāraṃbhānavanīpate \|*  
*mitrādiṣu samo maitraḥsamasteṣveva jantuṣu \|\| 26 \|\|*

He should forsake the threefold goals—love, wealth and virtue—and other undertakings, your majesty, and be just as well disposed to friends and foe and other living things. 26

## \|\| 3.9.27 \|\|

*jarāyujāṇḍajādīnāṃ vāḍmanaḥkāyakarmabhiḥ \|*  
*yuktaḥ kurvīta na drohaṃ sarvasaṃgāṃścavarjayet \|\| 27 \|\|*

Restrained in voice and thought and deed, he’ll not harm a womb-born being, or one that’s hatched or any other, while renouncing all attachments. 27

## \|\| 3.9.28 \|\|

*ekārātrasthitir grāme pañcarātrasthitiḥ pure \|*  
*tathā tiṣṭhedyathāprītirdveṣo vā nāsya jāyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

He’ll stay in a village for just one night, or in a city for five, lest he start to like or loathe them. 28

## \|\| 3.9.29 \|\|

*prāṇayātrānimittaṃ ca vyaṅgāre bhuktavajjane \|*  
*kāle praśastavarṇānāṃ bhikṣārthaṃ paryaṭedgṛhān \|\| 29 \|\|*

To support himself, he’ll visit the homes of the first three orders to ask for food when the fires are out and the people fed. 29

## \|\| 3.9.30 \|\|

*kāmaḥ krodhas tathā darpamohalobhādayaś ca ye \|*  
*tāṃstu sarvānparityajyaparivrāṇnirmamo bhavet \|\| 30 \|\|*

Having cast off desire, anger, pride, delusion, greed and every other fault, he’ll become a selfless mendicant. 30

## \|\| 3.9.31 \|\|

*abhayaṃ sarvabhūtebhyo dattvā yaścarate muniḥ \|*  
*tasyāpi sarvabhūtebhyo na bhayaṃ vidyate kvacit \|\| 31 \|\|*

A sage who roams without a threat to any creature is not threatened by them in turn. 31

## \|\| 3.9.32 \|\|

*kṛtvāgnihotraṃsvaśarīrasaṃsthaṃ śārīramagniṃ svamukhe juhoti \|*  
*viprastu bhaikṣyopahitair havirbhiścitāgnikānāṃ vrahati sma lokān \|\| 32 \|\|*

The brahmin lights a sacrificial fire within his body and makes oblations to it through his mouth with any food that he has come by. With this body, a mass of fire, he proceeds to heavenly realms. 32

## \|\| 3.9.33 \|\|

*mokṣośramaṃ yaścarate yathoktaṃ śuciḥsukhaṃ kalpitabuddhiyuktaḥ \|*  
*anindhanaṃ jyotiriva praśāntaḥsa brahmalokaṃ śrayate dvijātiḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

A stainless brahmin who attains the state of liberation that I’ve described, fixed on the thought of his own resolution, will reach the tranquil realm of Brahmā, which shines like a light that needs no fuel. 33

## Chapter 10 - Birth and marriage

## \|\| 3.10.1 \|\|

*sagara uvāca*  
*kathitaṃ cāturāśramyaṃ cāturvarṇyakriyās tathā \|*  
*puṃsaḥ kriyāmahaṃ śrotumicchāmi dvijasattama \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sagara:

You’ve described the fourfold stages and the duties of each community, best of brahmins. Now I’d like to hear about the rituals folk perform. 1

## \|\| 3.10.2 \|\|

*nityanaimittikāḥ kāmyāḥ kriyāḥ puṃsāmaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*samākhyāhi bhṛguśreṣṭha sarvajño hyasi me mataḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Tell me all about the regular and occasional rites and those performed by people with a special need, outstanding Bhṛgu, as you know it all, or so it seems to me. 2

## \|\| 3.10.3 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*yadetaduktaṃ bhavatā nityanaimittikāśrayam \|*  
*tadahaṃ kathayiṣyāṃmi śṛṇuṣvaikamanā mama \|\| 3 \|\|*

I’ll now describe the regular and occasional rites you’ve asked about, your majesty, so listen carefully. 3

## \|\| 3.10.4 \|\|

*jātasya jātakarmādikriyākāṇḍamaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*putrasya kurvīta pitā śrāddhaṃ cābhyudayātmakam \|\| 4 \|\|*

A father should perform the entire cycle of birth rituals, as well as a joyous śrāddha, when a son is born. 4

## \|\| 3.10.5 \|\|

*yugmāṃstu prāḍmukhānviprānbhojayenmanubheśvara \|*  
*yathā tṛptis tathā kuryāddaivaṃ pitryaṃ dvijanmanām \|\| 5 \|\|*

He should offer food to pairs of brahmins facing east, sire, and, according to his means, sacrifice to the gods and ancestral spirits, as befits the twice-born orders. 5

## \|\| 3.10.6 \|\|

*dadhnā yuktaiḥ sabadarair miśrān piḍān mudā yutaḥ \|*  
*nāndīmukhebhyastīrthena dadyāddaivena pārthiva \|\| 6 \|\|*

Filled with joy, he should offer riceballs mixed with yoghurt, dates and barley to the Nāndīmukha spirits, sire, with the tips of those fingers sacred to the gods, 6

## \|\| 3.10.7 \|\|

*prājāpatyena vā sarvamupacāraṃ pradakṣiṇam \|*  
*kurvīta tattathāśeṣavṛddhikāleṣu bhūpate \|\| 7 \|\|*

or with the root of his little finger, sacrosanct to Brahmā. He should then perform all the rites and circumambulations to ensure the child will come of age, your majesty. 7

## \|\| 3.10.8 \|\|

*tataś ca nāma kurvīta pitaiva daśamehani \|*  
*devapūrvaṃ narākhyaṃ hi śarmavarmādisaṃyutam \|\| 8 \|\|*

Following that, on the tenth day, the father should name the child. The first part should be a deity’s name, and the second should befit a person, such as śarman or varman, ‘prosperity’ or ‘defence’. 8

## \|\| 3.10.9 \|\|

*śarmeti brāhyaṇasyoktaṃ varmeti kṣatrasaṃśrayam \|*  
*guptadāsātmakaṃ nāma praśastaṃ vaiśyaśudrayoḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Śarman is said to be for brahmins and varman for kṣatriyas, while gupta and dāsa, ‘protected’ and ‘servant’, are best for vaiśyas and for śūdras. 9

## \|\| 3.10.10 \|\|

*nārthahīnaṃ na cāśastaṃ nāpaśabdayutaṃ tathā \|*  
*nāmaṅgalyaṃ jugupsākaṃ nāma kuryātsamākṣaram \|\| 10 \|\|*

He shouldn’t choose a name that has no meaning, is unattested, vulgar, unlucky or unsuitable, and it should have an even number of syllables. 10

## \|\| 3.10.11 \|\|

*nātidīrghaṃnātihrasvaṃ nātigurvakṣarānvitam \|*  
*sukhoccāryaṃ tu tannāma kuryādyatpraṇavākṣaram \|\| 11 \|\|*

He should choose a name that’s not too long or short. It shouldn’t have too many long vowels but be easy to pronounce and end in a short vowel. 11

## \|\| 3.10.12 \|\|

*tato 'nantarasaṃskārasaṃskṛto guruveśmani \|*  
*yathoktavidhimāśritya kuryādvidyāparigraham \|\| 12 \|\|*

Following this, after the child has been blessed with successive rites, he should start his education in his guru’s home in accordance with tradition, as I’ve described. 12

## \|\| 3.10.13 \|\|

*gṛhītavidyo gurave dattvā ca gurudakṣiṇām \|*  
*gārhasthyamicchanbhūpāla kuryāddāraparigraham \|\| 13 \|\|*

Once his education is complete and he has recompensed his guru, your majesty, if he wants to be a householder, he should marry. 13

## \|\| 3.10.14 \|\|

*brahmacaryeṇa vā kālaṃ kuryātsaṃkalpapūrvakam \|*  
*gugoḥ śuśruṣaṇaṃ kuryāttatputrāderathāpi vā \|\| 14 \|\|*

Alternatively, he may remain a brahmacārin, having taken fitting vows, and serve his guru, his guru’s sons and dependants. 14

## \|\| 3.10.15 \|\|

*vaikhānaso vāpi bhavetparivrāḍatha vecchayā \|*  
*pūrvasaṃkalpitaṃ yādṛk tādṛkkuryānnarādhipa \|\| 15 \|\|*

He may decide to be an ascetic in the forest or a mendicant. Whichever path he takes, sire, he should abide by his decision. 15

## \|\| 3.10.16 \|\|

*varṣair ekaguṇāṃ bhāryāmudvahettrigumaḥsvayam \|*  
*nāti keśāmakeśāṃ vā nātikṛṣṇāṃ na piṅgalām \|\| 16 \|\|*

He should wed a girl one-third his own age, who’s neither bald nor hairy, dark nor pale. 16

## \|\| 3.10.17 \|\|

*nisargatodhikāṅgāṃ vā nyūnāṅgāmapi nodvahet \|*  
*nāviśuddhāṃ saromāṃ vākulajāṃ vāpi rogiṇīm \|\| 17 \|\|*

He shouldn’t wed a girl who was born a cripple, has extra limbs or is impure, hirsute, low-born or sickly, 17

## \|\| 3.10.18 \|\|

*na duṣṭāṃ duṣṭavākyāṃ vā vyaṅginīṃ putṛmātṛtaḥ \|*  
*na śmaśruvyañjanavatīṃ na caiva puruṣākṛtim \|\| 18 \|\|*

Or wicked, rude or who inherited deformity from her father or her mother, 18

## \|\| 3.10.19 \|\|

*na gharvarasvarāṃ kṣāmāṃ tathā kākasvarāṃ na ca \|*  
*nānibandhekṣaṇāṃ tadvadvṛttākṣīṃ nodvahedbudhaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

Who has signs of a beard or a mannish appearance or who mumbles, shrieks or whispers. Nor should he wed a girl who blinks too much or whose eyes are bulging. 19

## \|\| 3.10.20 \|\|

*yasyāś ca romaśe jaṅghe gulphau yasyāstathonnatau \|*  
*gaṇḍayoḥ kūparau yasyā hasaṃtyāstāṃ na codvahet \|\| 20 \|\|*

Nor should he wed a girl with hairy legs, thick ankles or dimples in her cheeks when laughing. 20

## \|\| 3.10.21 \|\|

*nātirūkṣacchaviṃ pāḍukarajāmaruṇekṣaṇām \|*  
*āpīna hastapādāṃ ca nakanyāmudvahedbudhaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

A wise man should never wed a girl with rough skin, white fingernails, red eyes, pudgy hands or fat feet. 21

## \|\| 3.10.22 \|\|

*na vāmanāṃ nātidīrghāṃ nodvahetsaṃhatabhruvam \|*  
*na cāti cchidradaśanāṃ na karālamukhīṃ naraḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

Nor should he wed a girl who’s too short or tall, whose eyebrows meet, whose teeth protrude or have gaps between them. 22

## \|\| 3.10.23 \|\|

*pañcamīṃ mātṛpakṣācca pitṛpakṣācca saptamīm \|*  
*gṛhasthaścodvahetkanyāṃ nyāyena vidhinā nṛpa \|\| 23 \|\|*

In accordance with tradition, your majesty, a householder should wed a girl five degrees removed on his mother’s side and seven on his father’s. 23

## \|\| 3.10.24 \|\|

*brāhmo daivastathaivārṣaḥ prājāpatyastathāsuraḥ \|*  
*gāndharvarākṣayau cānyau paiśācaś cāṣṭamo mataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Different forms of marriage are attributed to Brahmā, the deities, sages, patriarchs, demigods, gandharvas and rākṣasas, with paiśācas as the eighth and vilest form. 24

## \|\| 3.10.25 \|\|

*eteṣāṃ yasya yo dharmo varmasyokto maharṣibhiḥ \|*  
*kurvīta dāragrahaṇaṃ tenānyaṃ parivarjayet \|\| 25 \|\|*

One should marry according to whichever form great sages recommend for one’s community, avoiding all the others. 25

## \|\| 3.10.26 \|\|

*sadharmacāriṇīṃ prāpya gārhasthyaṃ sahitastayā \|*  
*samudvaheddadātyetatsamyagūḍhaṃ mahāphalam \|\| 26 \|\|*

When a man becomes a householder, he should wed a girl who practises virtue at his side. This custom, closely followed, yields magnificent rewards. 26

## Chapter 11 - Rituals of the householder

## \|\| 3.11.1 \|\|

*sagara uvāca*  
*gṛhasthasya sadācāraṃ śrotumicchāmyahaṃ mune \|*  
*lokādasmātparasmācca yamātiṣṭhanna hīyate \|\| 1 \|\|*

I’d like to hear about the proper conduct of a householder, sage, adhering to which he’ll never be excluded from this world or the next. 1

## \|\| 3.11.2 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*śrūyatāṃ pṛthivīpāla sadācārasya lakṣaṇam \|*  
*sadācāravatā puṃsā jitau lokāvubhāvapi \|\| 2 \|\|*

Listen, your majesty, to the marks of proper conduct. Anyone who follows these will conquer both the worlds. 2

## \|\| 3.11.3 \|\|

*sādhavaḥ kṣīṇadeṣāstu saccabdaḥ sādhuvācakaḥ \|*  
*teṣāmācaraṇaṃ yastu sadācāraḥsa ucyate \|\| 3 \|\|*

Sādhus are faultless, holy men. Sat is another word for sādhu, and the behaviour, ācāra, of such beings is called sat-ācāra, ‘good conduct’. 3

## \|\| 3.11.4 \|\|

*saptarṣayotha manavaḥ prajānāṃ patayas tathā \|*  
*sadācārasya vaktāraḥ kartāraś ca mahīpate \|\| 4 \|\|*

The seven sages, Manus and patriarchs, sire, both taught and practised such behaviour. 4

## \|\| 3.11.5 \|\|

*brāhmo muhūrte cotthāya manasā matimātrṛpa \|*  
*prabuddhaścintayeddharmamarthaṃ cāpyavirodhinam \|\| 5 \|\|*

The wise person wakes at the Brāhma-muhūrta, ‘the hour of Brahmā’, ninety minutes before the dawn, when his mind is clear, your majesty. When he’s wide awake, he should meditate on virtue, prosperity and the obstacles to both. 5

## \|\| 3.11.6 \|\|

*apīḍayā tayoḥ kāmamubhayorapi cintayet \|*  
*dṛṣṭādṛṣṭavināśāya trivarge samadarśinā \|\| 6 \|\|*

He should also meditate on love in forms that don’t conflict with other goals and should regard these threefold aims of life as equally important to overcome all obstacles, both visible and invisible. 6

## \|\| 3.11.7 \|\|

*parityajedarthakāmau dharmapīḍākarau nṛpa \|*  
*dharmamapyasukhodarkaṃ lokavidveṣṭameva ca \|\| 7 \|\|*

He should abandon wealth and love if they conflict with virtue, sire, but may depart from virtue if it leads to misery or offends against society. 7

## \|\| 3.11.8 \|\|

*tataḥ kālyaṃ samutthāya kuryānmūtraṃ nareśvara \|\| 8 \|\|*

Following this, after rising in the morning, your majesty, he should venerate the sun. 8

## \|\| 3.11.9 \|\|

*nairṛtyāmiṣuvikṣepamatītyābhyadhikaṃ bhuvaḥ \|*  
*dvarādāvasathānmūtra purīṣaṃ ca visarjayet \|\| 9 \|\|*

Leaving the village in a south-westerly direction, he should relieve himself at a distance further than an arrow flies. 9

## \|\| 3.11.10 \|\|

*pādāvanejanocchiṣṭe prakṣipenna gṛhāṅgaṇe \|\| 10 \|\|*

After washing his two feet, he should discard the dirty water in the courtyard of his house. 10

## \|\| 3.11.11 \|\|

*ātmacchāyāṃ tarucchāyāṃ gosūryāgnyanilāṃs tathā \|*  
*gurudvijādīṃstu budho nādhimehetkadācana \|\| 11 \|\|*

A wise man never urinates on his shadow or that of a tree or in the direction of a cow, the sun, a fire, the wind, his guru or a member of the twice-born orders. 11

## \|\| 3.11.12 \|\|

*na kṛṣṭe sasyamadhye vā gauvraje janasaṃsadi \|*  
*na vartmani na nadyāditīrtheṣu puruṣarṣabha \|\| 12 \|\|*

Nor should he defecate or urinate on cultivated land, in a field of corn, a cow-pen, in an assembly, on a road, in a river or at a sacred ford, you bull among men, 12

## \|\| 3.11.13 \|\|

*nāpsu naivāṃbhasastīre śmaśāne na samācaret \|*  
*utsargaṃ vai purīṣasya mūtrasya ca visarjanam \|\| 13 \|\|*

Nor should he defecate or urinate in water, on a riverbank or cremation ground. 13

## \|\| 3.11.14 \|\|

*udaṅmukho divāmūtraṃ viparītamukho niśi \|*  
*kurvitānāpadi prājño mūtrotsargaṃ ca pārthiva \|\| 14 \|\|*

During the day, a wise man should urinate facing north, and at night the opposite direction, except in an emergency, your majesty. 14

## \|\| 3.11.15 \|\|

*tṛṇair āstīrya vasudhāṃ vastraprāvṛtamastakaḥ \|*  
*tiṣṭhennāticiraṃ tatra naiva kiñcidudīrayet \|\| 15 \|\|*

Spreading grass on the ground and placing his clothes on his head, he shouldn’t tarry or engage in conversation. 15

## \|\| 3.11.16 \|\|

*valmīkamūṣikodbhūtāṃ mṛdaṃ nāntarjalāṃ tathā \|*  
*śaucāvaśiṣṭāṃ gehāccanādadyāllepasaṃbhavām \|\| 16 \|\|*

He shouldn’t use earth from an anthill, or dug up by rats, or taken from water, or used by someone else for cleaning, or left over from plastering a house, 16

## \|\| 3.11.17 \|\|

*aṇuprāṇyupapannāṃ ca halotkhātāṃ ca pārthiva \|*  
*parityajenmṛdo hyetāḥsakalāḥ śaucakarmaṇi \|\| 17 \|\|*

Or cast up by worms or turned by a plough, sire, but should avoid these types of earth to clean himself. 17

## \|\| 3.11.18 \|\|

*ekā liṅge gude tistro dasa vāmakare nṛpa \|*  
*hastadvaye ca spata syurmṛdaḥ śaucopapādikāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

He should use one handful of earth to clean his penis, three for his anus, ten for his left hand and seven for both hands, your majesty. 18

## \|\| 3.11.19 \|\|

*acchenāgandhalepena jalenābudbudena ca \|*  
*ācāmeccamṛdaṃbhūyas tathā dadyātsamāhitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

He should rinse his mouth with clear, odourless water, free from froth and bubbles. With a steadied mind, he should use more earth again. 19

## \|\| 3.11.20 \|\|

*niṣpāditāṅghriśaucastu pādāvabhyukṣya taiḥ punaḥ \|*  
*triḥ pibetsalilaṃ tena tathā dviḥ parimārjayet \|\| 20 \|\|*

When he’s cleaned his feet and rinsed them, he should take three sips of water and wash his face twice over. 20

## \|\| 3.11.21 \|\|

*śirṣaṇyāni tataḥ khāni mūrdhānaṃ ca samālabhet \|*  
*bāhū nābhiṃ ca toyena hṛdayaṃ cāpi saṃspṛśet \|\| 21 \|\|*

Next, he should splash water on the orifices of his head—the eyes, ears and nostrils—and on his crown, arms, chest and navel, sire. 21

## \|\| 3.11.22 \|\|

*svācāntastu tataḥ kuryātpumānkeśaprasādhanam \|*  
*ādarśāñjanamāṅgalyaṃ dūrvādyālaṃbhanāni ca \|\| 22 \|\|*

When he’s finished washing, he should tidy up his hair and place a tilak on his forehead before a mirror with a stalk of dūrvā-grass or something similar. 22

## \|\| 3.11.23 \|\|

*tataḥsvavarṇadharmeṇa vṛttyarthaṃ ca dhanārjanam \|*  
*kurvīta śraddhāsaṃpanno yajecca pṛthivīpate \|\| 23 \|\|*

In accordance with the practices of his community, sire, he should start to earn some money to support himself and carry out the sacrifices, filled with faith. 23

## \|\| 3.11.24 \|\|

*somasaṃsthā haviḥsaṃsthāḥ pākasaṃsthāstu saṃsthitāḥ \|*  
*dhane yato manuṣyāṇāṃ yatetāto dhanārjane \|\| 24 \|\|*

Since these rituals, based on offerings of soma, foods and butter, are undertaken to bring folk wealth, he may try to earn a living from them. 24

## \|\| 3.11.25 \|\|

*nadīnadataṭākeṣu devakhātajaleṣu ca \|*  
*nityakriyārthaṃ snāyīta gīriprastravaṇeṣu ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

Before conducting regular sacrifices, he should bathe in the water of a river great or small, a pool or mountain stream. 25

## \|\| 3.11.26 \|\|

*kūpeṣūddhṛtatoyena snānaṃ kurvīta vā bhuvi \|*  
*gṛheṣūddhṛtatoyena hyathavā bhuvyasaṃbhave \|\| 26 \|\|*

He may wash himself on land with water from a well, or with water brought for bathing from a nearby source. 26

## \|\| 3.11.27 \|\|

*śucivastradharaḥ snāto devarṣipitṛtarpaṇam \|*  
*teṣāmeva hi tīrthenakurvīta susamāhitaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

After he has washed, donned fresh clothes and calmed his mind, he should make libations to the gods, the sages and ancestral spirits with those parts of his hand sacred to each. 27

## \|\| 3.11.28 \|\|

*trirapaḥ prīṇanārthāya devānāmapavarjayet \|*  
*ṛṣīṇāṃ ca yathānyāyaṃ sakṛccāpi prajāpateḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

He should make three libations of water to please the gods, the same for the sages according to propriety and one for Brahmā the progenitor. 28

## \|\| 3.11.29 \|\|

*pitṝṇāṃ prīṇanārthāya tadapaḥ pṛtivīpate \|*  
*pitāmahebhyaś ca tathā prīmayetprapitāmahān \|\| 29 \|\|*

He should also make three offerings of water to please his ancestral spirits, sire, including his grandfathers and great-grandfathers. 29

## \|\| 3.11.30 \|\|

*mātāmahāya tatpitre tatpitre ca samāhitaḥ \|*  
*dadyātpaitreṇatīrthena kāmyaṃ cānyacchṛṇuṣva me \|\| 30 \|\|*

With his mind still focused, he should make offerings to his paternal grandmother, her father and his father with the forefinger sacred to each, and, if he so desires—listen to me— 30

## \|\| 3.11.31 \|\|

*mātre svamātre tanmātre gurupatnyai tathā nṛpa \|*  
*gurūṇāṃ mātulānāṃ ca snigdhamitrāya bhūbhūje \|\| 31 \|\|*

To his mother, maternal grandmother, her mother, his guru’s wife, his guru, his maternal uncles and other kin, to his dearest friends and to the king, your majesty. 31

## \|\| 3.11.32 \|\|

*idaṃ cāpi japedaṃbu dadyādātmecchayānṛpa \|*  
*upakārāya bhūtānāṃ kṛtadevāditarpaṇam \|\| 32 \|\|*

Once he has propitiated the deities and the rest, if he wishes, sire, he may recite this prayer and offer water for the benefit of all beings: 32

## \|\| 3.11.33 \|\|

*devāsurās tathā yakṣā nāgagandharvarākṣasāḥ \|*  
*piśācā guhyakāḥsiddhāḥ kūṣmāḍāḥ paśavaḥ khagāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

‘Gods, demigods, yakṣas, nāgas, gandharvas, rākṣasas, paiśācas, guhyakas, siddhas, kūṣmāṇḍas, trees, birds, 33

## \|\| 3.11.34 \|\|

*jalecarā bhūlecarā bhūnilayā vāyvāhārāś ca jatavaḥ \|*  
*tṛptimetena yāntyāśu maddattenāṃbunākhilāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

Those animals that live in water, on land or in the air—may all these beings be satisfied at once by this water that I offer. 34

## \|\| 3.11.35 \|\|

*narakeṣu samasteṣu yātanāsu ca saṃsthitāḥ \|*  
*teṣāmāpyāyanāyautaddīyate salilaṃ mayā \|\| 35 \|\|*

This I do to benefit all those beings enduring torment in the hells. 35

## \|\| 3.11.36 \|\|

*ye bāndhavābāndhavā ye yenyajanmani bāndhavāḥ \|*  
*te tṛptimakhilā yāntu ye cāsmattobhivāñchati \|\| 36 \|\|*

Those who are my kin and those who aren’t, those who were my kin in previous lives and any beings who desire an offering of water from me—may they all be satisfied! 36

## \|\| 3.11.37 \|\|

*yatra kvacanasaṃsthānāṃ kṣuttṛṣṇopahatātmanām \|*  
*idamāpyāyanāyāstu mayā dattaṃ tilodakam \|\| 37 \|\|*

May these drops of water that I offer relieve those beings beset by thirst and hunger wherever they may be.’ 37

## \|\| 3.11.38 \|\|

*kāmyodakapradānaṃ te mayaitatkathitaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*yaddattvā prīṇayatyetanmanuṣyaḥsakalaṃ jagat \|*  
*jagadāpyāyanodbhūtaṃ puṇyamāpnoti cānadha \|\| 38 \|\|*

A person may propitiate this whole world with the voluntary libations I’ve described, your majesty, and, having done so, may acquire the merit that arises from benefiting the world, blameless monarch. 38

## \|\| 3.11.39 \|\|

*dattvā kāmyodakaṃ samyagetebhyaḥ śrāddhayānvitaḥ \|*  
*ācamya ca tato dadyātsūryāya salilāñjalim \|\| 39 \|\|*

Having duly made these offerings, filled with faith and taking water for himself, he should cup his hands and offer it to the sun: 39

## \|\| 3.11.40 \|\|

*namo vivasvate brahmabhāsvate viṣṇutejase \|*  
*jagatsavitre śucaye savitre karmasākṣiṇe \|\| 40 \|\|*

‘I bow to the shining one, the Absolute, the brilliant sun whose energy comes from Viṣṇu, who enlivens the world, the pure stimulator who grants the reward of sacrifices.’ 40

## \|\| 3.11.41 \|\|

*tato gṛhār'canaṃ kuryādabhīṣṭasurapūjanam \|*  
*jalābhiṣekaiḥ puṣpaiś ca dhūpādyaiś ca nivedanam \|\| 41 \|\|*

Following this, he should perform a domestic ritual, worshipping his tutelary deity with offerings of water, flowers, incense and the rest. 41

## \|\| 3.11.42 \|\|

*apūrvamāgnihontra ca kuryātprāgbrahmaṇe nṛpa \|\| 42 \|\|*

Before all else, he should perform a fire sacrifice for Brahmā. 42

## \|\| 3.11.43 \|\|

*prajāpatiṃ samuddiśya dadyādāhutimādarāt \|*  
*gṛhebhyaḥ kāśya pāyātha tatonumataye kramāt \|\| 43 \|\|*

He should reverently offer an oblation to the progenitor, the household deities, Kāśyapa and Anumati in succession. 43

## \|\| 3.11.44 \|\|

*taccheṣaṃ maṇike pṛthvīrpajanyebhyaḥ kṣipettataḥ \|*  
*dvāre dhāturvidhātuś ca madhye ca brahmaṇe kṣipet \|\| 44 \|\|*

He should then pour the remaining water as a libation to the Maṇika and Parjanya rain gods, at the door of his house to Dhātṛ and Vidhātṛ and at its centre to Brahmā. 44

## \|\| 3.11.45 \|\|

*gṛhasya puraṣabyāghra digdevānapi me śṛṇu \|\| 45 \|\|*

Now, tiger among men, you’ll hear about the household deities of the four directions. 45

## \|\| 3.11.46 \|\|

*indrāya dharmarājāya varuṇāya tathendave \|*  
*prācyādiṣu budho dadyāddhutaśeṣātmakaṃ balim \|\| 46 \|\|*

A prudent man should offer food left over from the sacrifice to Indra, Yama, king of justice, Varuṇa and Indu the Moon in the east and other directions in succession. 46

## \|\| 3.11.47 \|\|

*prāguttare ca digbhāge dhanvantāribaliṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*nirvapaidvaiśvadevaṃ ca karma kuryādataḥ param \|\| 47 \|\|*

In the north-east corner, a wise person should scatter an offering of food for Dhanvantari, the heavenly physician, then offer some to Viśvadeva. 47

## \|\| 3.11.48 \|\|

*vāyavyāṃ vāyave dikṣu samastāsu yathādiśam \|*  
*brahmaṇe cāntārīkṣāya bhānave ca kṣipedbalim \|\| 48 \|\|*

In the north-west corner, he should offer food to Vāyu, god of winds, then to each direction, to Brahmā, the sky and sun, which is the source of everything. 48

## \|\| 3.11.49 \|\|

*viśvedavānviśvabhūtānaṣṭau viśvapatīnpitṝn \|*  
*yakṣāṇāṃ ca samuddiśya baliṃ dadyānnareśvara \|\| 49 \|\|*

He should offer food to all the deities and other beings, the universal lords, ancestral spirits and Yakṣman, god of tuberculosis, your majesty. 49

## \|\| 3.11.50 \|\|

*tatonyadannamādāya bhūmi bhāge śucau budhaḥ \|*  
*dadyādaśeṣabhūtebhyaḥ sveccayā susamāhitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

Bringing other foodstuffs, a wise person may make an offering with a focused mind to all the other beings on a clean patch of ground if he so desires. 50

## \|\| 3.11.51 \|\|

*devā manuṣyāḥ paśavo vayāṃsi siddhāḥsayakṣoragadaityasaṃghāḥ \|*  
*pratoḥ piśācāstaravaḥsa mastā ye cānnamicchanti mayātra dattam \|\| 51 \|\|*

‘Deities, humankind, animals, birds, siddhas, hosts of yakṣas, nāgas and Daityas, spirits of the dead, paiśācas, trees and all those who desire the food I offer, 51

## \|\| 3.11.52 \|\|

*pipīlikāḥ kīṭapataṅgakādyā bubhukṣitāḥ karmanibandhabaddhāḥ \|*  
*prayānti te tṛptimidaṃ mayānnantebhyo visṛṣṭaṃ sukhino bhavantu \|\| 52 \|\|*

Ants, worms, moths and other insects—hungry and bound by bonds of karma—may they all be sustained by this and may they all be happy. 52

## \|\| 3.11.53 \|\|

*yeṣāṃ na mātā na pitā na bandhurnaivānnasiddhirna nathānnamasti \|*  
*tattṛpye 'nnaṃ bhuvi dattameva te yāntu tṛptiṃ muditā bhavantu \|\| 53 \|\|*

I place this food upon the ground to nourish those who have no mother, father, family, sustenance or the means of preparing it, and may they all be satisfied and gladdened! 53

## \|\| 3.11.54 \|\|

*bhūtāni sarvāṇi tathānnametadahaṃ ca viṣṇurna yatonyadasti \|*  
*tasmādahaṃ bhūtanikāyabhūtamannaṃ prayacchāmi bhavāya teṣām \|\| 54 \|\|*

Since all creatures, this food, Viṣṇu and I are one, I make this offering, a form of every being, to benefit them all. 54

## \|\| 3.11.55 \|\|

*caturdaśo bhūtagaṇo ya eṣa tatra sthitā ye 'khilabhūtasaṃghāḥ \|*  
*tṛptyarthamannaṃ hi mayā nisṛṣṭaṃ teṣāmidaṃ te muditā bhavantu \|\| 55 \|\|*

Here I scatter food to nourish living beings of fourteen kinds and all the gathered hosts of creatures. May they all rejoice!’ 55

## \|\| 3.11.56 \|\|

*ityuccārya naro dadyādannaṃ śraddhāsamanvitaḥ \|*  
*bhuvi sarvopakārāya gṛhī sarvāśrayo yataḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

Reciting this prayer, a person filled with faith should cast some food upon the ground to benefit each living thing, because the householder is the refuge of them all. 56

## \|\| 3.11.57 \|\|

*śvacaṇḍālavihaṅgānāṃ bhuvi dadyānnareśvara \|*  
*ye cānye patitāḥ kecidaputrāḥ saṃtimānavāḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

Following this, one may place some food upon the ground for dogs, birds, untouchables and other fallen and unworthy people. 57

## \|\| 3.11.58 \|\|

*tato godohamātraṃ vai kālaṃ tiṣṭhedgṛhāṅgaṇe \|*  
*atithigrahaṇārthāya tadūrdhvaṃ tu tathaicchāyā \|\| 58 \|\|*

After this, he should stand in the courtyard of his house for as long as it takes to milk a cow, or longer if he so desires, to wait for any visitors. 58

## \|\| 3.11.59 \|\|

*atithiṃ tatra saṃprāptaṃ pūjayetsvāgadādinā \|*  
*tathāsanapradānena pādaprakṣālanena ca \|\| 59 \|\|*

He should warmly welcome any guest who comes, offer him a seat and allow him to wash his feet. 59

## \|\| 3.11.60 \|\|

*śraddhayā cānnadānena priyapraśnottareṇa ca \|*  
*gacchataścānuyānena prītimutpādayedgṛhī \|\| 60 \|\|*

He should sincerely offer food and ask and answer questions in a civil manner. When the visitor departs, the householder should show respect by seeing him on his way. 60

## \|\| 3.11.61 \|\|

*ajñātakulanāmānamanyadeśādupāgatam \|*  
*pūjayedatithiṃ samyaṅnaikagrāmanivāsinam \|\| 61 \|\|*

He should also duly welcome any guest whose clan and name he does not know, who comes from far away or who lives in another village. 61

## \|\| 3.11.62 \|\|

*akiñcanamasaṃbandhamajñātakulaśīlinam \|*  
*asaṃpūjyātithiṃ bhuktvā bhoktukāmaṃ vrajatyadhaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

One who feeds himself but doesn’t honour hungry guests who have no kin or come from a distant region goes to hell. 62

## \|\| 3.11.63 \|\|

*svādhyāyagotrācaraṇamapṛṣṭvā ca tathā kulam \|*  
*hiraṇyagarbhabuddhyā taṃ manyetābhyāgataṃ gṛhī \|\| 63 \|\|*

Without asking which branch of the Veda he recites, or about his lineage, religious practice or family, a householder should regard a guest as Brahmā, the deity born of the golden egg. 63

## \|\| 3.11.64 \|\|

*pitrarthaṃ cāparaṃ vipramekamapyāśayennṛpa \|*  
*taddeśyaṃ viditācārasaṃbhūtiṃ pāñcayajñikam \|\| 64 \|\|*

To benefit his ancestors, sire, he should accommodate one additional brahmin from the same locality as himself, whose conduct and lineage are known, in order to perform the five required sacrifices. 64

## \|\| 3.11.65 \|\|

*annāgrañca samuddhṛtya hantakāropakalpitam \|*  
*nirvāpabhūtaṃ bhūpāla śrotriyāyopapādayet \|\| 65 \|\|*

Taking a serve of grain, he should cry ‘Come!’, your majesty, and offer it to a brahmin who is expert in the Vedas. 65

## \|\| 3.11.66 \|\|

*dadyācca bhikṣātritayaṃ parivrāḍbrahmacārīṇām \|*  
*icchayā ca budho dadyādvibhave satyavāritam \|\| 66 \|\|*

He should also give three serves to mendicants and brahmacārins. The prudent man should give generously, in accordance with his wishes, if he can afford to do so. 66

## \|\| 3.11.67 \|\|

*ityetetithayaḥ proktā prāguktā bhikṣavaś ca ye \|*  
*caturaḥ pūjāyitvaitānnṛpa pāpātpramucyate \|\| 67 \|\|*

One should honour these four as guests, including the mendicants mentioned earlier. By doing so, he acquits his debt to humanity and the sacrifice. 67

## \|\| 3.11.68 \|\|

*atithiryasya bhagnāśo gṛhātpratinivartate \|*  
*sa tasmai duṣkṛtaṃ dattvā puṇyamādāya gacchati \|\| 68 \|\|*

A guest who turns from a house to go elsewhere, his hopes frustrated, leaves his sins with the householder and takes that person’s good luck with him. 68

## \|\| 3.11.69 \|\|

*dhātā prajāpatiḥ śakro vahnirvasugaṇor'yayā \|*  
*praviśyātithimete vai bhuñjantennaṃ nareśvara \|\| 69 \|\|*

Brahmā the progenitor, mighty Indra, Agni, god of fire, the host of Vasus and the Sun are all present in the person of a guest, sire, and eat the food he eats. 69

## \|\| 3.11.70 \|\|

*tasmādatithipūjāyāṃ yateta satataṃnaraḥ \|*  
*sa kevalamaghaṃ bhuṅkte yo bhuṅkte hyatithiṃ vinā \|\| 70 \|\|*

That’s why you should always do your best to honour guests. One who eats without offering food to guests will feed on suffering alone. 70

## \|\| 3.11.71 \|\|

*tataḥ svavāsinīṃ duḥkhīṃ gurviṇīṃ vṛddhabālakān \|*  
*bhojayetsaṃskṛtānnena prathamaṃ caramaṃ gṛhī \|\| 71 \|\|*

The householder should next give sanctified food to those daughters who still live with him, the afflicted, pregnant, elderly and the young, while he himself eats last. 71

## \|\| 3.11.72 \|\|

*abhuktavatsu caiteṣu bhuñjanbhuṅkte sa duṣkṛtam \|*  
*mṛtaś ca gatvā narakaṃ śloṣmabhugjāyate naraḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

One who dines before these others commits a grave misdeed and, when he dies, he’ll go to hell and live on nothing but phlegm. 72

## \|\| 3.11.73 \|\|

*astrātāśī malaṃ bhukte hyajapī pūyaśoṇitam \|*  
*asaṃskṛtānnabhuṅmūtraṃ bālādiprathamaṃ śakṛt \|\| 73 \|\|*

One who eats without bathing first will feed on filth; without a prayer, on pus and blood. One who eats unsanctified foods will drink urine, and one who eats before the young and others will swallow excrement. 73

## \|\| 3.11.74 \|\|

*ahomī ca kṛmīnbhuṅkte adattvā viṣamāśnute \|\| 74 \|\|*

Listen therefore, your majesty, to how a householder should eat, and how, when eating, he may avoid the bonds of sin. 74

## \|\| 3.11.75 \|\|

*tasmācchṛṇuṣva rājandra yathā bhuñjīta vai gṛhī \|*  
*bhuñjataś ca yathā puṃsaḥ pāpabandho na jāyate \|\| 75 \|\|*

And how in this life he may enjoy unequalled health, increased vigour, an end to strife and his enemies’ demise, your majesty. 75

## \|\| 3.11.76 \|\|

*iha cārogyavipulaṃ balabuddhis tathā nṛpa \|*  
*bhavatyariṣṭaśāntiś ca vairipakṣābhicāriṇaḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

After he has duly bathed and propitiated the gods, the ancestors and sages, the prudent householder, wearing an appropriate gemstone on his finger, may eat, 76

## \|\| 3.11.77 \|\|

*snāto tathāvatkṛtvā ca devarṣipitṛtarpaṇam \|*  
*praśastaratnapāṇistu bhuñjīta prayato gṛhī \|\| 77 \|\|*

But only after he’s said his prayers, made an offering to the fire, changed his clothes, your majesty, and offered food to guests and brahmins, elders and dependants. He should wear some pleasant scent and a fitting garland, sire. 77

## \|\| 3.11.78 \|\|

*kṛte jape hute vahnau śuddhavastradharo nṛpa \|*  
*dattvātithibhyo viprebhyo gurubhyaḥ saṃśritāya ca \|*  
*pumyagandhaśsastamālyadhārī caiva nareśvara \|\| 78 \|\|*

He shouldn’t eat while wearing just a single robe, or while his hands or feet are wet, your majesty. With a clean and cheerful visage, he should face one cardinal point or other: 78

## \|\| 3.11.79 \|\|

*naikavastradharo nārdrapāṇipādo mahīpate \|*  
*vuśuddhavadanaḥ prīto bhuñjīta na vidiṅmukhaḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

East or north are best. Let his mind not dwell on other matters, and let his food be fitting, wholesome and prepared with purest water. 79

## \|\| 3.11.80 \|\|

*prāṅmukhodaṅmukho vāpi na caivānyamanā naraḥ \|*  
*annaṃ praśastaṃ pathyaṃ ca prokṣitaṃ prokṣaṇodakaiḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

Food should not be accepted from those of loose morality, be unattractive or unconsecrated. The householder should offer food to hungry students. 80

## \|\| 3.11.81 \|\|

*na kutsitāhṛ-taṃ naiva jugupsāpadasaṃskṛtam \|*  
*dattvā tu bhaktaṃ śiṣyebhyaḥ kṣudhitebhyas tathā gṛhī \|\| 81 \|\|*

He may then dine cheerfully from vessels clean and suitable, your majesty. 81

## \|\| 3.11.82 \|\|

*praśastaśuddhapātre tu bhuñjītākupito dvijaḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

He shouldn’t eat from dishes on a stool, in an improper place, sire, or at improper times, or with a troubled mind, first having offered the best part to the fire. 82

## \|\| 3.11.83 \|\|

*nāsaṃdisaṃsthite pātre nādeśe ca nareśvara \|*  
*nākāle nātisaṃkīrṇe dattvāgraṃ ca narognaye \|\| 83 \|\|*

His food should be sanctified with mantras, be suitable and be fresh on the day, your majesty, except for fruit and meat and varieties of dried vegetables. 83

## \|\| 3.11.84 \|\|

*mantrābhimantritaṃ śastaṃ na ca paryuṣitaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*anyatra phalamūlebhyaḥ śuṣkaśākhādikāttathā \|\| 84 \|\|*

Dates and thickened treacle are also excepted, sire. He should never eat anything from which the essence has been extracted. 84

## \|\| 3.11.85 \|\|

*tadvaddhārītakebhyaś ca guḍabhakṣyebhya eva ca \|*  
*bhuñjītoddhṛtasārāṇi na kadāpi nareśvara \|\| 85 \|\|*

A discerning person should eat without leaving anything over, sire, except for honey, water, yoghurt, butter or barley grits. 85

## \|\| 3.11.86 \|\|

*na śeṣaṃ puruṣośnīyyādanyatra jagatīpate \|*  
*madhvaṃbudadhisarpibhyaḥ saktubhyaś ca vivekavān \|\| 86 \|\|*

With his mind on what he’s eating, he should first take something sweet, then eat something sour or salty as the middle course, followed by pungent or astringent foods. 86

## \|\| 3.11.87 \|\|

*aśnīyāttanmayo bhūtvā purvaṃ tu madhuraṃ rasam \|*  
*labaṇāmlo tathā madhye kaṭutiktādikāṃstataḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

One who begins and ends a meal with liquids, taking solid food between, will never be deprived of health or strength. 87

## \|\| 3.11.88 \|\|

*prāgdravaṃ puruṣośnīyānmadhye kaṭhinabhojanaḥ \|*  
*ante punardravāśī tu balārogye na muñcati \|\| 88 \|\|*

One should take five mouthfuls of wholesome food like this, in silence, without complaint or conversation, in order to sustain himself. 88

## \|\| 3.11.89 \|\|

*anindyaṃ bhakṣayeditthaṃ vāgyatonnamakutsayan \|*  
*pañcagrāsaṃ mahāmaunaṃ prāṇādyāpyāyanaṃ hi tat \|\| 89 \|\|*

When he’s finished eating and rinsed his mouth, facing east or north, he should duly rinse his mouth a second time and wash his hands below the wrist. 89

## \|\| 3.11.90 \|\|

*bhuktvā samyagathācamya prāṅmukhodaṅmukho 'pi vā \|*  
*yathāvatpunarācāmetpāṇī prakṣālya mūlataḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

Content, his mind at rest and seated comfortably, he should turn his thoughts to his tutelary deities. 90

## \|\| 3.11.91 \|\|

*svasthaḥ praśāntacittastu kṛtāsanaparigrahaḥ \|*  
*abhīṣṭadevatānāṃ tu kurvīta smaraṇaṃ naraḥ \|\| 91 \|\|*

‘May fire, stirred by wind, reduce this food to earthy elements, and in the space that air provides, digest it, and may I be satisfied. 91

## \|\| 3.11.92 \|\|

*agnirāpyāyayeddhātuṃ pārthivaṃ pavaneritaḥ \|*  
*dattāvakāśaṃnabhasā jarayatyastu me sukham \|\| 92 \|\|*

May this food, when digested, replenish the elements of earth, water, fire and wind within me, and may I enjoy unbroken happiness. 92

## \|\| 3.11.93 \|\|

*annaṃ balāya me bhūmerapāmagnyanilasya ca \|*  
*bhavatyetatparīṇataṃ mamāstyavyāhataṃ sukham \|\| 93 \|\|*

May this food vivify the five vital airs, prāṇa, apāna, samāna, udāna and vyāna, and may I enjoy unbroken happiness. 93

## \|\| 3.11.94 \|\|

*prāmāpānasamānānāmudānavyānayos tathā \|*  
*annaṃ puṣṭikaraṃ cāstu mamāpyavyāhataṃ sukham \|\| 94 \|\|*

May the fires known as Agasti, Agni and Vaḍavā digest all the food I’ve eaten. May they grant me the happiness that arises from the digestion of that food, and may I be free from physical afflictions. 94

## \|\| 3.11.95 \|\|

*agastiragnirvaḍavānalaś ca bhuktaṃ mayānnaṃ jarayatvaśeṣam \|*  
*sukhaṃ ca me tpariṇāmasaṃbhavaṃ yacchantvarogaṃ mama cāstu dehe \|\| 95 \|\|*

Viṣṇu, primary being and universal lord, is embodied in all the senses and in everybody. By this truth, may all the health-giving food I’ve eaten be digested. 95

## \|\| 3.11.96 \|\|

*viṣṇuḥsamastendriyadehadehī vṛthā na bhūto bhagavānyathaikaḥ \|*  
*satyena tenāttamaśeṣamannamārogyatāṃ me pariṇāmametu \|\| 96 \|\|*

Viṣṇu is consumer, food and digestion. By this truth also, may all the food I’ve eaten be digested.’ 96

## \|\| 3.11.97 \|\|

*viṣṇurattā tathaivānnaṃ parimāmaś ca vai tathā \|*  
*satyena tena madbhuktaṃ jīryatyannamidaṃ tathā \|\| 97 \|\|*

Having made this prayer, one should rub his stomach with his hand, then eagerly devote himself to relaxation. 97

## \|\| 3.11.98 \|\|

*ityuccārya svahastena parimṛjya tathodaram \|*  
*anāyāsapa3dāyīni kuryātkarmaṇyatandritaḥ \|\| 98 \|\|*

Dusk: The sandhyā rituals

One should spend the remainder of the day engaged in pastimes endorsed by proper scriptural authority and which don’t conflict with the path of virtue, after which one should perform the sandhyā rituals with devotion. 98

## \|\| 3.11.99 \|\|

*sacchāstrādivinodena sanmārgādavirodhinā \|*  
*dinaṃ nayettataḥsaṃdhyāmupatiṣṭhetsamāhitaḥ \|\| 99 \|\|*

At day’s end, a wise person should duly undertake these rites as the sun goes down, and in the morning before the stars have faded, having properly rinsed his mouth, your majesty. 99

## \|\| 3.11.100 \|\|

*dināntasaṃdhyāṃ sūryeṇa pūrvāmṛkṣair yutāṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*upatiṣṭhedhyathānyāyyaṃ samyagācamya pārthiva \|\| 100 \|\|*

The two sandhyā rituals should always be performed, sire, except when a baby is being born or at a time of impurity, anxiety, illness or danger. 100

## \|\| 3.11.101 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 101 \|\|*

One who rises after the sun, or retires before it sets, is obliged to perform some penance, unless he is unwell. 101

## \|\| 3.11.102 \|\|

*saryeṇābhyudito yaś ca tyaktaḥ sūryeṇa vā svapan \|*  
*anyatrāturabhāvāttu prāyaścittī bhavennaraḥ \|\| 102 \|\|*

Having risen before the sun, sire, one should perform the sandhyā ritual, and, before he sleeps, the ritual suited to the dusk. 102

## \|\| 3.11.103 \|\|

*tasmādanudite sūrye samutthāya mahīpate \|*  
*upatiṣṭhennaraḥsaṃdhyāmasvapaṃś ca dināntajām \|\| 103 \|\|*

Wicked folk who fail to offer these at dawn and dusk go to Tāmisra, the hell of darkness, sire. 103

## \|\| 3.11.104 \|\|

*upatiṣṭhanti vai saṃdhyāṃ ye na pūrvaṃ na paścimām \|*  
*vrajanti te durātmānastamistraṃ narakaṃ nṛpa \|\| 104 \|\|*

In the evening, furthermore, a householder’s wife should offer food to the Viśvadevas without a mantra, sire. 104

## \|\| 3.11.105 \|\|

*punaḥ pākamupādāya sāyamapyavanīpate \|*  
*vaiśvadevanimittaṃ vai patnyā sārdhaṃ baliṃ haret \|\| 105 \|\|*

At that same time, she should scatter food for dog-eating śvapacas and other untouchables. 105

## \|\| 3.11.106 \|\|

*tatrāpi śvapacādibyastathaivānnavisarjanam \|\| 106 \|\|*

A wise person should hospitably honour a guest who arrives at that hour, to the best of his ability, with water for washing his feet, a seat, a polite bow and words of welcome. He should then offer him food and a bed, your majesty. 106

## \|\| 3.11.107 \|\|

*atithiṃ cāgataṃ tatra svaśaktyā pūjayedbudhaḥ \|*  
*pādaśaucāsanaprahvasvāgatoktyā ca pūjanam \|*  
*tataścānnapradānena śayanena ca parthiva \|\| 107 \|\|*

The sin of turning back a guest arriving after sunset is eight times greater than that of refusing one who comes in daylight, sire. 107

## \|\| 3.11.108 \|\|

*divātithau tu vimukhe gate yatpātakaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*tadevāṣṭaguṇaṃpuṃsaḥsūryoḍhe vimukhe gate \|\| 108 \|\|*

One should therefore honour guests who come at night with every effort, sire, as when such guests are welcomed, all the gods are honoured. 108

## \|\| 3.11.109 \|\|

*tasmātsvaśaktyā rājendra sūryoḍhamatithiṃ naraḥ \|*  
*pūjayetpūjite tasminpūjitāḥsarvadevatā \|\| 109 \|\|*

One should try to please a guest as much as possible by offering rice, vegetables, water, a bed, a mattress or just a place on the floor. 109

## \|\| 3.11.110 \|\|

*annaśākāṃbudānena svaśaktyā pūjayetpumān \|*  
*śayanaprastaramahīpradānair athavāpi tam \|\| 110 \|\|*

Night: Marital relations

In the evening, when he has washed his feet and all his body, and has finished dinner, sire, the householder should retire to a sturdy wooden bed. 110

## \|\| 3.11.111 \|\|

*sṛtapādādiśaucastu bhuktvā sāyaṃ tato guhī \|*  
*gacchecchayyāmaṃsphuṭitāmapi dārumayīṃ nṛpa \|\| 111 \|\|*

He shouldn’t sleep on one that’s narrow, broken, uneven, dirty, bug-infested or lacks a bedspread. 111

## \|\| 3.11.112 \|\|

*nāviśālaṃ na vai bhugnāṃ nāsamāṃ malināṃ na ca \|*  
*naca jantumayīṃśayyāmadhitiṣṭhedanāstṛtām \|\| 112 \|\|*

They recommend that he should sleep with his head to the east or south, sire, as any other direction is unhealthy. 112

## \|\| 3.11.113 \|\|

*prācyāṃ diśi śiraḥ śastaṃ yāmyāyāmatha vā nṛpa \|*  
*sadaiva svapataḥ puṃso viparītaṃ tu rogadam \|\| 113 \|\|*

It’s also recommended that a man approach his wife at the proper season, sire, under an asterism with a masculine name, at an auspicious hour and on even nights. 113

## \|\| 3.11.114 \|\|

*ṛtāvupagamaḥ śastaḥsvapatnyāmavanīpate \|*  
*punnāmarkṣe śubhe kāle jyeṣṭhāyugmāsu rātriṣu \|\| 114 \|\|*

One should not approach a woman who hasn’t washed or is unwell, menstruating, unattractive, angry, unsuitable, pregnant, 114

## \|\| 3.11.115 \|\|

*nādyunāṃ tu striyaṃ gacchennāturāṃ na rajasvalām \|*  
*nāniṣṭāṃ na prakupitāṃ na trastāṃ na ca gārbhiṇīm \|\| 115 \|\|*

Unskilled, has another lover, is not affectionate, is the wife of another man, is hungry or has overeaten. He himself should have these qualities: 115

## \|\| 3.11.116 \|\|

*nādakṣiṇāṃ nānyakāmāṃ nākāmāṃ nānyayoṣitam \|*  
*kṣutkṣāmāṃ nātibhuktāṃ vā svayaṃ caibhir gurṇair yutaḥ \|\| 116 \|\|*

Having bathed, and wearing garlands and some fragrance, he should show affection, being neither full nor hungry, and approach intercourse with love and passion. 116

## \|\| 3.11.117 \|\|

*snātaḥsraggandhadhṛkprīto nādhmātaḥ kṣudhitopi vā \|*  
*sakāmaḥsānurāgaś ca vyavāyaṃ puruṣo vrajet \|\| 117 \|\|*

On the eighth and fourteenth days of the lunar month, and on days of the new and full moon, sire, and when the sun first enters a sign of the zodiac— 117

## \|\| 3.11.118 \|\|

*caturdaśyaṣṭamī caiva tathā mācātha pūrṇimā \|*  
*parvāṇyetāni rājandra ravīsaṃkrāntireva ca \|\| 118 \|\|*

A person who enjoys oil, women or meat on these days proceeds after death to a hell realm where he’ll feed on excrement and urine. 118

## \|\| 3.11.119 \|\|

*tailastrīmāsasaṃbhogī sarvaiṃṣveteṣu vai pumān \|*  
*viṇmūtrabhojanaṃ nāma prayāti narakaṃ mṛtaḥ \|\| 119 \|\|*

On all such days, therefore, wise, self-restrained men devote themselves to holy scripture, divine sacrifice, meditation and prayer. 119

## \|\| 3.11.120 \|\|

*aśeṣaparvasveteṣu tasmātsaṃyamibhir budhaiḥ \|*  
*bhāvyaṃ sacchāstradevejyādhyānajapyaparair naraiḥ \|\| 120 \|\|*

There should be no intercourse in a vagina other than a woman’s, or in an orifice other than a vagina, or under the influence of drugs, or in the home of a deity, brahmin or guru, 120

## \|\| 3.11.121 \|\|

*nānyayonāv ayonau vā nopayuktauṣadhas tathā \|*  
*dvijadevagurūṇāṃ ca vyavāyī nāśrame bhavet \|\| 121 \|\|*

Or at sacred sites, public squares, riverbanks, assembly rooms, crossroads, cremation grounds or in groves or water, sire. 121

## \|\| 3.11.122 \|\|

*caityacatvaratīreṣu naiva goṣṭhe catuṣpathe \|*  
*naiva śmaśānopavane salileṣu mahīpate \|\| 122 \|\|*

A wise person should avoid intercourse during all the days I mentioned and during twilight, sire, or he’ll be punished with urine and faeces in the next world. 122

## \|\| 3.11.123 \|\|

*proktaparvasvaśeṣeṣu naiva bhūpāla saṃdhyayoḥ \|*  
*gacchevdyavāyaṃ manasā na mūtroccārapīḍitaḥ \|\| 123 \|\|*

Intercourse at any of these times is unproductive, and during the day it’s simply wrong, your majesty. Sex on the ground makes people sick, and sex in the water is not recommended either. 123

## \|\| 3.11.124 \|\|

*parvasvabhigamonindyo divā pāpaprado nṛpa \|*  
*bhuvi rogāvaho nṝṇāmapraśasto jalāśaye \|\| 124 \|\|*

One should never approach another man’s wife for intercourse, or even contemplate it, let alone discuss it with her, as this will weaken his bones and joints. 124

## \|\| 3.11.125 \|\|

*paradārānna gaccheta manasāpi pathañcana \|*  
*kimu vācāsthibandhopi nāsti teṣu vyavāyinām \|\| 125 \|\|*

One who has enjoyed the wife of another man will be punished in both worlds: in this, his life is short, and in the next, he’ll go to hell. 125

## \|\| 3.11.126 \|\|

*mṛto narakamabhyeti hīyate trāpi cāyuṣaḥ \|*  
*paradāraratiḥ puṃsāmiha cāmutra bhītidā \|\| 126 \|\|*

Bearing all this in mind, a wise man may approach his wife in season, when she is free from all the states I’ve mentioned and if she’s in the mood, even if the timing is not exactly right. 126

## Chapter 12 - The conduct of the wise

## \|\| 3.12.1 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*devagobrāhmaṇāntsiddhānvṛddhācāryaṃ stathārcayet \|*  
*dvikālaṃ ca nametsaṃdhyāmagnīnupacarettathā \|\| 1 \|\|*

The householder should worship gods, cattle, brahmins, holy men, the elderly and his teachers. Twice a day he should perform the sandhyā rituals and fire sacrifices. 1

## \|\| 3.12.2 \|\|

*sadānupahate vastre praśastāś ca mahoṣadhīḥ \|*  
*gāruḍāni ca ratnāni bibhṛyātprayato naraḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

A prudent person should always wear garments that have no holes, take medicine as recommended and carry gemstones for protection. 2

## \|\| 3.12.3 \|\|

*prasnigdhāmalakeśaś ca sugandhaścāruveṣadhṛk \|*  
*sitāḥ sumanaso hṛdyā vibhṛyācca naraḥ sadā \|\| 3 \|\|*

With clean and freshly pomaded hair, a man should always wear a pleasant scent and dress in tasteful garments with attractive garlands of white flowers. 3

## \|\| 3.12.4 \|\|

*kiñcitparasvaṃ na harennalpamapyapriyaṃ vadet \|*  
*priyaṃ ca nānṛtaṃ brūyānnānyadoṣānudīrayet \|\| 4 \|\|*

He should never take another’s property, or make the slightest harsh remark, but should speak politely and truthfully, without mentioning another person’s faults. 4

## \|\| 3.12.5 \|\|

*nānyastriyaṃ tathā vairaṃ rocayetpuruṣarṣabha \|*  
*na duṣṭaṃ yānamārohetkūlacchāyāṃ na saṃśrayet \|\| 5 \|\|*

He shouldn’t covet another man’s wife, nor seek enmity with him, sire, nor should he ride in an unsafe vehicle or rest in the shade of an overhanging cliff. 5

## \|\| 3.12.6 \|\|

*vidviṣṭapatitonmattabahuvair ādikīṭakaiḥ \|*  
*vardhakībandhakībhartuḥ kṣudrānṛtakathaiḥ saha \|\| 6 \|\|*

Those who are odious, wicked, drunk, aggressive or louse-infested, prostitutes and their clients, the lowly, liars, wastrels, slanderers and cheats— 6

## \|\| 3.12.7 \|\|

*tathātivyayaśīlaiś ca parivādarataiḥ śaṭhaiḥ \|*  
*budho maitrīṃ na kurvāta naikaḥ panthānamāśrayet \|\| 7 \|\|*

A wise person should befriend none of these, nor should he travel with them alone. 7

## \|\| 3.12.8 \|\|

*nāvagāhejjaloghasya vegamagre napeśvara \|*  
*pradīptaṃveśma na viśennārohecchikharaṃ taroḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

He should never dive into a fast-flowing river, sire, or enter a burning house or climb to the top of a tree. 8

## \|\| 3.12.9 \|\|

*na kuryāddantasaṃgharṣaṃ kuṣṇiyācca na nāsikām \|*  
*nāsaṃvṛtamukho jṛṃbhecchvāsakāso visarjayet \|\| 9 \|\|*

He shouldn’t grind his teeth, pick his nose or yawn without covering his mouth, and he should try not to sigh or cough. 9

## \|\| 3.12.10 \|\|

*noccair haset sa śabdaṃ ca na muñcet pavanaṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*nakhānna khādayecchindyānna tṛmaṃ na mahīṃ likhet \|\| 10 \|\|*

A wise person shouldn’t laugh raucously, break wind loudly, bite his nails, snap off stalks of grass or scratch the ground. 10

## \|\| 3.12.11 \|\|

*na śmaśru bhakṣayelloṣṭaṃ na mṛdrīyādvicakṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*jyotīṃṣyamedhyaśastāni nābhivīkṣeta ca prabho \|\| 11 \|\|*

A wise man doesn’t chew his beard or crumble clods of earth. He doesn’t look at auspicious heavenly bodies when he’s impure, master, 11

## \|\| 3.12.12 \|\|

*nagnāṃ parastriyaṃ caiva sūryaṃ cāstamayodaye \|*  
*na huṅkuryācchavaṃ gandhaṃ śavāgandhohi somaja \|\| 12 \|\|*

Or at another man’s wife when she is naked, or at the setting sun. He shouldn’t express disgust when he sees a corpse, because the odour of the dead arises from the moon. 12

## \|\| 3.12.13 \|\|

*catuṣpathaṃ caityataruṃ śmaśānopavanāni ca \|*  
*duṣṭastrīsannikarṣaṃ ca varjayennaśi sarvadā \|\| 13 \|\|*

At night, he should always avoid crossroads, sacred trees, groves near cremation grounds and the company of immoral women. 13

## \|\| 3.12.14 \|\|

*pūjyadevadvijajyotiśchāyāṃ nātikramed budhaḥ \|*  
*naikaḥ śūnyāṭavīṃ gacchettathā śūnyagṛhe vaset \|\| 14 \|\|*

A wise man never oversteps the shadow of a sacred object, a deity, a brahmin or a heavenly body, nor should he enter a lonely forest by himself or sleep in a vacant house. 14

## \|\| 3.12.15 \|\|

*keśāsthikaṇṭakāmedhyabalibhasmatuṣāṃs tathā \|*  
*snānārdradharaṇīṃ caiva dūrataḥ parivarjayet \|\| 15 \|\|*

He should avoid hair, bones, thorns, filth, food offerings, ashes, chaff and places splashed with other people’s bathwater. 15

## \|\| 3.12.16 \|\|

*nānāryānāśrayetkāścinna jihyaṃ rocayedbudhaḥ \|*  
*upasarpenna vai vyālaṃ ciraṃ tiṣṭhenna votthitaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

A wise person should never seek refuge with unworthy folk or amuse himself with rogues. He shouldn’t approach wild animals, nor should he dawdle when he rises in the morning. 16

## \|\| 3.12.17 \|\|

*atīva jāgarasvapne tadvatsthānāsane budhaḥ \|*  
*na seveta tathā śayyāṃ vyāyāmaṃ ca nareśvara \|\| 17 \|\|*

A prudent person wastes no time when waking up or turning in, or when standing, sitting, lying down or working, sire. 17

## \|\| 3.12.18 \|\|

*daṃṣṭriṇaḥ śṛṅgiṇaścaiva prājño dūreṇa varjayet \|*  
*avaśyāyaṃ ca rājendra purovātātapau tathā \|\| 18 \|\|*

He should keep clear of animals with tusks and horns, your majesty, and avoid frosts, thunderstorms and heat. 18

## \|\| 3.12.19 \|\|

*na snāyānna svapennagno na caivopaspṛśedbudhaḥ \|*  
*muktakeśaś ca nācāmeddevādyarcāṃ ca varjayet \|\| 19 \|\|*

A wise man never bathes or sleeps or drinks water while he’s naked, nor should he drink anything or worship deities if the top of his lower garment is loose. 19

## \|\| 3.12.20 \|\|

*homadevārcanādyāsu kriyāsvācamane tathā \|*  
*naikavastraḥ pravarteta dvijapādāvanejane \|\| 20 \|\|*

He shouldn’t perform a fire sacrifice, worship the deities, undertake any other ritual, rinse his mouth, make a speech or recite a prayer while wearing just a single garment. 20

## \|\| 3.12.21 \|\|

*nāsamañjasaśīlais tu sahāsīta kathañcana \|*  
*sadbuttasannikarṣo hi kṣaṇārdhamapi śasyate \|\| 21 \|\|*

He should never sit with those who misbehave; decent people shouldn’t spend as much as half a moment in such company. 21

## \|\| 3.12.22 \|\|

*virodhaṃ nottamair gacchennādhamaiś ca sadā budhaḥ \|*  
*vivāhaś ca vivādaś ca tulyaśīlair nṛpeṣyate \|\| 22 \|\|*

A wise man never quarrels with superiors or inferiors, sire—marriages and arguments are best conducted between equals. 22

## \|\| 3.12.23 \|\|

*nārabheta kalaṃ prājñaḥ śuṣkavairaṃ ca varjayet \|*  
*apyalpahāniḥsoḍhavyā vaireṇārthāgamantyajet \|\| 23 \|\|*

A wise man shouldn’t start an argument but avoid pointless hostility; a little loss may be endured, but the wise reject the wealth that’s won with aggravation. 23

## \|\| 3.12.24 \|\|

*snāto nāṅgāni saṃmārjetsnānaśāṭyā na pāṇinā \|*  
*na ca nirdhūtayetkeśānnācāmeccaiva cotthitaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

After bathing, a person shouldn’t wipe his limbs with a towel or with his hands or shake his hair or rinse his mouth before he’s standing. 24

## \|\| 3.12.25 \|\|

*pādena nākrametpādaṃ na pūjyābimukhaṃ nayet \|*  
*noccāsanaṃ guroragre bhajetāvinayānvitaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

He shouldn’t sit cross-legged in the presence of important people or stretch his legs towards them. He should kneel with modesty when he’s with his guru. 25

## \|\| 3.12.26 \|\|

*apasavyaṃ na gacchecca devāgāracatuṣpathān \|*  
*māṅgalyapūjyāṃś ca tathā viparītānna dakṣiṇam \|\| 26 \|\|*

He shouldn’t pass auspicious or holy places or crossroads on the left, nor should he pass inauspicious ones on the right. 26

## \|\| 3.12.27 \|\|

*somārkāgnyaṃbuvāyūnāṃ pūjyānāṃ ca na saṃmukham \|*  
*kuryānniṣṭhīvaviṇmutrasamutsargaṃ ca paṇḍitaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

An educated person does not spit, defecate or urinate while facing the moon, fire, the sun, water, the wind or important people. 27

## \|\| 3.12.28 \|\|

*tiṣṭhanna mūtrayettadvatpathiṣvapi na mūtrayet \|*  
*śleṣmaviṇmūtraraktāni sarvadaiva na laṅghayet \|\| 28 \|\|*

He shouldn’t urinate while standing on a road, nor should he ever step over mucus, excrement, blood or urine. 28

## \|\| 3.12.29 \|\|

*śleṣmaśiṅghāṇikotsargau nānnakāle praśasyate \|*  
*balimaṅgalajapyādau na home na mahājane \|\| 29 \|\|*

Clearing the throat and blowing the nose are not recommended at mealtimes, when making food offerings, during other auspicious rituals, prayers or fire sacrifices or in the presence of important people. 29

## \|\| 3.12.30 \|\|

*yopito nāvamanyeta na cāsāṃ viśvasedbudhaḥ \|*  
*na caiverṣyā bhavettāsu na dhikkuryātkadācana \|\| 30 \|\|*

A prudent man should never disrespect women, but neither should he trust them. He shouldn’t be impatient with them, but neither should he grant them authority. 30

## \|\| 3.12.31 \|\|

*maṅgalyapūrvaratnājyapūjyānanabhivādya ca \|*  
*na niṣkramedgṛhātprājñaḥsadācāraparo naraḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

A wise person who is attentive to correct conduct shouldn’t leave his house, your majesty, without respectfully acknowledging the sacred images, flowers, gems, sacrificial butter and venerable persons inside it. 31

## \|\| 3.12.32 \|\|

*catuṣpathānnamaskuryātkāle homaparo bhavet \|*  
*dīnānabhyuddharetsādhūnupāsīta bahuśrutān \|\| 32 \|\|*

He should be circumspect at crossroads and attentive towards sacrifices at the proper season. He should assist virtuous people in distress and seek the company of the learned. 32

## \|\| 3.12.33 \|\|

*devarṣipūjakaḥsamyakpitṛpiṇḍojakapradaḥ \|*  
*satkartā cātithīnāṃ yaḥ sa lokānuttamānvrajet \|\| 33 \|\|*

One who venerates the deities and sages with devotion, who offers food and water to the spirits, and who welcomes guests hospitably, may reach the highest realms. 33

## \|\| 3.12.34 \|\|

*hitaṃ mitaṃ priyaṃ kāle vaśyātmā yobhibhāṣate \|*  
*sa yāti lokānāhlādahetubhūtān nṛpākṣayān \|\| 34 \|\|*

A restrained individual whose speech is beneficial, measured, kind and timely, sire, will reach those worlds that are the source of everlasting happiness. 34

## \|\| 3.12.35 \|\|

*dhīmānhrīpānkṣamāyukto hyāstiko vinayānvitaḥ \|*  
*vidyābhijanavṛddhānāṃ yāti lokānanuttamān \|\| 35 \|\|*

One who is intelligent, modest, patient, devout and polite, and who respects wisdom, worthy people and his elders, will proceed to peerless realms. 35

## \|\| 3.12.36 \|\|

*akālagārjitādau ca parvasvāśaucakādiṣu \|*  
*anadhyāyaṃ budhaḥ kuryāduparāgādike tathā \|\| 36 \|\|*

A wise man shouldn’t study Vedas when there is unseasonal thunder or anything inauspicious, or on days when he’s impure or during an eclipse. 36

## \|\| 3.12.37 \|\|

*samaṃ nayati yaḥ krudvānsarvabandhuramatsarī \|*  
*bhītāśvāsanakṛtsādhuḥsvargastasyālpakaṃ phalam \|\| 37 \|\|*

Heaven is the least reward for that good man who calms the angry, who treats each person as his kin, who is selfless and who reassures the timid. 37

## \|\| 3.12.38 \|\|

*varṣātapādiṣu cchatrī daṇḍī rātryaṭavīṣu ca \|*  
*śarīratrāṇakāmo vai sopānatkaḥsadā vrajet \|\| 38 \|\|*

One concerned for his own welfare should carry an umbrella in rain or shine, a stick at night or in a forest and should always wear sandals. 38

## \|\| 3.12.39 \|\|

*nordhvaṃ na tiryagdūraṃ vā na paśyanparyaṭedbudhaḥ \|*  
*yugamātraṃ mahīṣṭaṣṭhaṃ naro gacchedvilokayan \|\| 39 \|\|*

A wise man shouldn’t look up, to either side or in the distance when walking, but should keep his eyes on the ground two paces in front of him. 39

## \|\| 3.12.40 \|\|

*doṣahetūnaśeṣāṃś ca vaśyātmā yo nirasyati \|*  
*tasya dharmārthakāmānāṃ hānirnālpāpi jāyate \|\| 40 \|\|*

The restrained man who shuns each cause of wickedness will never know the slightest diminution of virtue, wealth or love. 40

## \|\| 3.12.41 \|\|

*sadācārarataḥ prājño vidyāvinayaśikṣitaḥ \|*  
*pāpepyapāpaḥ paruṣe hyabhidhatte priyāṇi yaḥ \|*  
*maitrīdravāntaḥ karaṇastasya muktiḥ kare sthitā \|\| 41 \|\|*

Sinless in the presence of the sinful, a kindly word upon his lips, a heart that’s filled with benevolence—such a man holds liberation in the palm of his hand. 41

## \|\| 3.12.42 \|\|

*ye kāmakrodhalobhānāṃ vītarāgā na gocare \|*  
*sadācārasthitāsteṣāmanubhāvair dhṛtā mahī \|\| 42 \|\|*

The world is upheld by the dignity of those who are always free from passion, beyond the reach of lust and greed and anger and firmly set on perfect conduct. 42

## \|\| 3.12.43 \|\|

*tasmātsatyaṃ vadetprājño yatparaprītikāraṇam \|*  
*satyaṃ yatparaduḥkhāya tadā maunaparo bhavet \|\| 43 \|\|*

A wise person, therefore, speaks the truth when it’s pleasing to another, but when the truth is painful, he stays silent. 43

## \|\| 3.12.44 \|\|

*prāṇīnāmupakārāya yathaiveha paratra ca \|*  
*karmaṇā manasā vācā tadeva matīmānvadet \|\| 44 \|\|*

He shouldn’t speak if he thinks his words are merely pleasant but unhelpful. It’s better to offer something beneficial even if it’s hard to hear. 44

## \|\| 3.12.45 \|\|

*sanskrit not found \|\| 45 \|\|*

A wise man strives with body, voice and mind for the benefit of living beings in this world and the next. 45

## Chapter 13 - Rituals relating to birth and death

## \|\| 3.13.1 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*sacailasya pituḥ snānaṃ jāte putre vidhīyate \|*  
*jātakarma tadā kuryācchrāddhamabhyudaye ca yat \|\| 1 \|\|*

When a son is born, it’s recommended that a father bathe himself fully clothed, after which he should undertake those rituals pertaining to the birth and a śrāddha befitting the occasion. 1

## \|\| 3.13.2 \|\|

*yugmāndevāṃś ca vitryāṃś ca samyaksavyakramāddvijān \|*  
*pūjāyedbhojayeccaiva tanmanā nānyamānasaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

After he has respectfully circumambulated the deities associated with even days and the ancestral spirits, he should honour brahmins and offer food with his mind on this and nothing else. 2

## \|\| 3.13.3 \|\|

*dadhyakṣataiḥ sabadaraiḥ prāṅmukhodaṅmukho 'pi vā \|*  
*devatīrthena vai piṇḍāndadyātkāyena vā nṛpa \|\| 3 \|\|*

Facing east or north, he should offer food such as yoghurt, whole grains of barley and dates with that part of his hand or body sacred to each deity, your majesty. 3

## \|\| 3.13.4 \|\|

*nāndīmukhaḥ pitṛgaṇas tena śrāddhena pārthiva \|*  
*prīyate tattu kartavyaṃ puruṣaiḥ sarvavṛddhiṣu \|\| 4 \|\|*

As the host of ancestral spirits known as the Nāndīmukhas are propitiated by this śrāddha, sire, it should be performed by people at all such happy times. 4

## \|\| 3.13.5 \|\|

*kanyāputravivāheṣu praveśeṣu ca veśmanaḥ \|*  
*nāmakarmaṇi bālānāṃ cūḍākarmādike tathā \|\| 5 \|\|*

At the marriage of a son or daughter, on entering a new house, at the naming ceremony of a child, at the first cutting of the hair and so on, 5

## \|\| 3.13.6 \|\|

*sīmantonnayane caiva putrādimukhadarśane \|*  
*nāndīmukhaṃ pitṛgaṇaṃ pūjayetprayato gṛhī \|\| 6 \|\|*

At the parting of a pregnant woman’s hair and at the first showing of a baby’s face, the faithful householder should venerate the host of Nāndīmukhas. 6

## \|\| 3.13.7 \|\|

*pitṛpūjākramaḥ prokto vṛddhāveṣa sanātanaḥ \|*  
*śrūyatāmavanīpāla preta karma kriyāvidhiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

I’ve described in brief a set of rituals for the ancestors to be conducted on occasions marked by joy. Now you’ll hear about the rules for undertaking funerals, your majesty. 7

## \|\| 3.13.8 \|\|

*pretadehaṃ śabhaiḥ snānaiḥ snāpitaṃ sragvibhūṣitam \|*  
*dagdhvā grāmādbahiḥ snātvā sacailaḥsalilāśaye \|\| 8 \|\|*

After the corpse has been washed with fresh water and garlanded with flowers, it’s cremated outside the village. The relatives of the deceased should immerse themselves in water fully dressed. 8

## \|\| 3.13.9 \|\|

*yatra tatra sthitāyaitadamukāyeti vādinaḥ \|*  
*dakṣiṇābhimukhā dadyurbāndhavāḥsalilāñjalīn \|\| 9 \|\|*

Facing south, they offer water in cupped hands, saying, ‘This is for you, wherever you are.’ 9

## \|\| 3.13.10 \|\|

*praviṣṭāś ca samaṃ gobhir grāmaṃ nakṣatradarśane \|*  
*kaṭakarma tataḥ kuryād bhūmau prastaraśāyinaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Entering the village with the cows when the stars begin to shine, they should sleep on straw mats spread on the floor. 10

## \|\| 3.13.11 \|\|

*dātavyonadinaṃ piḍaḥ pretāya bhuvi pārthiva \|*  
*divā ca bhaktaṃ bhoktavyamamāṃsaṃ manujarṣabha \|\| 11 \|\|*

Every day, they should place food on the ground as an offering for the deceased, your majesty. They may eat boiled rice during the day, but no meat, you bull among men. 11

## \|\| 3.13.12 \|\|

*dināni tāni cecchātaḥ kartavya viprabhojanam \|*  
*pretā yānti tathā tṛptiṃ bandhuvargeṇa bhuñjatā \|\| 12 \|\|*

Brahmins are to be fed for as many days as the mourner sees fit, for the deceased person is also nourished when his kin are dining. 12

## \|\| 3.13.13 \|\|

*prathamehni tṛtīye ca saptame navame tathā \|*  
*vastratyāgabahiḥsnāne kṛtvā dadyāt tilodakam \|\| 13 \|\|*

On the first, third, seventh and ninth days, having changed his clothes and washed himself outdoors, he should make an offering of sesame seeds and water. 13

## \|\| 3.13.14 \|\|

*caturthehnica kartavyaṃ tasyāsthicayanaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*tadūrdhvamaṅgasaṃsparśaḥsapiṇḍānāmapīṣyate \|\| 14 \|\|*

On the fourth day, the bones and ashes should be gathered, sire, after which the bodies of those who make offerings of food may be touched again. 14

## \|\| 3.13.15 \|\|

*yogyāḥsarvakriyāṇāṃ tu samanasalilās tathā \|*  
*anulepanapuṣpādibhogād anyatra pārthiva \|\| 15 \|\|*

Those who made offerings of water are also able to resume all their usual activities, but should refrain from luxuries, such as garlands and body lotions, sire. 15

## \|\| 3.13.16 \|\|

*śayyāsanopabhogaś ca sapiṇḍānāmapīṣyate \|*  
*bhasmāsthicayanādūrdhvaṃ saṃyogo na tu yoṣitām \|\| 16 \|\|*

Those who made food offerings are permitted to use beds and seats after the deceased’s ashes and bones have been gathered, but sexual intercourse with women is forbidden. 16

## \|\| 3.13.17 \|\|

*bāle deśāntarasthe ca patite ca munau mṛte \|*  
*sadyaḥ śaucaṃ tathecchāto jalāgnyudbandhanādiṣu \|\| 17 \|\|*

If the deceased is a child, or in another place, or is a fallen person or a sage, purity returns more quickly, and the rituals involving water, fire and so on are discretionary. 17

## \|\| 3.13.18 \|\|

*mṛtabandhordaśāhāni kulasyānnaṃ na bhujyate \|*  
*dānaṃ pratigraho homaḥ svādhyāyaś ca nivartate \|\| 18 \|\|*

The food of a family whose relative has died may not be eaten for ten days, and the giving and receiving of gifts, sacrifice and Vedic studies are suspended. 18

## \|\| 3.13.19 \|\|

*viprasyaitaddvādaśāhaṃ rājanyasyāpyaśaucakam \|*  
*ardhamāsaṃ tu vaiśyasya māsaṃ śūdrasya śuddhaye \|\| 19 \|\|*

Such are the rules when the deceased is a brahmin. For a kṣatriya, impurity persists for twelve days; for a vaiśya, two weeks; but for a śūdra, purity returns only one month later. 19

## \|\| 3.13.20 \|\|

*ayujo bhojayetkāmaṃ dvijānante tato dine \|*  
*dadyāddarbhaṣu piṇḍaṃ ca pretāyocchiṣṭasannidhau \|\| 20 \|\|*

One might choose to feed the brahmins on odd days, and on each following day, one should place an offering of food on a bed of kuśa-grass for the spirit of the deceased, along with any leftovers. 20

## \|\| 3.13.21 \|\|

*vāryāyudhapratodāstu daṇḍaś ca dvijabhojanāt \|*  
*spraṣṭavyonantaraṃ varṇaiḥ śuddherate tataḥ kramāt \|\| 21 \|\|*

As soon as a man has fed the brahmins, he should touch some water, a weapon, a goad or stick, depending on his community, as he’ll be purified by doing so. 21

## \|\| 3.13.22 \|\|

*tataḥsarvaṇadharmā ye viprādināsudāhṛtāḥ \|*  
*tānkurvīta pumāñjīvennijadharmārjanais tathā \|\| 22 \|\|*

After this, a man may resume his occupation as befits the brahmin order and the others, so he might live on earnings from a fitting source. 22

## \|\| 3.13.23 \|\|

*mṛtāhati ca kartavyamekoddiṣṭamataḥ param \|*  
*āhvānādikriyā daivaniyogarahitena yat \|\| 23 \|\|*

In every month that follows, the ekodiṣṭa ceremony should be undertaken on the date of death, but rituals such as invocation of the deities are unnecessary. 23

## \|\| 3.13.24 \|\|

*ekordhyastatra dātavyastathaivaikapavitrakam \|*  
*pretāya piṇḍo dātavyo bhuktavatsu dvijātiṣu \|\| 24 \|\|*

A single offering of water should be made on these occasions to purify one person. A food offering should be made for the deceased, while brahmins take a meal. 24

## \|\| 3.13.25 \|\|

*praśnaś ca tatrābhiratir yajamānadvijanmanām \|*  
*akṣayyamamukasyeti vaktavyaṃ viratau tathā \|\| 25 \|\|*

The brahmins who perform the sacrifice are to be asked if they are satisfied, and, if they are, then the words, ‘May he also be eternally so’, should be addressed to the deceased. 25

## \|\| 3.13.26 \|\|

*ekoddiṣṭamayo dharma itthamāvatsarātsmṛtaḥ \|*  
*sapiṇḍīkaraṇaṃ tasminkāle rājendra catchṛṇu \|\| 26 \|\|*

The practice known as ekodiṣṭa takes place for one year after death, and an offering of food is made on such occasions. Now you’ll hear some more about this, sire. 26

## \|\| 3.13.27 \|\|

*ekoddiṣṭavidhānena kāryaṃ tadapi pārthiva \|*  
*saṃvatsaretha ṣaṣṭhe vā māse vā dvādaśehni tat \|\| 27 \|\|*

In the performance of this ritual, a set of four vessels filled with sesame seeds, fragrances and water is used, your majesty. 27

## \|\| 3.13.28 \|\|

*tilagandhodakair yuktaṃ tatra pātracatuṣṭayam \|\| 28 \|\|*

One is for the deceased, while the others are for the ancestors. The contents of the first are poured into the other three. 28

## \|\| 3.13.29 \|\|

*pātraṃ pretasya tatraikaṃ paitraṃ pātratrayaṃ tathā \|*  
*secayetpitṛpātreṣu pretapātraṃ tatastriṣu \|\| 29 \|\|*

When the deceased has reached the status of an ancestor by this process, sire, one should venerate the old ancestral spirits with all the śrāddha rituals. 29

## \|\| 3.13.30 \|\|

*tataḥ pitṛtvamāpanne tasminprete mahīpate \|*  
*śrāddhadharmair aśaṣastu tatpūrvānarcayetpitṝn \|\| 30 \|\|*

A son, grandson, great-grandson, other relative, a brother’s descendant or a descendant related through participation in a food-offering ritual are all entitled to perform these funerary rites, your majesty. 30

## \|\| 3.13.31 \|\|

*putraḥ pautraḥ prapautro vā bhrātā vā bhrātṛsaṃtatiḥ \|*  
*sapiṇḍa saṃtatirvāpi kriyārhe nṛpa jāyate \|\| 31 \|\|*

In the absence of all these, a descendant related through shared participation in a water offering, or a relative connected through a food or water offering on the mother’s side, may officiate. 31

## \|\| 3.13.32 \|\|

*teṣāmabhāve savaiṣāṃ samānodakasatatiḥ \|*  
*mātṛpakṣamapiṇḍena saṃbandhā ye jalena vā \|\| 32 \|\|*

If the male line is extinct on both sides, these rituals may be performed by women, sire. The rites may even be performed by the deceased’s acquaintances. Further, the king himself may cause the rites to be performed with wealth left by a person without heirs. 32

## \|\| 3.13.33 \|\|

*kuladvayepi cocchinne strībhiḥ kāryāḥ kriyā nṛpa \|*  
*pitṛmātṛsapiṇḍais tu samānasalilais tathā \|\| 33 \|\|*

Funerary rites are of three kinds: initial, medial and final. Now you’ll hear from me about the differences among them. 33

## \|\| 3.13.34 \|\|

*saṃghātāntargatair vāpi kāryāḥ pretasya ca kriyāḥ \|*  
*utsannabandhurikthādvā kārayedavanīpatiḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

The initial rites are those performed after the cremation and the touching of water, a weapon and so on. The ekodiṣṭa rituals performed in months that follow are known as medial rites. 34

## \|\| 3.13.35 \|\|

*pūrvāḥ kriyā madhyamāś ca tathā caivottarāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*triḥprakārāḥ kriyāḥsarvāstāsāṃbhedaṃ śṛṇuṣva me \|\| 35 \|\|*

The rituals after the food offering for the deceased, during which he acquires the status of an ancestral spirit, are called the final rites, your majesty. 35

## \|\| 3.13.36 \|\|

*ādāhādyādaśāhāś ca sparśādyantāstu yāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*tāḥ pūrvā madhyamā māsi māsyekoddiṣṭasaṃjñitāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

The initial rites should be performed by those connected through a food or water offering on the father’s or mother’s side, or by the deceased’s acquaintances or by the king when he has taken over the property of the deceased. 36

## \|\| 3.13.37 \|\|

*prete pitṛtvamāpanne sapiṇḍikaraṇādanu \|*  
*kriyante yāḥ kriyāḥ pitryāḥ procyante tā nṛpottarāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

The final rites are performed by the deceased’s son or his descendants, the deceased’s daughter’s sons or by their descendants, sire. 37

## \|\| 3.13.38 \|\|

*pitṛmātṛsapiṇḍais tu samānāsalilais tathā \|*  
*saṃghātāntargatair vāpi rājñā taddhanahāriṇā \|\| 38 \|\|*

The final rite of ekodiṣṭa should also be performed each year by women on the anniversary of the death, your majesty. 38

## \|\| 3.13.39 \|\|

*pūrvāḥ kriyāś ca kartavyāḥ putrādyair eva cottarāḥ \|*  
*dauhitrair vā nṛpaśreṣṭha kāryāstattanayais tathā \|\| 39 \|\|*

For this reason, you should hear about these final rites, sire, the times at which they are performed and the procedure to be followed, flawless monarch. 39

## Chapter 14 - The Śrāddha ritual for the ancestral spirits

## \|\| 3.14.1 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*brahmendrarudranāsatyasūryāgnivasumārutān \|*  
*viśvedevānpitṛgaṇānvayāṃsi manujānpaśun \|\| 1 \|\|*

Brahmā, Indra, Rudra, the Aśvins, Sūrya, Agni, the Vasus, Maruts and Viśvadevas, the hosts of sages, humankind, beasts and birds, 1

## \|\| 3.14.2 \|\|

*sarīsṛpānṛṣigaṇānyaccānyadbhūta saṃjñitam \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ śraddhānvitaḥ kurvanprīṇayatyakhilaṃ jagat \|\| 2 \|\|*

Things that creep and crawl, ancestral spirits and all other beings—a faithful individual who performs a śrāddha nourishes this whole world. 2

## \|\| 3.14.3 \|\|

*māsimāsyasite pakṣe pañcadaśyāṃ nareśvara \|*  
*dathāṣṭakāsu kurvīta kāmyānkālāñchṛṇuṣva me \|\| 3 \|\|*

A man should undertake a śrāddha on the fifteenth day of the dark fortnight each month, your majesty, and on the eighth day, or at any other time he wishes. Listen to me: 3

## \|\| 3.14.4 \|\|

*śrāddhārhamāgataṃ dravyaṃ viśiṣṭamatha vā dvijam \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ kurvīta vijñāya vyatīpāte 'yate tathā \|\| 4 \|\|*

A householder should perform this ritual when he feels that an occasion worthy of a śrāddha has arisen, or when a distinguished brahmin arrives. On the day of the new moon, or when the sun crosses the celestial equator, 4

## \|\| 3.14.5 \|\|

*viṣuve cāpi saṃprāpte grahaṇe śaśi sūryayoḥ \|*  
*samasteṣveva bhūpāla rāśiṣvarke ca gacchati \|\| 5 \|\|*

At the equinox, at an eclipse of the sun or moon, when the sun first enters a sign of the zodiac, your majesty, 5

## \|\| 3.14.6 \|\|

*nakṣatragrahapīḍāsu duṣṭasvapnāvalokane \|*  
*icchāśrāddhāni kurvīta navasasyāgame tathā \|\| 6 \|\|*

When an asterism or planet becomes inauspicious, when he has a nightmare or when the grain harvest begins, he may perform a discretionary śrāddha. 6

## \|\| 3.14.7 \|\|
