*amāvāsyā yadā maitraviśākhāsvātiyoginī \|*  
*śrīddhaiḥ pitṛgaṇastṛptiṃ tathāpnotyaṣṭavārṣikīm \|\| 7 \|\|*

The hosts of ancestral spirits are nourished for eight years by a śrāddha performed on the day of the new moon in the lunar mansions of Anurādhā, Viśākhā and Svāti. 7

## \|\| 3.14.8 \|\|

*amavāsyā yadā puṣye raudretharkṣe punarvasau \|*  
*dvādaśābdaṃ tadā tṛptiṃ prayānti piratorcitāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

When the ancestors are honoured on the new-moon day in the mansions of Puṣya, Raudra or Punarvasu, they are sustained for twelve years. 8

## \|\| 3.14.9 \|\|

*vāsavājaikapādarkṣe pitaṇāṃ tṛptimicchatām \|*  
*vāruṇe vāpyamāvāsyā devānāmapi durlabhā \|\| 9 \|\|*

On the day of the new moon in Dhaniṣṭhā, Pūrvabhādrapadā or Śatabhiśak, the ancestral spirits, desiring nourishment, receive as much as even deities rarely do. 9

## \|\| 3.14.10 \|\|

*navasvṛkṣeṣvamāvāsyā yadaiteṣvavanīpate \|*  
*tadā hi tṛptidaṃ śrāddhaṃ pitṝṇāṃ śṛṇu cāparam \|\| 10 \|\|*

Now hear more about the śrāddhas conducted on new-moon days in these nine mansions, and which afford such satisfaction to the ancestors, sire. 10

## \|\| 3.14.11 \|\|

*gītaṃ sanatkumāreṇa yathailāna mahātmane \|*  
*pṛcchate pitṛbhaktāya praśrayāvanātāya ca \|\| 11 \|\|*

Brahmā’s son Sanatkumāra sang this song to great Purūravas, son of Ilā, who, bowing with respect, had asked about the śrāddhas, and who was devoted to his ancestors: 11

## \|\| 3.14.12 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca*  
*vaiśākhamāsasya ca yā tṛtīyā navamyasau kartikaśuklapakṣe \|*  
*nabhasyamāsasya ca kṛṣṇapakṣe trayodaśī pañcadaśī ca māghe \|\| 12 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra:

The third day of Vaiśākha, the ninth in the bright fortnight of Kārttika, the thirteenth in the dark fortnight of Nabhas and the fifteenth day of Māgha— 12

## \|\| 3.14.13 \|\|

*etā yugādyāḥ kathitāḥ purāṇeṣvanantapuṇyāstitha yaścatastraḥ \|*  
*upaplave candramaso raveś ca triṣvaṣṭakāsvapyayanadvaye ca \|\| 13 \|\|*

These four, the Yugādya, or ‘first days of the age’, are said to be endlessly auspicious, your majesty. Eclipses of the sun and moon, the eighth day of the three months of Agrahāyana, Māgha and Phālguna and the two days on which the sun crosses the celestial equator— 13

## \|\| 3.14.14 \|\|

*pānīyamapyatra tilair vimiśraṃ dadyātpitṛbhyaḥ prayato manuṣyaḥ \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ kṛte tena samāsahasraṃ rahasyametatpitaro vadanti \|\| 14 \|\|*

On these occasions, a dutiful man should offer water mixed with sesame to the ancestors. This is equal to a śrāddha of a thousand years, and the ancestors declare this rite is secret. 14

## \|\| 3.14.15 \|\|

*māghe 'siti pañcadaśī kadācidupaiti yogaṃ yadi vāruṇena \|*  
*ṛkṣeṇa kālaḥsa paraḥ pitṝṇāṃ na hyalpapuṇyair nṛpa labhyatesau \|\| 15 \|\|*

Whenever the fifteenth day of the dark fortnight of Māgha comes into conjunction with the constellation Śatabhiṣaj, presided over by Varuṇa, this is the best time for a śrāddha, sire, as the benefit for the ancestors is not inconsiderable. 15

## \|\| 3.14.16 \|\|

*kāle dhaniṣṭhā yadi nāma tasminbhavettu bhūpāla tadā pitṛbhyaḥ \|*  
*dattaṃ jalānnaṃ pradadāti tṛptiṃ varṣāyutaṃ tatkulajair manuṣyaiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

Food and water offered by individuals born at the time of a conjunction with the constellation Dhaniṣṭhā, sire, will satisfy the ancestors of their families for ten thousand years. 16

## \|\| 3.14.17 \|\|

*tatraiva cedbhādrapadānupūrvāḥ kāle yathāvatkriyate pitṛbhyaḥ \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ parāṃ tṛptimupaiti tena yugaṃ sahasraṃ pitaraḥsvapanti \|\| 17 \|\|*

If a śrāddha is performed when the conjunction occurs in the Former Bhādrapadas, the ancestors experience deepest satisfaction, and will be able to repose for an entire age. 17

## \|\| 3.14.18 \|\|

*gaṅgāṃ śatadrūṃ yamunāṃ vipāśāṃ sarasvatīṃ naimiśagomatīṃ vā \|*  
*tatrāvagāhyarcanamādareṇa kṛtvā pitṝṇāṃ duritāni hanti \|\| 18 \|\|*

A person who venerates the ancestors with respect, having first bathed in the Gaṅgā, Śatadrū, Vipāśā, Sarasvatī or the Gomatī at Naimiṣa, casts off all his sins. 18

## \|\| 3.14.19 \|\|

*gāyanti caitatpitaraḥ kadānu harṣādamī tṛptimavāpya bhūyaḥ \|*  
*māghāsitānte śubhatīrthatoyair yāsyāma tṛptiṃ tanayādidattaiḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

The ancestors always sing this song: ‘Having enjoyed a year of unalloyed satisfaction from water offered by our descendants at the end of the dark fortnight in the month of Māgha at a sacred ford, let us be satisfied again.’ 19

## \|\| 3.14.20 \|\|

*cittaṃ ca vittaṃ ca nṛṇāṃ viśuddhaṃ śastaṃ ca kālaḥ kathito vidhiś ca \|*  
*pātraṃ yathoktaṃ paramā ca bhaktirnṛṇāṃ prayacchantyabhivāñchitāni \|\| 20 \|\|*

The abovementioned rites undertaken at an appropriate time with pure devotion on behalf of a suitable recipient will enable everyone to achieve their wishes, unbroken prosperity and all else they might desire. 20

## \|\| 3.14.21 \|\|

*pitṛgītāntataivātra ślokāṃstañchṛṇu pārthiva \|*  
*śrutvā tathaiva bhavatā bhāvyaṃ tatrādṛtātmanā \|\| 21 \|\|*

Now you’ll hear from me the verses sung by the ancestors. Listen carefully and all this will come to pass. 21

## \|\| 3.14.22 \|\|

*api dhanyaḥ kule jāyādasmākaṃ matimānnaraḥ \|*  
*akurvanvittaśāṭhyaṃ yaḥ piṇḍānno nirvapiṣyati \|\| 22 \|\|*

‘The prudent man who undertakes food offerings for us, and doesn’t try to hide his wealth, may be reborn a fortunate being in our own lineage. 22

## \|\| 3.14.23 \|\|

*ratnaṃ vastraṃ mahāyānaṃ sarvabhogādikaṃvasu \|*  
*vibhave sati viprebhyo yosmānuddiśya dāsyati \|\| 23 \|\|*

If a man, when rich, gives wealth in the form of jewels, garments, lands, carriages or other great luxuries to brahmins for our benefit, 23

## \|\| 3.14.24 \|\|

*annena vā yathāśaktyā kālesminbhaktinamradhīḥ \|*  
*bhojayiṣyati viprāgryāṃstanmātravibhavo naraḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Or gives food according to his capacity at such a time with his heart filled with faith and devotion, and if he entertains the foremost brahmins, then he’ll receive such wealth in equal measure. 24

## \|\| 3.14.25 \|\|

*asamarthonnadānasya dhānyamāmaṃ svaśaktitaḥ \|*  
*pradāsyati dvijāgrebhyaḥ svalpālpāṃ vāpi dakṣiṇām \|\| 25 \|\|*

If he cannot offer food, let him give to eminent brahmins as much uncooked grain as he is able, or even a little sacrificial fee. 25

## \|\| 3.14.26 \|\|

*tatrāpyasāmarthyayutaḥ karāgrāgrasthitāṃstilān \|*  
*praṇamya dvijamukhyāya kasmaicidbhūpa dāsyati \|\| 26 \|\|*

If even that lies beyond his means, your majesty, he should bow down before a brahmin and offer him as many sesame seeds as he can hold on the tip of one finger. 26

## \|\| 3.14.27 \|\|

*tilaiḥ saptāṣṭabhir vāpi samavetaṃ jalāñjalim \|*  
*bhaktinamraḥ samuddiśya bhuvyasmākaṃ pradāsyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

Or, bowing with devotion, he may offer us seven or eight sesame seeds on the ground, along with water from his cupped hands. 27

## \|\| 3.14.28 \|\|

*yataḥ kutāścitsaṃprāpya gobhyo vāpi gavāhnikam \|*  
*abhāve prīṇayannasmāñcchraddhāyuktaḥ pradāsyati \|\| 28 \|\|*

If even that’s not possible, the devotee may gather enough fodder for just one day from somewhere, and, bowing to us, filled with piety, he should feed it to a cow. 28

## \|\| 3.14.29 \|\|

*sarvābhāve vanaṃ gatvā kakṣamūlapradarśakaḥ \|*  
*sūryādilokapālānāmidamuccervadiṣyati \|\| 29 \|\|*

If none of these is possible, he must go to the forest and, raising his arms to salute the sun and the guardians of the four directions, he should recite this verse aloud: 29

## \|\| 3.14.30 \|\|

*na me 'sti vittaṃ na dhanaṃ ca nānyacchrāddhopayogyaṃ svapitṝnnatosmi \|*  
*tṛpyantu bhaktayā pitaro mayaitau kṛtau bhujau vartmani mārutasya \|\| 30 \|\|*

“I have no possessions, wealth or anything else with which to express my faith. I bow down to my ancestors. May they be satisfied with these two arms, which I’ve raised with devotion in the path of the wind.”’ 30

## \|\| 3.14.31 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*ity etat pitṛbhir gītaṃ bhāvābhāvaprayojanam \|*  
*yaḥ karoti kṛtaṃ tena śrāddhaṃ bhavati pārthiva \|\| 31 \|\|*

Aurva:

Such is the song the ancestral spirits sang, and which befits times of both wealth and poverty. One who undertakes a śrāddha should follow this prescription, sire. 31

## Chapter 15 - Conducting a Śrāddha

## \|\| 3.15.1 \|\|

*aureva uvāca*  
*brāhmaṇānbhojayecchraddhe yadguṇāṃstānnibhodha me \|\| 1 \|\|*

Now you’ll hear about the ranks of brahmins to be offered food when a śrāddha is conducted: 1

## \|\| 3.15.2 \|\|

*triṇāciketastrimadhustrisuparmaḥṣajaṅgavit \|*  
*vedavicchrotriyo yogī tathā vai jyeṣṭhasāmagaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

One who knows the three Nāciketas, three Madhus or three Suparṇas, the six Vedic supplements or the Vedas themselves; a scholar; an ascetic; one who chants the Jyeṣṭhasāman; 2

## \|\| 3.15.3 \|\|

*ṛtviksvastreyadauhitrajāmātṛśvaśurās tathā \|*  
*mātulotha taponiṣṭhaḥ pañcāgnyabhiratas tathā \|*  
*śiṣyāḥsaṃbandhinaścaiva mātāpitṛrataś ca yaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

An officiating priest; the householder’s sister’s son, daughter’s son, son-in-law, father-in-law or maternal uncle; one who practises austerities or maintains five sacred fires; the householder’s students or relatives; and one who is devoted to his mother and father. 3

## \|\| 3.15.4 \|\|

*etānniyojayecchrāddhe pūrvoktānprathame nṛpa \|*  
*brāhmaṇān pitṛtuṣṭyartham anukalpeṣv anantarān \|\| 4 \|\|*

The householder should first engage the brahmins mentioned above in a śrāddha, sire, in order to nourish his ancestral spirits, while others may be employed for less important rituals. 4

## \|\| 3.15.5 \|\|

*mitradhrukkunakhī klībaḥ śyāvadantas tathā dvijaḥ \|*  
*kanyā dūṣayitāvahnivedojjhaḥsomavikrayī \|\| 5 \|\|*

A false friend, a person with bad fingernails, one who is impotent, a brahmin with discoloured teeth, one who has sexually assaulted young women, one who has abandoned his sacred fires or Vedic studies, one who sells soma, 5

## \|\| 3.15.6 \|\|

*abhiśastas tathā stenaḥ puśuno grāmayājakaḥ \|*  
*bhṛtakādhyāpakastadvadbhṛtakādhyāpitaś ca yaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

A person accused of a crime, a thief, a slanderer, one who performs sacrifices for villagers, one who teaches sacred texts to his servants or learns such texts from them, 6

## \|\| 3.15.7 \|\|

*parapūrvāpatiścaiva mātāpitrostathojjhakaḥ \|*  
*vṛṣalīsūtipoṣṭā ca vṛṣalīpatireva ca \|\| 7 \|\|*

The husband of a woman who was previously married, one who has abandoned his mother and father, one who supports the children of a śūdra or who is married to one, 7

## \|\| 3.15.8 \|\|

*tathā devalakaścaiva śrāddhe nārhati ketanam \|\| 8 \|\|*

One who lives off offerings made to an idol—none of these is worthy of an invitation to a śrāddha. 8

## \|\| 3.15.9 \|\|

*prathamehni budhaḥ śastāñchrotriyādīnnimantrayet \|*  
*kathayecca tathaivaiṣāṃ niyogānpitṛdaivikān \|\| 9 \|\|*

On the first day, the prudent man should welcome outstanding scholars and other dignitaries and should explain to them his intention with regard to his ancestors and the deities. 9

## \|\| 3.15.10 \|\|

*tataḥ krodhavyavāyādīnāyāsaṃ tair dhijaiḥ saha \|*  
*yajamāno na kurvīta doṣastatra mahānayam \|\| 10 \|\|*

After this, the initiator of the sacrifice, together with the brahmins, should refrain from anger, sexual intercourse and other business, as these are major obstacles to the ritual. 10

## \|\| 3.15.11 \|\|

*śrāddhe niyukto bhuktvā vā bhojayitvā niyujya ca \|*  
*vyavāyī retaso garte majjayatyātmanaḥ pitṝn \|\| 11 \|\|*

If a man who holds a śrāddha, having begun to eat or having fed the brahmins whom he tasked with the ritual, then has sexual intercourse, he will cause his own ancestors to drown in a pool of semen. 11

## \|\| 3.15.12 \|\|

*tasmātprathamamatroktaṃ dvijāgryāṇāṃ nimantraṇam \|*  
*animantrya dvijānevamāgatānbho jayedyatīn \|\| 12 \|\|*

First inviting eminent brahmins, as I mentioned, he should also offer food to any pious members of that order who arrive at his home unexpectedly. 12

## \|\| 3.15.13 \|\|

*pādaśaucādinā gehamāgatānpūjayedddhijān \|\| 13 \|\|*

He should welcome all such individuals who come to his door with water for their feet and so on. 13

## \|\| 3.15.14 \|\|

*pavitrapāṇirācāntānāsaneṣūpaveśayet \|*  
*pitṝṇāmayujo yugmāndevānā micchayā dvijān \|\| 14 \|\|*

With blades of kuśa-grass in his hands, he should offer them water to drink and invite them to be seated—an odd number of brahmins for an ancestral śrāddha, and an even number for rituals for the deities, or as he wishes. 14

## \|\| 3.15.15 \|\|

*devānāmekamekaṃ vā pitṝṇāṃ ca niyojayet \|\| 15 \|\|*

He may also engage a single brahmin for both the deities and his ancestors. 15

## \|\| 3.15.16 \|\|

*tathā mātāmahaśrāddhaṃ vaiśvadevasamanvitam \|*  
*kurvīta bhaktīsaṃpannastatra vā vaiśvadaivikam \|\| 16 \|\|*

Filled with devotion, he should perform a śrāddha for his maternal grandfather, which incorporates the worship of the Viśvadevas, or he may perform a ritual specifically for them. 16

## \|\| 3.15.17 \|\|

*prāṅmukhānbhojayedviprāndevānāmubhayātmakān \|*  
*pitṝn pitāmahānāṃ ca bhojayeccāpyudaṅmukhān \|\| 17 \|\|*

He should offer food to those brahmins engaged for the deities, or those engaged for both paternal and maternal ancestors, while they are facing east, and to those engaged solely for his paternal ancestors, while facing north. 17

## \|\| 3.15.18 \|\|

*pṛthaktayoḥ kecidāhuḥ śrāddhasya karaṇaṃ nṛpa \|*  
*ekatraikena pākena vadantyanye maharṣayaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

Some say that śrāddhas for paternal and maternal ancestors should be undertaken separately, sire, while other famous sages say they may be held together and with the same offerings of food. 18

## \|\| 3.15.19 \|\|

*viṣṭarārthaṃ kuśaṃ dattvā saṃpūjyārghyaṃ vidhānataḥ \|*  
*kuryādāvāhanaṃ prājño devānāṃ tadanujñayā \|\| 19 \|\|*

After spreading kuśa-grass upon the ground and duly welcoming brahmins with hospitality, the wise man should, with the brahmins’ permission, call upon the deities. 19

## \|\| 3.15.20 \|\|

*yavāṃbunā ca devānāṃ dadyādarghyaṃ vidhānavit \|*  
*sraggandhadhūpadīpāṃś ca tebhyo dadyādyathāvidhi \|\| 20 \|\|*

He should welcome them with grains of barley and some water, having offered garlands, perfumes, incense, lamps and other things, in accordance with tradition. 20

## \|\| 3.15.21 \|\|

*pitṝṇāmapasavyaṃ tatsarvamevopakalpayet \|*  
*anujñāṃ ca tataḥ prāpya dattvā darbhandvidhākṛtān \|\| 21 \|\|*

He should also offer these, placed on the left, to his ancestors, having first obtained permission from the brahmins and after spreading two layers of darbha-grass. 21

## \|\| 3.15.22 \|\|

*mantrapūrvaṃ pitṝṇāṃ tu kuryāccāvāhanaṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*tilāṃbunā cāpasavya dadyādarghyādikaṃ nṛpa \|\| 22 \|\|*

The wise man should then invoke the deities, preceded by mantras, sire, and on the lefthand side should make an offering to them with sesame and water. 22

## \|\| 3.15.23 \|\|

*kāle tatrātithiṃ prāptamannakāmaṃ nṛpādhvagam \|*  
*brahmaṇair abhyanujñātaḥ kāmaṃ tamapi bhojayet \|\| 23 \|\|*

At that time, again with the permission of the brahmins, sire, he should willingly offer food to any hungry traveller who arrives. 23

## \|\| 3.15.24 \|\|

*yogino vividhai rūpair narāmāmupakārīṇaḥ \|*  
*bhramanti pṛthivīmetāmavijñātasvarūpimaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

Ascetics, who appear in various forms to do folk favours, wander about the world disguised. 24

## \|\| 3.15.25 \|\|

*tasmādabhyarcayetprāptaṃ śrāddhakāle 'tithiṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*śrāddhakriyāphalaṃ hṝnti naredrāpūjito 'tithiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

For this reason, a prudent man should always welcome visitors when a śrāddha is conducted, sire, for when a guest is deprived of hospitality, the outcome of the ritual is imperilled. 25

## \|\| 3.15.26 \|\|

*juhuyādvyañjanakṣāravarjamannaṃ tato 'nale \|*  
*anujñāto dvijais tais tu trikṛtvaḥ puruṣarṣabha \|\| 26 \|\|*

With permission from the brahmins, the householder should then offer three handfuls of plain, unsalted food into the fire, you bull among men. 26

## \|\| 3.15.27 \|\|

*agnaye kavyavāhāya svadhetyādau nṛpāhutiḥ \|*  
*somāya vai pitṛmate dātavyā tadanantaram \|\| 27 \|\|*

He should first say, ‘Svāhā to the fire, bearer of the offering!’ Thereupon, your majesty, he should make an offering to Soma, the Moon, who nourishes ancestral spirits. 27

## \|\| 3.15.28 \|\|

*vaivasvatāya caivānyā tṛtīyā dīyate tataḥ \|*  
*hutāvaśiṣṭamalpānnaṃ viprapātreṣu nirvepet \|\| 28 \|\|*

The householder should then make a third offering to Vaivasvata, the Sun’s own son, after which he places a portion of the remnant food in the brahmins’ bowls. 28

## \|\| 3.15.29 \|\|

*tatonnaṃ mṛṣṭamatyarthamabhīṣṭamatisaṃskṛtam \|*  
*dattvā juṣadhvamicchāto vācyametadaniṣṭhuram \|\| 29 \|\|*

He should then offer the brahmins some delicious, highly desirable, carefully prepared food, with the polite invitation, ‘Please enjoy this at your leisure.’ 29

## \|\| 3.15.30 \|\|

*bhoktavyaṃ taiś ca taccittair maunibhiḥ sumukhaiḥ sukham \|*  
*akrudvyatā cātvaratā deyaṃ tenāpi bhaktitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

The brahmins should happily partake of this meal attentively, in silence and with cheerful smiles. It should be served without anger or haste, but rather with devotion. 30

## \|\| 3.15.31 \|\|

*rakṣoghramantrapaṭhanaṃ bhūmerāstaraṇaṃ tilaiḥ \|*  
*kṛtvādhyeyāḥsvapitarasta eva dvijasattamāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Having uttered mantras to ward off rākṣasas and scattered sesame on the ground, the householder should then regard the leading brahmins as his own forebears. 31

## \|\| 3.15.32 \|\|

*pitā pitāmahaścaiva tathaiva prapitāmahaḥ \|*  
*mama tṛptiṃ prayāntvadya vipradeheṣu saṃsthitāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

‘May my father, grandfather and great-grandfather, in the form of these brahmins, be satisfied today. 32

## \|\| 3.15.33 \|\|

*pitā pitāmahaścaiva tathaiva prapitāmahaḥ \|*  
*mama tṛptiṃ prayāntvadya homāpyāyitamūrtayaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

May my father, grandfather and great-grandfather be satisfied, their bodies nourished by this offering made into the fire. 33

## \|\| 3.15.34 \|\|

*pitā pitāmahaścaiva tathaiva prapitāmahaḥ \|*  
*tṛptiṃ prayātu piṇḍena mayā dattena bhūtale \|\| 34 \|\|*

May my father, grandfather and great-grandfather be satisfied with the food I’ve scattered on the ground. 34

## \|\| 3.15.35 \|\|

*pitā pitāmahaścaiva tathaiva prapitāmahaḥ \|*  
*tṛptiṃ prayāntu me bhaktyā mayaitatsamudāhṛtam \|\| 35 \|\|*

May my father, grandfather and great-grandfather be satisfied by this offering I’ve made here with devotion. 35

## \|\| 3.15.36 \|\|

*mātāmahas tṛptim upaitu tasya tathā pitā tasya pitā tato 'nyaḥ \|*  
*viśve ca devāḥ paramāṃ prayāntu tṛptiṃ praṇaśyantu ca yātudhānāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

May my maternal grandfather, his father, his father and his father be satisfied. May all the deities achieve highest satisfaction, and may all evil spirits be dispelled. 36

## \|\| 3.15.37 \|\|

*yajñeśvaro havyasamastakavyo bhoktāvyayātmā harirīśvaro 'tra \|*  
*tatsaṃnidhānādapayāntu sadyo rakṣāṃsyaśeṣaṇyasurāś ca sarve \|\| 37 \|\|*

May the master of the sacrifice, Hari, the everlasting lord, enjoy all the offerings I’ve made here. May all rākṣasas and demons be gone at once from the vicinity of this sacrifice.’ 37

## \|\| 3.15.38 \|\|

*tṛpteṣveteṣu vikiredannaṃ vipreṣu bhūtale \|*  
*dadyādācamanārthāya tebhyo vārī sakṛtsakṛt \|\| 38 \|\|*

When the brahmins have been satisfied, the householder should scatter leftover food upon the ground and offer every brahmin some water to rinse his mouth. 38

## \|\| 3.15.39 \|\|

*sutṛptais tair anujñātaḥsarveṇānnena bhūtale \|*  
*satilona tataḥ piṇḍānsamyagdadyātsamāhitaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

With the permission of the well-fed brahmins, and with careful attention, he should place an offering of food mixed with sesame seeds on the ground. 39

## \|\| 3.15.40 \|\|

*pirṛtīrthena salilaṃ tathaiva salilāñjalim \|*  
*mātāmahebhyastenaiva piṇḍāṃstīrthena nirvapet \|\| 40 \|\|*

With the part of his hand sacred to the ancestors, he should offer water in his cupped hands to his maternal forefathers, and with the same part of his hand, make an offering of food. 40

## \|\| 3.15.41 \|\|

*dakṣiṇāgreṣu darbheṣu puṣpadhūpādipūjitam \|*  
*svapitre prathamaṃ piṇḍaṃ dadyāducchiṣṭasannidhau \|\| 41 \|\|*

On a mat of darbha-grass with its tips pointing south, he should place the first food offering for his own father, consecrated with flowers, incense and so on, next to the leftover foods. 41

## \|\| 3.15.42 \|\|

*pitāmahāya caivānyaṃ tatpitre ca tathāparam \|*  
*darbhamūle lepabhujaḥ prīṇayellepagharṣaṇaiḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

He should then make a second offering to his grandfather, and another to his great-grandfather. He should then satisfy his distant ancestors by scattering remnants of food wiped from his fingers on to the roots of the grass. 42

## \|\| 3.15.43 \|\|

*piṇḍair mātāmahāṃstadvadgandhamālyādisaṃyutaiḥ \|*  
*pūjāyitvā dvijāgryāṇāṃ dadyāccācamanaṃ tataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

Having honoured his maternal ancestors in the same manner with offerings of food accompanied by incense and garlands, the householder should again give the leading brahmins water with which to rinse their mouths. 43

## \|\| 3.15.44 \|\|

*pitṛbhyaḥ prathamaṃ bhaktyātanmanaskonareśvara \|*  
*susvadhetyāśiṣā yuktāṃ dadyācchaktyā ca dakṣiṇām \|\| 44 \|\|*

Thinking of them alone, sire, he should first make offerings to the ancestors with devotion, and then, requesting their benediction, while uttering ‘susvadhā’, he should give gifts such as he can afford to the brahmins. 44

## \|\| 3.15.45 \|\|

*dattvā ca dakṣiṇāṃ tebhyo vācayed vaiśvadevikān \|*  
*prīyantāmihaye viśvedevāstena itīrayet \|\| 45 \|\|*

After he has given gifts to the Viśvadevas, he should address these words to them: ‘May those who are Viśvadevas be pleased with this offering.’ 45

## \|\| 3.15.46 \|\|

*tatheti cokte tair vipraiḥ prārthanīyāstathāsiṣaḥ \|*  
*paścādvisarjayeddevānpūrvaṃ pai3tryānmahīpate \|\| 46 \|\|*

When the brahmins have replied ‘So be it’, the householder should request their benediction, wise sovereign, after which he should first dismiss the ancestral spirits and then the deities. 46

## \|\| 3.15.47 \|\|

*mātāmahānāmapyevaṃ saha devaiḥ kramaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*bhojayecca svaśaktyā ca dāne tadvadvisarjane \|\| 47 \|\|*

The offering of food, the giving of gifts in accordance with one’s capacity and the dismissal—all these follow the same order for both maternal ancestors and the deities. 47

## \|\| 3.15.48 \|\|

*āpādaśaucanātpūrvaṃ kuryāddevadvijanmasu \|*  
*visarjanaṃ tu prathamaṃ paitraṃ mātāmaheṣu vai \|\| 48 \|\|*

From the initial washing of the feet to the dismissal of the gods and brahmins, the householder should make offerings first to his paternal and then to his maternal ancestors. 48

## \|\| 3.15.49 \|\|

*visarjayetprītivacaḥsaṃmānyābhyarthitāstataḥ \|*  
*nivartetābhyanujñāta ādvāraṃ tānanuvrajet \|\| 49 \|\|*

He should send the brahmins off with kind words, having paid them due respect, and then accompany them to the gate, after which, with their permission, he may return. 49

## \|\| 3.15.50 \|\|

*tatastu vaiśvadevākhyaṃ kuryānnityakriyaṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*bhuñjīyācca samaṃ pūjya bhṛtyabandhubhir ātmanaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

The wise man should then perform the usual ritual, the so-called vaiśvadeva rite, for all the deities. He may then enjoy a meal together with respected individuals, his dependants and relations. 50

## \|\| 3.15.51 \|\|

*evaṃ śrāddhaṃ budhaḥ kuryātpaitryaṃ mātāmahaṃ tathā \|*  
*śrāddhair āpyāyitā dadyuḥsarvānkāmanpitāmahāḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

In this way, a wise man should conduct śrāddha rituals for his paternal and maternal ancestors. Nourished by such śrāddhas, his ancestors will grant his every wish. 51

## \|\| 3.15.52 \|\|

*trīṇi śrāddhe pavitrāṇi dauhitraḥ kutapastilāḥ \|*  
*rajatasya kathā dānaṃ tathā saṃkīrtanādikam \|\| 52 \|\|*

Three things have purifying power at a śrāddha: one’s daughter’s son, a cashmere blanket and sesame seeds. There is also the giving, naming and displaying of silver. 52

## \|\| 3.15.53 \|\|

*jarjyāni kurvatā śrāddhaṃ krodhodhvagamanaṃ tvārā \|*  
*bhokturapyatra rājendra trayametanna śasyate \|\| 53 \|\|*

A person offering a śrāddha should eschew anger, wandering and haste, sire, as these three are not recommended for those who take part in this ritual. 53

## \|\| 3.15.54 \|\|

*viśvedevāḥsapitaras tathā mātāmahā nṛpa \|*  
*kulaṃ cāpyāyate puṃsāṃ sarvaṃ śrāddhaṃ prakurvatām \|\| 54 \|\|*

The Viśvadevas, the paternal and maternal ancestors and the entire family of those who perform a śrāddha are nourished, sire. 54

## \|\| 3.15.55 \|\|

*somādhāraḥ pitṛgaṇo yogādhāraś ca candramāḥ \|*  
*śrāddhe yoginiyogastu tasmādbhupālaśasyate \|\| 55 \|\|*

The host of ancestors is supported by the moon, and the moon is supported by ascetic practices. That’s why, your majesty, it’s recommended that ascetics be engaged to undertake a śrāddha. 55

## \|\| 3.15.56 \|\|

*sahasrasyāpi viprāṇāṃ yogī cetpurataḥsthitaḥ \|*  
*sarvān bhoktṝṃs tārayati yajamānaṃ tathā nṛpa \|\| 56 \|\|*

If just one ascetic stands before a thousand brahmins, sire, he will lead all participants, including the initiator, to the further shore. 56

## Chapter 16 - Śrāddha offerings

## \|\| 3.16.1 \|\|

*aurva uvāca*  
*haviṣyamatsyamāsais tu śaśasya nakulasya ca \|*  
*sokaracchākalaiṇeyarauravair gavayena ca \|\| 1 \|\|*

An offering of fish or the flesh of a hare, bird, pig, goat, black antelope, deer, wild ox, 1

## \|\| 3.16.2 \|\|

*aurabhragavyaiś ca tathā māsavṛddhyā pitāmahāḥ \|*  
*prayānti tṛptiṃ māṃsais tu nityaṃ vārdhroṇasāmiṣaiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sheep or cow—each of these nourishes the ancestors for one month longer than the offering named before it, but they will be satisfied forever with the flesh of the leather-billed bird, the vādhrīṇasa. 2

## \|\| 3.16.3 \|\|

*khaḍgamāṃsamatīvātra kālaśākaṃ tathā madhu \|*  
*śastāni karmaṇyatyantatṛptidāni nareśvara \|\| 3 \|\|*

The flesh of a rhinoceros, sacred basil and honey are highly recommended for these offerings, sire, as they, too, provide eternal nourishment. 3

## \|\| 3.16.4 \|\|

*gayāmupetya yaḥ śrāddhaṃ karoti pṛthivīpate \|*  
*saphalaṃ tasya tajjanma jāyate pitṛtīṣṭidam \|\| 4 \|\|*

The life of one who undertakes a śrāddha at Gayā is fulfilled, sire, because it nourishes his ancestors. 4

## \|\| 3.16.5 \|\|

*praśāntikāḥsamīvārāḥ śyāmākā dvividhās tathā \|*  
*vanyaiṣadhīpradhānāstu śrāddhārhāḥ puruṣarṣabha \|\| 5 \|\|*

Small-grained rice, wild rice, sorghum of both kinds and forest herbs are all fit for a śrāddha, you bull among men. 5

## \|\| 3.16.6 \|\|

*yavāḥ priyaṅgavo mudgā godhūmā vrīhayastilāḥ \|*  
*niṣpāvāḥ kovidārāś ca sarṣapāścātra sobhanāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Barley, foxtail millet, black lentils, wheat, rice, sesame, cowpeas, kodo millet and mustard seeds are also suitable. 6

## \|\| 3.16.7 \|\|

*akṛtāgrayaṇaṃ yacca dhānyajātaṃ nareśvara \|*  
*rājamāṣānaṇuṃścaiva masūrāṃś ca visarjayet \|\| 7 \|\|*

The householder should not use grain unconsecrated at the time of the first harvest, sire, or black-eyed peas, proso millet or garlic. 7

## \|\| 3.16.8 \|\|

*alābuṃ gṛñjanaṃ caiva palāṇḍuṃ piṇḍamūlakam \|*  
*gāndhārakakaraṃvādilavāṇānyauṣarāṇi ca \|\| 8 \|\|*

Bottle gourds, red or brown onions, wild carrots, gandhāraka, groats, sea salt, rock salt, 8

## \|\| 3.16.9 \|\|

*āraktāścaiva niryāsāḥ pratyakṣalavaṇāni ca \|*  
*varjyānyetāni vai śrāddhe yacca vācā na śasyate \|\| 9 \|\|*

Any reddish gums and other substances resembling salt—these are best avoided for a śrāddha, along with other substances that have not been recommended. 9

## \|\| 3.16.10 \|\|

*naktāhṛtamanucchinnaṃ tṛpyate na ca yatra gauḥ \|*  
*durghadhiphenilaṃ cāṃbu śrāddhayogyaṃ na pārthiva \|\| 10 \|\|*

Water that was drawn at night, or isn’t fresh, sire, or is insufficient to satisfy a cow, or smells unpleasant or is frothy is unsuitable for a śrāddha. 10

## \|\| 3.16.11 \|\|

*kṣiramekasaphānāṃ yadauṣṭramāvikameva ca \|*  
*mārgaṃ ca māhiṣaṃ caiva varjayecchrāddhakameṇi \|\| 11 \|\|*

During the performance of a śrāddha, one should avoid the milk of animals whose hooves are not cloven, including camels, sheep, deer and buffalo. 11

## \|\| 3.16.12 \|\|

*ṣaṇḍāpaviddhacaṇḍālapāpipāṣaṇḍarogibhiḥ \|*  
*kṛkavākuśvānanagna-vānaragrāmasūkaraiḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

A eunuch, outcaste, untouchable, heretic, drunk, invalid, rooster, dog, naked person, monkey, village pig, 12

## \|\| 3.16.13 \|\|

*udakyāsūtikāśaucamṛtahāraiś ca vīkṣite \|*  
*śrāddhe surā na pitaro bhuñjante puruṣarṣabha \|\| 13 \|\|*

Menstruating woman or a woman who has recently given birth—neither deities nor ancestors will accept a śrāddha observed by any of these. 13

## \|\| 3.16.14 \|\|

*tasmātparīśrite kuryācchrāddhaṃ śraddhāsamanvitaḥ \|*  
*urvyāṃ ca tilavikṣepādyātudhānānnivārayet \|\| 14 \|\|*

The devout individual should therefore conduct a śrāddha in an enclosed space and should drive off evil spirits by strewing sesame on the ground. 14

## \|\| 3.16.15 \|\|

*nakhādinā copapannaṃ keśakīṭādibhir nṛpa \|*  
*na caivābhiṣavair miśram annaṃ paryuṣitaṃ tathā \|\| 15 \|\|*

Food should not be rotten, contaminated with hairs, insects or anything else, sire, or be mixed with substances that lead to fermentation, but must be fresh. 15

## \|\| 3.16.16 \|\|

*śraddhāsamanvitair dattaṃ pitṛbhyo nāmagotrataḥ \|*  
*yadāhārāstu te jātāstadāhāratvameti tat \|\| 16 \|\|*

Food offered by the faithful to the ancestors, while intoning their names and lineages, is sustenance for those very beings. 16

## \|\| 3.16.17 \|\|

*śrūyate cāpi pitṛrbhigītā gāthā mahīpate \|*  
*ikṣvākormanuputrasya kalāpopavane purā \|\| 17 \|\|*

The following song was sung by ancestors long ago, your majesty, and was heard by Manu’s son Ikṣvāku in the grove of Kalāpa: 17

## \|\| 3.16.18 \|\|

*api naste bhaviṣyanti kule sanmārgaśīlinaḥ \|*  
*gayāmupetya ye piṇḍāndāsyantyasmākamādarāt \|\| 18 \|\|*

‘May those who come to Gayā to make food offerings, out of respect for us, be reborn in our lineage as followers of the true path. 18

## \|\| 3.16.19 \|\|

*api naḥsa kule jāyādyo no dadyāttrayodaśīm \|*  
*pāyasaṃ madhusarpirbhyāṃ varṣāsu ca maghāsu ca \|\| 19 \|\|*

May one who offers us rice pudding with honey and butter on the thirteenth day of Bhādrapada or Maghā be reborn in our lineage, 19

## \|\| 3.16.20 \|\|

*gaurīṃ vāpyudvahetkanyāṃ nīlaṃ vā vṛṣamutsṛjet \|*  
*yajeta vāśvamedhena vidhivaddakṣiṇāvatā \|\| 20 \|\|*

Or when he marries a fair girl, or liberates a black bull or performs a horse-sacrifice in accordance with the rules and with the proper recompense.’ 20

## Chapter 17 - The protection afforded by the Vedas; Viṣṇu’s phantom

## \|\| 3.17.1 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityāha bhagavānaurvaḥsagarāya mahātmane \|*  
*sadācāraṃ purā samyaṅ maitreya paripṛcchate \|\| 1 \|\|*

This is precisely what holy Aurva told the great sage Sagara, Maitreya, when he asked about good conduct long ago. 1

## \|\| 3.17.2 \|\|

*mayāpyetadaśeṣeṇa tathitaṃ bhavato dvija \|*  
*samullaṅghya sadācāraṃ kaścinnāpnoti śobhanam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Now I’ve told you everything, brahmin. One who strays from this will never win success. 2

## \|\| 3.17.3 \|\|

*śrīmaitreya uvāca*  
*ṣaṇḍāpaviddhapramukhā viditā bhagavanmayā \|*  
*udakyādyāś ca me samyaṅ nagnamicchāmi veditum \|\| 3 \|\|*

Maitreya:

I know about the eunuchs, outcastes, menstruating women and the others, master, but I’d like to hear some more about the naked ones. 3

## \|\| 3.17.4 \|\|

*konagnaḥ kiṃ samācāro nagnasaṃjñāṃ naro labhet \|*  
*nagnasvarūpamicchāmi yathāvatkathitaṃ tvāya \|*  
*śrotuṃ dharmabhṛtāṃ śreṣṭha na hyastyaviditaṃ tava \|\| 4 \|\|*

Who goes naked, and what acts lead to a person being called this? I’d like you to describe exactly what is meant by nakedness. 4

## \|\| 3.17.5 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ṛgyajuḥsāmasaṃjñeyaṃ trayī varṇāvṛttirdvija \|*  
*etāmujjhati yo mohātsa nagnaḥ pātakī dvajaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The Ṛg, Yajur and Sāma Vedas are a threefold garment for the various communities, brahmin, but a deluded individual who casts off this mantle is regarded as both sinful and naked. 5

## \|\| 3.17.6 \|\|

*trayī samastavarṇānāṃ dvijasaṃvāraṇaṃ yataḥ \|*  
*nagno bhavatyujjhitāyāṃ yatastasyāṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

Because the three Vedas clad each community, when they’re cast aside, an individual is surely rendered naked. 6

## \|\| 3.17.7 \|\|

*idaṃ ca śruyatāmanyadyadbhīṣmāya mahātmane \|*  
*kathayāmāsa dharmajño vasiṣṭho 'smatpitāmahaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

Listen to what our ancestor Vasiṣṭha, who understood tradition, said to mighty Bhīṣma. 7

## \|\| 3.17.8 \|\|

*mayāpi tasya gadataḥ śrutametanmahātmanaḥ \|*  
*nagnasaṃbandhi maitreya yatpṛṣṭohamiha tvāyā \|\| 8 \|\|*

This is what I heard that great-souled individual say with regard to nakedness, and which you asked about just now. 8

## \|\| 3.17.9 \|\|

*devāsuramabhūdyuddhaṃ dvivyamabdaśataṃ purā \|*  
*tasminparajitā devā daityair hrādapurogamaiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

Long ago, the deities waged a war against the demigods, brahmin. During that confrontation, which continued for one divine year, they were routed by the Daityas led by Hrāda. 9

## \|\| 3.17.10 \|\|

*kṣīrodasyottaraṃ kūlaṃ gatvā tapyanta vai tapaḥ \|*  
*viṣṇorārādhanārthāya jaguścemaṃ stavaṃ tadā \|\| 10 \|\|*

The deities then retreated to the Milky Ocean’s northern shore, where they undertook austerities to propitiate Lord Viṣṇu, and they sang this song of praise: 10

## \|\| 3.17.11 \|\|

*devā ūcuḥ*  
*ārādhanāya lokānāṃ viṣṇorīśasya yāṃ giram \|*  
*vakṣyāmo bhagavānadya tayā viṣṇuḥ prasīdatu \|\| 11 \|\|*

The deities:

May Lord Viṣṇu, first among beings and master of the worlds, be pleased with this song we sing to worship him. 11

## \|\| 3.17.12 \|\|

*yato bhūtānyaśeṣāṇi prasūtāni mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*yasmiś ca layameṣyanti kastaṃ stotumiheśvaraḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Who can sing the praises of that great being, the origin of all creation, into whom it dissolves again? 12

## \|\| 3.17.13 \|\|

*tathāpyarātividhvaṃsadhvastavīryā bhayārthinaḥ \|*  
*tvāṃ stoṣyāmastavoktīnāṃ yāthārthyaṃ naiva gocare \|\| 13 \|\|*

Although our power was thwarted in this battle, desiring our own felicity, we still praise you, even though your actual nature is beyond the reach of words. 13

## \|\| 3.17.14 \|\|

*tvamurvī salilaṃ vahnirvāyurākāśameva ca \|*  
*samastamantaḥkaraṇaṃ pradhānaṃ tatparaḥ pumān \|\| 14 \|\|*

You are earth, water, fire, wind, space, mind, primal matter and the highest spirit beyond them all. 14

## \|\| 3.17.15 \|\|

*ekaṃ tavaitadbhūtātmanmūrtamūrtamayaṃ vapuḥ \|*  
*ābrahmastaṃbaparyantaṃ sthānakālavibhedavat \|\| 15 \|\|*

You are at the heart of all creation. This whole world, from Brahmā to a tuft of grass, both formed and formless, is your single body, only split by time and place. 15

## \|\| 3.17.16 \|\|

*tatraiśaṃ tava yatpūrvaṃ tvannābhikamalodbhavam \|*  
*rūpaṃ viśvopakārāya tasmai brahmātmane namaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

We bow to you, Lord, in the form of Brahmā, who first arose from the lotus at your navel to create the world. 16

## \|\| 3.17.17 \|\|

*śakrārkarudravasvaśvimarutsomādibhedavat \|*  
*vayamekaṃ svarūpaṃ te tasmai devātmane namaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

We bow to your divine form, divided among ourselves as mighty Indra, the Sun, Rudra, the Vasus, Aśvins, Maruts, the Moon and others. 17

## \|\| 3.17.18 \|\|

*daṃbhaprāyamasaṃbodhi titiśrādamavarjitam \|*  
*yadrūpaṃ tava govinda tasmai daityātmane namaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

We bow to you, Govinda, in the form of Daityas: deceitful, ignorant, impatient and unrestrained. 18

## \|\| 3.17.19 \|\|

*nātijñānavahā yasminnāḍyastimitatejasi \|*  
*śabdādilobhi yastasmai tubhyaṃ yatrātmane namaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

We bow to you in the form of yakṣas, whose frivolous hearts lack discernment and who hanker for sensations such as sound. 19

## \|\| 3.17.20 \|\|

*krauryaṃ māyāmayaṃ ghoraṃ yacca rūpaṃ tavāsitam \|*  
*niśācarātmane tasmai namaste puruṣottama \|\| 20 \|\|*

We bow to you, highest spirit, in the form of night-ranging rākṣasas: cruel, deceptive, fierce and dark. 20

## \|\| 3.17.21 \|\|

*svargasthadharmisaddharmaphalopakaraṇaṃ tava \|*  
*dharmākhyaṃ ca tathā rūpaṃ namastasmai janārdana \|\| 21 \|\|*

We bow to you, Janārdana, in the form called dharma, which yields rewards of truest virtue for pious beings who dwell in heaven. 21

## \|\| 3.17.22 \|\|

*harṣaprāyamasaṃsargi gatimadgamanādiṣu \|*  
*siddhākhyaṃ tava yadrūpaṃ tasmai siddhātmane namaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

We bow to you, success embodied, in the form of siddhas, those perfected beings consisting of unalloyed bliss, the goal of all departures. 22

## \|\| 3.17.23 \|\|

*atitikṣāyanaṃ krūramupabhogasahaṃ hare \|*  
*dvijīhvantava yadrūpaṃ tasmai nāgātmane namaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

We bow to you, Hari, in the form of nāgas: fork-tongued, resilient, cruel and bent on pleasure. 23

## \|\| 3.17.24 \|\|

*avabodhi ca yacchāntamadoṣamapakalpaṣam \|*  
*ṛṣirūpātmane tasmai viśvarūpāya te namaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

We bow to you, Viṣṇu, in the form of sages: knowledgeable, tranquil, pure and faultless. 24

## \|\| 3.17.25 \|\|

*bhakṣayatyatha kalpānte bhūtāni yadavāritam \|*  
*tvadrūpaṃ puṇḍharīkākṣa tasmai kālātmane namaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

We bow to you, lotus-eyed deity, in the form of Time, which, unstoppable, consumes all creatures when each aeon ends. 25

## \|\| 3.17.26 \|\|

*saṃbhakṣya sarvabhūtāni devādīny aviśeṣataḥ \|*  
*nṛtyatyante ca yadrūpaṃ tasmai rudrātmane namaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

We bow to you in the form of Rudra, who, having swallowed all creation including gods, dances at the destruction of the world. 26

## \|\| 3.17.27 \|\|

*pravṛttyā rajaso yacca karmaṇāṃ karaṇātmakam \|*  
*janardana namastasmai tvadrūpāya narātmane \|\| 27 \|\|*

We bow to you, Janārdana, in the form of humankind, the motive force of acts that are carried out with energy. 27

## \|\| 3.17.28 \|\|

*aṣṭāviśadvadhopetaṃ yadrūpaṃ tāmasaṃ tava \|*  
*unmārgagāmi sarvātmaṃstasmai vaśyātmane namaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

We bow to you, the universal being, in the form of animal life, which ends in twenty-eight modes of death and, marked by stupidity, often goes astray. 28

## \|\| 3.17.29 \|\|

*yajñāṅgabhūtaṃ yadrūpaṃ jagataḥ sthitisādhanam \|*  
*vṛkṣādibhedaiḥ ṣaḍbhedi tasmai mukyātmane namaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

We bow to you, the foremost one, the diverse being differentiated into trees and other kinds of plants, which provide the basis for the sacrifice, and which in turn allow perfection of the world. 29

## \|\| 3.17.30 \|\|

*tiryaṅmanuṣyadevādivyomaśabdādikaṃ ca yat \|*  
*rūpaṃ tavādeḥ sarvasya tasmai sarvātmane namaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

We bow to you, universal being, whose initial form is the elements including space and sensations such as sound, all of which are experienced by animals, humans, deities and the rest. 30

## \|\| 3.17.31 \|\|

*pradhānabuddhyādimayādaśeṣādyadanyadasmātparamaṃ parātman \|*  
*rūpaṃ tavādyaṃ yadananyatulyaṃ tasmai namaḥ kāraṇakāraṇāya \|\| 31 \|\|*

We bow to you, the highest being, cause of every cause, whose ultimate form is distinct from us and all that consists of matter, intelligence and so on, and has no equal. 31

## \|\| 3.17.32 \|\|

*śuklādidīrghādighanādihīnamagocaraṃ yacca viśeṣaṇānām \|*  
*suddhātiśuddhaṃ paramarṣidṛśyaṃ rūpāya tasmai bhagavannatāḥ smaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

We bow, Lord, to that form that has no colour such as white, no dimension such as length and no quality such as density, which is beyond the range of descriptions, which is the purest of the pure and is perceptible only to the highest sages. 32

## \|\| 3.17.33 \|\|

*yannaḥ śarīreṣu yadanyadeheṣvaśeṣavastuṣvajamakṣayaṃ yat \|*  
*tasmācca nānyavdyatiriktamasti brahmasvarūpāya natāḥ sma tasmai \|\| 33 \|\|*

We bow to that form of the Absolute that is in our bodies, and in others, and in all things, unborn, unchanging and beyond which there is nothing. 33

## \|\| 3.17.34 \|\|

*sakalamidamajasya yasya rūpaṃ paramapadātmavataḥsanātanasya \|*  
*tamanidhanasaśeṣabījabhūtaṃ prabhumamalaṃ praṇatāḥsma vāsudevam \|\| 34 \|\|*

We bow to the stainless Lord Vāsudeva, the highest state, the everlasting unborn being whose form is all the universe, who has no end and is the seed of all creation. 34

## \|\| 3.17.35 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*stotrasya cāvasāne te dadṛśuḥ parameśvaram \|*  
*śaṅkhacakragadāpāṇiṃ garuḍasthaṃ surā harim \|\| 35 \|\|*

Parāśara:

At the conclusion of this paean, the deities beheld the supreme Lord Hari, holding the conch, sword and discus, mounted on Garuḍa. 35

## \|\| 3.17.36 \|\|

*tamucuḥsakalā devāḥ praṇipātapuraḥsuram \|*  
*prasīda nātha daityebhyastrāhi naḥ śaraṇārthinaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

Falling to the ground, the host of deities, seeking his protection, beseeched him: ‘Be merciful to us, Lord, and save us from these Daityas. 36

## \|\| 3.17.37 \|\|

*trelokyayajñabhāgāś ca daityair hlādapurogamaiḥ \|*  
*hṛtā no brahmaṇopyayājñāmullaṅghya paramśvara \|\| 37 \|\|*

Led by Hrāda, the demons have deprived us of all three worlds and our portion of the sacrifice, highest Lord, ignoring the injunctions of the Vedas. 37

## \|\| 3.17.38 \|\|

*yadyapyaśeṣabhū tasya vayaṃ te ca tāvaṃśajāḥ \|*  
*tathāpyavidyābhedena bhinnaṃ paśyāmahe jagat \|\| 38 \|\|*

Even though we deities and Daityas are merely aspects of you, the universal being, we still perceive the world to be divided, such differences appearing as the result of ignorance. 38

## \|\| 3.17.39 \|\|

*svavarṇadharmābhiratā vedamārgānusāriṇaḥ \|*  
*na śakyāsterayo hantumasmābhis tapasāvṛtāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

Adhering to the traditions of our own community, following the Vedic path and filled with ascetic energy, we still cannot destroy our enemies. 39

## \|\| 3.17.40 \|\|

*tamupāyamaśeṣātmannasmākaṃ dātumarhasi \|*  
*yena tānasurānhantuṃ bhavema bhagavankṣamāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

Give us, please, the means, universal Lord, by which we may defeat the demigods.’ 40

## \|\| 3.17.41 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityukto bhagavāṃstebhyo māyāmohaṃ śarīrataḥ \|*  
*samutpādya dadau viṣṇuḥ prāha cedaṃ surottamān \|\| 41 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Thus addressed by the deities, Lord Viṣṇu produced a phantom from his body, which he presented to the best of gods with this explanation: 41

## \|\| 3.17.42 \|\|

*māyāmohoyamakhilāndaityāṃ stānmohayiṣyati \|*  
*tato vadhyā bhaviṣyanti vedamārgabahiṣkṛtāḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

The Lord:

When this phantom has confused the Daityas, you’ll be able to kill them all, as they will have left the Vedic path. 42

## \|\| 3.17.43 \|\|

*sthitau sthitasya me vadhyā yāvantraḥ paripanthinaḥ \|*  
*brahmaṇo hyadhikārasya devā daityādikāḥ surāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

I’m inherent in all creation, and any who offend the Vedas will be destroyed by me. All divine beings, including gods and Daityas and the rest, are subject to Vedic authority. 43

## \|\| 3.17.44 \|\|

*tadgacchata nabhīḥ kāryā māyāmohoyamagrataḥ \|*  
*gacchannadyopakārāya bhavatāṃ bhavitā surāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Go, therefore, and do not fear. Proceed with the phantom before you, gods—it will serve you well this very day. 44

## \|\| 3.17.45 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*ityuktāḥ praṇipatyainaṃ yayurdevā yathāgatam \|*  
*māyāmoho 'pi taiḥ sārdha yayau yatra mahāsurāḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Hearing this, the deities bowed again to Viṣṇu and returned as they had come, accompanied by the phantom, to confront the mighty demigods. 45

## Chapter 18 - The phantom confounds the demigods

## \|\| 3.18.1 \|\|

*śrīpāraśara uvāca*  
*tapasy abhiratān so 'tha māyāmoho mahāsurān \|*  
*maitreya dadṛśe gatvānarmadātīrasaṃśritān \|\| 1 \|\|*

When the phantom reached the banks of the Narmadā River, Maitreya, he saw the mighty demigods engaged in ascetic practice. 1

## \|\| 3.18.2 \|\|

*tato digaṃbaro muṇḍo barhipicchadharo dvija \|*  
*māyāmoho 'surān ślakṣṇamidaṃ vacanamabravīt \|\| 2 \|\|*

Taking the form of a naked holy man, shaven-headed and carrying a peacock feather, brahmin, the phantom addressed the demigods with gentle words. 2

## \|\| 3.18.3 \|\|

*māyāmoha uvāca*  
*he daityapatayo brūta yadartha tapyate tapaḥ \|*  
*aihikārthaś ca pāratryaṃ tapasaḥ phalamiccatha \|\| 3 \|\|*

The phantom:

Greetings, Daitya lords! Tell me, why do you practise these austerities? Do you desire a reward in this world or the next? 3

## \|\| 3.18.4 \|\|

*asurā ūcuḥ*  
*pāratryaphalalābhāya tapaścaryā mahāmate \|*  
*asmābhir iyam ārabdhā kiṃ vā te 'tra vivakṣitam \|\| 4 \|\|*

The demigods:

We undertake these practices because we want rewards hereafter. Why do you ask, wise sage? 4

## \|\| 3.18.5 \|\|

*māyāmoha uvāca*  
*kurudhvaṃ mama vākyāni yadi muktimabhīpsatha \|*  
*arhadhvaṃ dharmametaṃ ca sarve yūyaṃ mahābalāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

The phantom:

You must follow my instructions if you wish for liberation. You must be worthy of my religion, as it’s the door to freedom that never closes. 5

## \|\| 3.18.6 \|\|

*dharmo vimukterarheyaṃ naitasmādaparo varaḥ \|*  
*atraiva saṃsthitāḥ svargaṃ vimuktiṃ vā gamiṣyatha \|\| 6 \|\|*

This noble religion of liberation is the best. Once established in this tradition, you’ll proceed to heaven or to freedom from existence. 6

## \|\| 3.18.7 \|\|

*arhadhvaṃ dharmametaṃ ca sarve yūyaṃ mahābalāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

But you mighty beings must first prove worthy! 7

## \|\| 3.18.8 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*evaṃprakārair bahubhir yuktidarśanacarcitaiḥ \|*  
*māyāmohena te daityā vedamārgādapā kṛtāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

Parāśara:

With many such cunning words to inculcate his views, the phantom led those Daityas from the Vedic path. 8

## \|\| 3.18.9 \|\|

*dharmāyaitadadharmāya sadetanna sadityapi \|*  
*vimuktaye tvidaṃ naitadvimuktiṃ saṃprayacchati \|\| 9 \|\|*

He taught that vice is virtue, truth is falsehood and that which leads to liberation does not do so, 9

## \|\| 3.18.10 \|\|

*paramārthoyamatyarthaṃ paramārtho na cāpyayam \|*  
*kāryametadakāryaṃ ca naitadevaṃ sphuṭaṃ tvidam \|\| 10 \|\|*

That which is absolute highest truth is not so; 10

## \|\| 3.18.11 \|\|

*digvāsasāmayaṃ dharmo dharmoyaṃ bahuvāsasām \|\| 11 \|\|*

That which is to be done is not so; that this is wrong and that is right; that this is the religion of naked ascetics, and that is the religion of those who dress. 11

## \|\| 3.18.12 \|\|

*ityanekāntavādaṃ ca māyāmohena naikadhā \|*  
*tena darśayatā daityāḥsvadharmaṃ tyājitā dvija \|\| 12 \|\|*

Such were the endlessly repeated blandishments, brahmin, by which the phantom induced the Daityas to shun their own religion. 12

## \|\| 3.18.13 \|\|

*arhataitaṃ mahādharmaṃ māyāmohena te yataḥ \|*  
*proktāstamāśritā dharmamarhatāstena te 'bhavan \|\| 13 \|\|*

Since the apparition told them they were worthy (arhatha) of his great tradition, they trusted him, converted to Jainism and became Arhats. 13

## \|\| 3.18.14 \|\|

*trayīdharmasamutsargaṃ māyāmohena te 'surāḥ \|*  
*kārītāstanmayā hyāsaṃstatonye tatpracoditāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

After the phantom caused the demigods to abandon the traditions of the threefold Vedas, they began to instruct others. 14

## \|\| 3.18.15 \|\|

*tair apy anye pare taiś ca tair apy anye pare ca taiḥ \|*  
*alpair ahobhiḥ saṃtyaktā tair daityaiḥ prāyaśastrayī \|\| 15 \|\|*

These others taught others again, and they in turn taught even more, so that within a few days every Daitya left the Vedic path. 15

## \|\| 3.18.16 \|\|

*punaś ca raktāṃbaradhṛṅ māyāmoho jitendriyaḥ \|*  
*anyānāhāsurān gatvāmṛddhalpamadhurākṣaram \|\| 16 \|\|*

Some time later, wearing monkish saffron robes, the phantom, whose gaze was irresistible, approached another group of Daityas and spoke to them with simple words, both soft and sweet: 16

## \|\| 3.18.17 \|\|

*svargārthaṃ yadi vo vāñchā nirvāṇārthamathāsurāḥ \|*  
*tadalaṃ paśughātādiduṣcadharmair nibodhata \|\| 17 \|\|*

The phantom:

If you want to go to heaven or reach nirvāṇa, you demigods must give up this false religion, including the animal sacrifice. Listen! 17

## \|\| 3.18.18 \|\|

*vijñānamayamevaitadaśeṣamavagacchata \|*  
*budhyadhvaṃ me vacaḥ samyagbudhair evamihoditam \|\| 18 \|\|*

Understand that this whole world consists of knowledge. Understand my words in full, as they were uttered by the wise. 18

## \|\| 3.18.19 \|\|

*jagadetadanādhāraṃ bhrāntijñānārthatatparam \|*  
*rāgādiduṣṭamatyarthaṃ bhrāmyate bhavasaṃkaṭe \|\| 19 \|\|*

All the world has no foundation, but is ever bent on chasing baseless knowledge. Plagued by passion and other such emotions, it’s caused to wander through the straits of being. 19

## \|\| 3.18.20 \|\|

*evaṃ budhyata budhyadhvaṃ budhyataivamitīrayan \|*  
*māyāmohaḥ sa daityeyāndharmamatyāñjayannijam \|\| 20 \|\|*

Parāśara:

When the phantom said, ‘Understand! Understand!’, the Daityas said, ‘We understand’, and thus he led them from their own traditions. 20

## \|\| 3.18.21 \|\|

*nānāprakāravacanaṃ sa teṣāṃ yuktiyojanam \|*  
*tathātathā trayīdharmaṃ tatyajuste yathāyathā \|\| 21 \|\|*

Precisely in accord with these varied false instructions, which the phantom thrust upon them, the Daityas then abandoned true religion. 21

## \|\| 3.18.22 \|\|

*tepyanyeṣāṃ tathaivocuranyair anye tathoditā \|*  
*maitreya tatyajurdharmaṃ vedesmṛtyuditaṃ param \|\| 22 \|\|*

They also taught this doctrine to others, and those others taught others again, Maitreya, and they all forsook the true religion prescribed by the Vedas and sacred law. 22

## \|\| 3.18.23 \|\|

*anyānapyanyapāṣaṇḍaprakārair bahubirdvija \|*  
*daiteyānmohayāmāsa māyāmohodimohakṛt \|\| 23 \|\|*

The phantom, that great purveyor of delusion, brahmin, also confounded other Daityas with many varied heresies. 23

## \|\| 3.18.24 \|\|

*svalpenaiva hi kālena māyāmohena te 'surāḥ \|*  
*mohitāstatyajuḥsarvāṃ trayīmārgāśritāṃ kathām \|\| 24 \|\|*

In little time, the demigods, led astray by him, no longer spoke of the threefold Vedic path. 24

## \|\| 3.18.25 \|\|

*kecidvinindāṃ vedānāṃ devānāmapare dvija \|*  
*yajñakarmakalāpasya tathānte ca dvijanmanām \|\| 25 \|\|*

Some disparaged the Vedas, others insulted the deities, brahmin, and some belittled the totality of sacrifice, ritual action and even the brahmin order: 25

## \|\| 3.18.26 \|\|

*nainadyuktisahaṃ vākyaṃ hiṃsādharmāya ceṣyate \|*  
*havīṃṣyanaladaghdhāni phalāyetyarthakoditam \|\| 26 \|\|*

‘Violence towards animals for a sacrifice has no part in true religion. Such a necessity is mistaken. To say that butter burnt in fire can yield rewards is childish nonsense. 26

## \|\| 3.18.27 \|\|

*yajñair anekair devatvam avāpyendreṇa bhujyate \|*  
*śamyādi yadi cetkāṣṭhaṃ tadvaraṃ patrabhukpaśu \|\| 27 \|\|*

If Indra, having reached the status of a god through many sacrifices, is fed with wood from śamī-trees or similar, then he’s no better than wild beasts which eat the leaves. 27

## \|\| 3.18.28 \|\|

*nihatasya paśorjajñe svargaprāptiryadīṣyate \|*  
*svapitā yajamānena kinnu tasmānna hanyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

If the beast that’s sacrificed then goes to heaven, why doesn’t the sacrificer kill his father? 28

## \|\| 3.18.29 \|\|

*tṛptaye jāyate puṃso bhuktamanyena cettataḥ \|*  
*kuryācchrāddhaṃ śramāyānnaṃ na vaheyuḥ pravāsinaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

If food that’s eaten by someone at a śrāddha nourishes another, then those who live afar could offer it wherever they are and need not faithfully bring it to the ceremony. 29

## \|\| 3.18.30 \|\|

*janaśraddheyamityetadavagamya tatotra vaḥ \|*  
*upekṣā śreyase vākyaṃ rocatāṃ yan mayeritam \|\| 30 \|\|*

Once you understand what people may reasonably believe, then you’ll see the benefits expected from the words I’ve spoken. 30

## \|\| 3.18.31 \|\|

*na hyaptavādā nabhaso nipatanti mahīṃ surāḥ \|*  
*yuktimadvacanaṃ grāhyaṃ mayānyaiś ca bhavadvidhaiḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

Wise words don’t simply fall from heaven, mighty demigods, and reasonable instruction should be acceptable to me and others of your kind.’ 31

## \|\| 3.18.32 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*mayāmohena te daityāḥ prakārair bahubhis tathā \|*  
*vṛthāpitā yathā naiṣāṃ trayī kaścidarocayat \|\| 32 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The phantom misled the Daityas with so many notions that not one continued to accept the authority of the threefold Vedas. 32

## \|\| 3.18.33 \|\|

*itthamunmārgayāteṣu teṣu daityeṣu te 'marāḥ \|*  
*udyogaṃ paramaṃ kṛtvā yuddhāya samupasthitāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

When the Daityas strayed along these paths of falsehood in this manner, the deities prepared for war and advanced upon their foe. 33

## \|\| 3.18.34 \|\|

*tato daivāsuraṃ yuddhaṃ punarevābhavaddvija \|*  
*hatāścate 'surā daivaiḥ sanmārgaparipanthinaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

The battle of the gods and demigods resumed, brahmin, but on this occasion, the demons were overcome by those who took the path of truth. 34

## \|\| 3.18.35 \|\|

*svadharmakavacaṃ teṣāmabhūdyatprathamaṃ dvija \|*  
*tena rakṣābhavatpūrvaṃ neśurnaṣṭe ca tatra te \|\| 35 \|\|*

As the Daityas had forsaken the armour of true religion that protected them before, brahmin, they were overcome. 35

## \|\| 3.18.36 \|\|

*tato maitreya tanmārgavartino yebhavañjanāḥ \|*  
*nagnāste tair yatastyakta trayīsaṃvaraṇaṃ tathā \|*  
*kṛtāś ca te 'surā devair nānā vedavinindakāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

That’s why, Maitreya, those who took the path of truth, but vainly shed the safety of the threefold Vedas, are called ‘naked’. 36

## \|\| 3.18.37 \|\|

*brahmacārīgṛhasthaś ca vānaprasthastathāśramī \|*  
*parivrāḍ vā caturthotra pañcamo nopapadyate \|\| 37 \|\|*

Student, householder and forest-dweller, with wandering mendicant as the fourth—these are the stages of life. There is no fifth. 37

## \|\| 3.18.38 \|\|

*vastu saṃtyajya gārhasthyaṃ vānaprastho na jāyate \|*  
*parivrāṭ cāpi maitreya sa nagnaḥ pāpakṛnnaraḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

The wicked man who abandons married life but doesn’t retire to the forest or become a mendicant, Maitreya, is also naked. 38

## \|\| 3.18.39 \|\|

*nityānāṃ karmaṇāṃ vipra yasya hāniraharniśam \|*  
*akurvanvihitaṃ karma śaktaḥ patati taddine \|\| 39 \|\|*

One who fails to perform an obligatory ritual, brahmin, even though he’s able, spoils his obligatory rites for an entire day and night, and on that very day, is fallen. 39

## \|\| 3.18.40 \|\|

*prāyaścittena mahātā śuddhimāpnotyanāpadi \|*  
*pakṣaṃ nityakriyāhāneḥ kartā maitreya mānavaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

One who neglects his regular rites, except in an emergency, Maitreya, only regains his state of purity after a fortnight of sincere atonement. 40

## \|\| 3.18.41 \|\|

*saṃvatsaraṃ kriyāhāniryasya puṃsobhijāyate \|*  
*tasyāvalokanātsūryo nirīkṣyaḥsādhubhiḥ sadā \|\| 41 \|\|*

Seeing a man who has neglected his rituals for a year, virtuous people must turn their gaze upon the sun. 41

## \|\| 3.18.42 \|\|

*spṛṣṭe snānaṃ sacelasya śuddherheturmahāmate \|*  
*puṃso bhavati tasyoktā na śuddhiḥ pāpakarmaṇaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

If they touch that man, they purify themselves by bathing fully clothed, great sage, but no expatiation is prescribed for the evildoer himself. 42

## \|\| 3.18.43 \|\|

*devarṣipitṛbhītāni yasya viprasya veśmani \|*  
*prayāntyanarcitānyatra loke tasmānna pāpakṛt \|\| 43 \|\|*

There is no baser person in the world than one in whose house the deities, sages, ancestors and spirits, being neglected, heave a sigh and go away. 43

## \|\| 3.18.44 \|\|

*saṃbhāṣaṇānupraśnādi sahāsyāṃ caiva kurvataḥ \|*  
*jāyate tulyatā tasya tenaiva dvija vatsarāt \|\| 44 \|\|*

One should avoid the house, the seat or property of a person whose body or residence has felt the sighs of gods and other beings. 44

## \|\| 3.18.45 \|\|

*devādinisvāsahataṃ śarīraṃ yasya veśma ca \|*  
*na tena saṃkaraṃ kuryādgṛhāsanaparicchadaiḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

One who converses with, inquires after or socialises with such a person, brahmin, incurs the same portion of guilt, which endures for one whole year. 45

## \|\| 3.18.46 \|\|

*atha bhuktaṃ gṛhe tasya karotyāsyāṃ tathāsane \|*  
*śete cāpyekaśayane sa sadyastatsamo bhavet \|\| 46 \|\|*

If one eats with such a person, sits in his house or lies on a couch with him, he immediately becomes the equal of the sinner. 46

## \|\| 3.18.47 \|\|

*devatāpitṛbhūtāni tathānabhyarcya yo 'tithīn \|*  
*bhukte sa pātakaṃ bhuṅkte niṣkṛtistasya neṣyate \|\| 47 \|\|*

One who eats without honouring the deities, ancestors, spirits or guests is guilty of a misdeed. How great is his negligence! 47

## \|\| 3.18.48 \|\|

*brāhmaṇādyāstu ye varṇāḥsvadharmādanyatomukhāḥ \|*  
*yānti te nagnasaṃjñāṃ tu hīnakarmasvavasthitaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

Brahmins and members of other orders who turn their backs on their own traditions are also described as ‘naked’ and are ranked with those who neglect their rites. 48

## \|\| 3.18.49 \|\|

*caturṇāṃ yatra varṇānāṃ maitreyātyantasaṃkaraḥ \|*  
*tatrāsyā sādhuvṛttināmupaghātāya jāyate \|\| 49 \|\|*

Living in a place where there’s excessive contact among the orders is harmful to those of virtuous conduct. 49

## \|\| 3.18.50 \|\|

*anabhyarcya ṛṣīndevānpitṛbhūtātithīs tathā \|*  
*yo bhuṅkte tasya saṃllāpātpatantinarake narāḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

Those who speak with a person who eats before honouring sages, deities, ancestors, spirits and guests will go to hell. 50

## \|\| 3.18.51 \|\|

*tasmādetānnaro nagnāṃstrayīsaṃtyāgadūṣitān \|*  
*sarvadā varjayetprājña ālāpasparśanādiṣu \|\| 51 \|\|*

A wise man should therefore always avoid conversation or any other contact with these ‘naked’ individuals, who are defiled by rejection of the threefold Vedas. 51

## \|\| 3.18.52 \|\|

*śraddhāvadbhiḥ kṛtaṃ yatnā ddevānpitṛpitāmahān \|*  
*na prīṇayati tacchrāddhaṃ yadyebhir avalokitam \|\| 52 \|\|*

A śrāddha diligently performed by pious individuals brings no satisfaction to gods or spirits if ‘naked’ people see it. 52

## \|\| 3.18.53 \|\|

*śrūyate ca purākhyāto rājā śatadhanurbhuvi \|*  
*patnī ca śaivyātasyābhūdatidharmaparāyaṇā \|\| 53 \|\|*

Long ago, it’s said, a king called Śatadhanu had a pious wife named Śaibyā. 53

## \|\| 3.18.54 \|\|

*pativratā mahābhāgā satyaśaucadayānvitā \|*  
*sarvalakṣaṇasaṃpannā vinayena yanena ca \|\| 54 \|\|*

Faithful, fortunate, truthful, pure and kind, Śaibyā was blessed with decorum and every other virtue. 54

## \|\| 3.18.55 \|\|

*sa tu rājā tayā sārdhaṃ devadevaṃ janārdanam \|*  
*ārādhayāmāsa vibhuṃ parameṇa samādhinā \|\| 55 \|\|*

The royal couple worshipped Lord Janārdana, god of gods, with deep devotion. 55

## \|\| 3.18.56 \|\|

*homair japais tathā dānair upavāsaiś ca bhaktitaḥ \|*  
*pujābhiścānudivasaṃ tanmanā nānyamānasaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

Day after day, they devoutly undertook oblations, prayers, donations, fasts and rituals with their hearts on him alone. 56

## \|\| 3.18.57 \|\|

*ekadā tu samaṃ snātau tau tu bhāryāpatī jale \|*  
*bhāgīrathyāḥsamuttīrṇau kārttikyāṃ samupoṣitau \|*  
*pāṣaṇḍinamapaśyetāmāyāntaṃ saṃmukhaṃ dvija \|\| 57 \|\|*

One day, the royal couple, who were fasting on the full-moon day of the month of Kārttika, emerged from the waters of the Bhāgīrathī River in which they’d bathed together, brahmin, and saw a heretic drawing near. 57

## \|\| 3.18.58 \|\|

*cāpācāryasya tasyāsau sakhā rājño mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*atastadgauravāttena sakhābhāvamathākarot \|\| 58 \|\|*

Because the heretic was a friend of the monarch’s archery instructor, the king, out of respect for his teacher, engaged the man in conversation. 58

## \|\| 3.18.59 \|\|

*na tu sā vāgyatā devī tasya patnī pativratā \|*  
*upoṣitāsmīti raviṃ tasmindṛṣṭe dadarśa ca \|\| 59 \|\|*

But his wife, the queen, observant of her vow, said nothing and, because she was fasting, raised her eyes to the sun when she saw the stranger. 59

## \|\| 3.18.60 \|\|

*samāgamya yathānyāyaṃ daṃpatī tau yathāvidhi \|*  
*viṣṇoḥ pūjādikaṃ sarvaṃ kṛtaṃvatau dvijottama \|\| 60 \|\|*

On returning to the palace, the king and queen duly completed their worship of Viṣṇu and other deities as prescribed, best of brahmins. 60

## \|\| 3.18.61 \|\|

*kālena gacchatā rājā mamārāsau sapatnajit \|*  
*anvāruroha taṃ devī citāsthaṃ bhūpatiṃ patim \|\| 61 \|\|*

In time, the once victorious king expired, and the queen ascended his pyre to join her husband where he lay. 61

## \|\| 3.18.62 \|\|

*sa tu tenāpacāreṇa śvā jajñe vasudhādhipaḥ \|*  
*upoṣitena pāṣaṇḍasaṃllāpo yatkṛto 'bhavat \|\| 62 \|\|*

Because of his transgression of speaking to a heretic in a time of fasting, the king was born as a dog. 62

## \|\| 3.18.63 \|\|

*sā tu jāti smarā jajñe kāśīrājasutā śubhā \|*  
*sarvavijñānasaṃpūrṇā sarvalakṣaṇapūjitā \|\| 63 \|\|*

The queen, however, was born as the brilliant daughter of the king of Kāśī and could recall her previous lives. She possessed all forms of knowledge and wore each desirable quality as an ornament. 63

## \|\| 3.18.64 \|\|

*tāṃ pitā dātukāmobhūdvarāya vinivāritaḥ \|*  
*tayaiva tanvyā virato vivāhāraṃbhato nṛpaḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

Her royal father sought a husband for her, but that slender girl dissuaded him and so she never married. 64

## \|\| 3.18.65 \|\|

*tataḥsā divyayā dṛṣṭyā dṛṣṭvā śvānaṃ nijaṃ patim \|*  
*vidiśākhyaṃ puraṃ gatvā tadavasthaṃ dadarśa tam \|\| 65 \|\|*

Sometime later, the princess, who possessed divine insight, beheld the dog, her former husband. She journeyed to the city known as Vaidiśa, where she found him. 65

## \|\| 3.18.66 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvaiva mahābhāgaṃ śvabhūtaṃ tu patiṃ tadā \|*  
*dadau tasmai varāhāraṃ satkārapravaṇaṃ śubhā \|\| 66 \|\|*

The fortunate girl, recognising her husband in that form, gave him nice things to eat and treated him with kindness. 66

## \|\| 3.18.67 \|\|

*bhuñjan dattaṃ tayā so 'nnam atimṛṣṭam abhīpsitam \|*  
*svajāti lalitaṃ kurvanbahu cāṭu cakāra vai \|\| 67 \|\|*

Eating the tasty and desirable food she gave him, and playing about as dogs are wont to do, he repeatedly showed her his affection. 67

## \|\| 3.18.68 \|\|

*atīva vrīḍitā balā kurvatā cāṭu tena sā \|*  
*praṇāmapūrvamāhedaṃ dayitaṃ taṃ kuyonijam \|\| 68 \|\|*

The young girl, however, was embarrassed by his friendliness and, bowing deeply, addressed her beloved husband, who had suffered such a lowly birth. 68

## \|\| 3.18.69 \|\|

*smaryatāṃ tanmahārāja dākṣiṇyalalitaṃ tvayā \|*  
*yena śvayonimāpanno mama cāṭukaro bhavān \|\| 69 \|\|*

The wife:

Try to remember the kindly greeting you gave the heretic, your majesty, on account of which you were born as this dog, now showing me such friendship. 69

## \|\| 3.18.70 \|\|

*pāṣaṇḍinaṃ samābhāṣya tīrthasnānādanantaram \|*  
*prāptosi kutsitāṃ yoniṃ kinna smarasi tatprabho \|\| 70 \|\|*

You addressed that man after we bathed together at the sacred ford, and now you’ve had this deplorable rebirth. Do you not remember, lord? 70

## \|\| 3.18.71 \|\|

*śrīparāśara uvāca*  
*tayaivaṃ smārīti tasminpūrvajātikṛte tadā \|*  
*dadhyau ciramathā vāra nirvedamatidurlabham \|\| 71 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Reminded of his former life, he reflected for a while and was filled with dreadful pain. 71

## \|\| 3.18.72 \|\|

*nirviṇṇacittaḥsa tato nirgamya nagarādvahiḥ \|*  
*marutprapatanaṃ kṛtvā sārgālī yonimāgataḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

Broken-hearted, the dog set out from the city and died in the desert, where he was born as a jackal. 72

## \|\| 3.18.73 \|\|

*sāpi dvitīye saṃprāpte vīkṣya divyena cakṣuṣā \|*  
*jñātvā sṛgālaṃ taṃ draṣṭuṃ yayau kolāhalaṃ girim \|\| 73 \|\|*

The following year, the princess, using her divine insight, perceived all this and journeyed to Mount Kolāhala to see him. 73

## \|\| 3.18.74 \|\|

*tatrāpi dṛṣṭvā taṃ prāha sārgālīṃ yonimāgatam \|*  
*bhartāramapi cārvaṅgī tanayā pṛthivīkṣitaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

Beholding him there, the delicate princess addressed her husband born from a jackal’s womb. 74

## \|\| 3.18.75 \|\|

*api smarasi rājendra śvayonisthasya yanmayā \|*  
*proktaṃ te pūrvacaritaṃ pāṣaṇḍālāpasaṃśrayam \|\| 75 \|\|*

The wife:

Don’t you remember, your majesty, what I told you when you were born as a dog, about the time you addressed that heretic? 75

## \|\| 3.18.76 \|\|

*punasta yoktaṃ sa jñātvā satyaṃ satyavatāṃ varaḥ \|*  
*kānane sa nirāhārastatyāja svaṃ kalevaram \|\| 76 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Realising she had spoken truly, and being the best of those who understand such words, the jackal in the wilderness then starved himself to death. 76

## \|\| 3.18.77 \|\|

*būyastato vṛko jajñe gatvā taṃ nirjane vane \|*  
*smārayāmāsa bhartāraṃ pūrvavṛttamaninditā \|\| 77 \|\|*

Once more, the blameless princess found him, reborn this time as a wolf in a lonely forest, and reminded him of his prior fault: 77

## \|\| 3.18.78 \|\|

*na tvaṃ vṛko mahābhāga rājā śatadhanurbhavān \|*  
*śvā bhūtvā tvaṃ sṛgālo 'bhūrvṛkatvaṃ sāṃprataṃ gataḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

‘You’re not a wolf, you fortunate being, but King Śatadhanu. You were born as a dog, a jackal and now this creature.’ 78

## \|\| 3.18.79 \|\|

*smāritena yathā vyaktastenātmā gṛdhratāṃ gataḥ \|*  
*apāpā sā punaścainaṃ bodhayāmāsa bhāmiti \|\| 79 \|\|*

Parāśara:

Reminded of his true identity, the king was born as a vulture. Once more, the sinless beauty informed him of his past: 79

## \|\| 3.18.80 \|\|

*naredra smaryatāmātmā hyalaṃ te gṛdhraceṣṭayā \|*  
*pāṣaṇḍālāpajāto 'yaṃ doṣo yadgṛdhratāṃ gataḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

‘Your majesty, remember what you used to be. Give up this vulture’s life! You’re in this state because you erred in talking to a heretic.’ 80

## \|\| 3.18.81 \|\|

*tataḥ kākatvamāpannaṃ samanantarajanmani \|*  
*uvāca tanvī bhartāramupalabhyātmayogataḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

As a result, he was born as a crow in his next life. Using her special powers, that slender girl located him and said: 81

## \|\| 3.18.82 \|\|

*aśeṣabhūbhṛtaḥ pūrvaṃ vaśyā yasmai baliṃ daduḥ \|*  
*sa tvaṃ kākatvamāpanno jātodya balibhuk prabho \|\| 82 \|\|*

‘All the sovereigns on the earth paid tribute to you formerly, but now you’re born as a crow that scavenges food oblations, lord.’ 82

## \|\| 3.18.83 \|\|

*evameva bakatvepi smāritaḥ sa purātanam \|*  
*tat tyāja bhūpatiḥ prāṇān mayūratvamavāpa ca \|\| 83 \|\|*

Parāśara:

The king in corvine form was thus reminded of his past, and, having drawn his final breath, was born as a peacock. 83

## \|\| 3.18.84 \|\|

*mayūratve tataḥsā vai cakārānugatiṃ śubhā \|*  
*dattaiḥ pratikṣaṇaṃ bhojyair bālā tajjatibhojanaiḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

The splendid girl pursued that bird and always fed it things that peacocks like to eat. 84

## \|\| 3.18.85 \|\|

*tatastu janako rājā vājimedhaṃ mahākratum \|*  
*cakāra tasyāvabhṛthe snāpayāmāsa taṃ tadā \|\| 85 \|\|*

A little later, her royal father performed a great horse-sacrifice, at the end of which the peacock received a ritual bath. 85

## \|\| 3.18.86 \|\|

*sasnau svayaṃ ca tanvaṅgī smārayāmāsa cāpi tam \|*  
*yathāsau śvasṛgālādiyoniṃ jagrāha pārthivaḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

The girl with slender limbs then bathed herself and reminded the former king how he’d been born as a dog, a jackal and the others. 86

## \|\| 3.18.87 \|\|

*smṛtajanmakramaḥsotha tattyāja svakalevaram \|*  
*jajñe sa janakasyaiva putrosau sumahātmanaḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

Remembering this string of births, he left his body and was born as the son of a man of exemplary standing. 87

## \|\| 3.18.88 \|\|

*tataḥsā pitaraṃ tanvī vivāhārtham acodayat \|*  
*sa cāpi kāra yāmāsa tasyā rājā svayaṃvaram \|\| 88 \|\|*

When the slim girl pressed her father on the matter of her wedding, he arranged a svayaṃvara for her. 88

## \|\| 3.18.89 \|\|

*svayaṃvare kṛte sā taṃ saṃprāptaṃ patimātmanaḥ \|*  
*varayāmāsa bhūyo 'pi bhartṛbhāvena bhāminī \|\| 89 \|\|*

On the day of that event, her former husband was the man the splendid princess chose. 89

## \|\| 3.18.90 \|\|

*bubhuje ca tayā sārdhaṃ saṃbhogānnṛpanandanaḥ \|*  
*pitaryuparate rājyaṃ videheṣu cakāra saḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

The prince partook of many pleasures together with his wife, and, when her royal father died, the realm of Videha passed to him. 90

## \|\| 3.18.91 \|\|

*iyāja jajñānsubahūndadau dānāni cārthinām \|*  
*putrānutpādayāmāsa yuyudhe ca sahāribhiḥ \|\| 91 \|\|*

He performed many great sacrifices, made donations to the poor, fathered many sons and vanquished all his enemies. 91

## \|\| 3.18.92 \|\|

*rājyaṃ bhuktvā yathānyāyaṃ pālayitvā vasuṃdharām \|*  
*tattyāja sa priyānprāṇānsaṃgrāme dharmatonṛpaḥ \|\| 92 \|\|*

Having duly ruled his realm and defended all the earth, the king, following tradition, died in battle. 92

## \|\| 3.18.93 \|\|

*tataścitāsthaṃ taṃ bhūyo bhartāraṃ sā śubhekṣaṇā \|*  
*anvāruroha vidhivadyathāpūrvaṃ mudānvitā \|\| 93 \|\|*

Once more that fair-eyed woman, filled with joy, duly climbed her husband’s pyre as she had done so long ago. 93

## \|\| 3.18.94 \|\|

*tato 'vāpa tayā sārdhaṃ rājaputryā sa pārthivaḥ \|*  
*aindrān atītya vai lokāṃllokānprāpa tadākṣayān \|\| 94 \|\|*

Together with the princess, the king now passed beyond the heavenly realms of Indra to reach eternal regions where all wishes are fulfilled: 94

## \|\| 3.18.95 \|\|

*svargākṣayatvamatulaṃ dāṃpatyamatidurlabham \|*  
*prāptaṃ puṇyaphalaṃ prāpya saṃśuddhiṃ tāṃ dvijottama \|\| 95 \|\|*

That unequalled, unending paradise, which is the rarely won reward for merit earned in marriage, best of brahmins. There he reached a state of perfect purity. 95

## \|\| 3.18.96 \|\|

*eṣa pāṣaṇṭasaṃbhāṣāddoṣaḥ prokto mayā dvija \|*  
*tathāśvamedhāvabhṛthasnānamāhātmyameva ca \|\| 96 \|\|*

Such is the sin of speaking with a heretic that I’ve described, brahmin, and the efficacy of a ritual bath at the conclusion of a horse-sacrifice. 96

## \|\| 3.18.97 \|\|

*tasmātpāṣaṇḍibhiḥ pāpair ālāpasparśanaṃ tyajet \|*  
*viśeṣataḥ kriyākāle yajñādau cāpi dīkṣitaḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

That’s why one should never speak with wicked apostates or contact them, especially during rituals or when initiated for a sacrifice. 97

## \|\| 3.18.98 \|\|

*kriyāhānirgṛhe yasya māsamekaṃ prajāyate \|*  
*tasyāvalokanātsūryaṃ paśyeta matimānnaraḥ \|\| 98 \|\|*

If a prudent person is required to look up to the sun when he’s seen a man whose rituals have been neglected for a month, 98

## \|\| 3.18.99 \|\|

*kiṃ punaryais tu saṃtyaktā trayī sarṃvātmanā dvija \|*  
*pāṣaṇḍabhojibhiḥ pāpaurvedavādavirodhibhiḥ \|\| 99 \|\|*

How much greater the expatiation when one has contact with a sinner who whole-heartedly forsakes the threefold Vedas, eats food prepared by members of the lower orders or opposes Vedic teachings? 99

## \|\| 3.18.100 \|\|

*sahālāpastu saṃsargaḥ sahāsyā cātipāpinī \|*  
*pāṣaṇḍibhir durācārais tasmāttāṃ parivarjayet \|\| 100 \|\|*

One should never honour heretics, those who act unlawfully, false saints like the cat in the famous story, charlatans, sceptics or hypocrites like the fabled heron, or even speak with them. 100

## \|\| 3.18.101 \|\|

*pāṣaṇḍino vikarmasthānbaiḍālavratikāñchaṭhān \|*  
*haitukānbakavṛttīṃś ca vāṅmātreṇāpi nārcayet \|\| 101 \|\|*

One should keep one’s distance from the wicked, heretics and those of doubtful conduct, and avoid their company. It’s therefore best to stay away from them. 101

## \|\| 3.18.102 \|\|

*dvaratastais tu saṃparkastyājyaścāpyatipāpibhiḥ \|*  
*pāṣaṇḍibhir durājārais tasmāt tān parivarjayet \|\| 102 \|\|*

I’ve described to you the people known as ‘naked’, the sight of whom will spoil a śrāddha. By speaking to such apostates, a full day’s merit will be lost. 102

## \|\| 3.18.103 \|\|

*ete nagnāstavākhyātā dṛṣṭā śrāddho paghātakāḥ \|*  
*yeṣāṃ saṃbhāṣaṇātpuṃsāṃ dinapuṇyaṃ praṇaśyati \|\| 103 \|\|*

As these heretics are evil, a wise man shouldn’t speak with them, but if he does, all the merit earned that day is lost. 103

## \|\| 3.18.104 \|\|

*ete pāṣaṇḍinaḥ pāpā na hyetānālapedbudhaḥ \|*  
*puṇyaṃ naśyati saṃbhāṣādeteṣāṃ taddinodbhavam \|\| 104 \|\|*

Men will go to hell for speaking with those who wear their hair in matted locks, or shave their heads, or vainly eat before others do, or disregard the rules of purity, or are forbidden to offer food or water to the ancestors. 104
